{"id": "746b698ea54e35aa", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:35:15.974038+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C28 complete projection-capacity calibration: among ALL primitive nonnegative TWO-step schedules, the exact maximum shifted mass is D_two=[2+sqrt(2)+sqrt(10+8sqrt(2))]/2, the root >4 of D^4-4D^3-1=0. Actual interval-projection cuts plus the curvature1 projection witness prove the upper bound; an explicit nonnegative full five-edge interpolation identity for (a,sqrt(2)), a²+sqrt(2)a=2+2sqrt(2), proves attainment in every dimension. This is a finite-length calibration, not an all-length capacity theorem.", "evidence": "# Projection capacity calibration and an exponential false-positive family\n\nStatus: complete candidate calculations, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit. All new ingredients are proved below. Accepted fact\n1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the smooth-convex interpolation inequalities;\nb98bb807d514e80d supplies only the named exact benchmark recursion.\nThis is a capacity test, not a proof of the all-length capacity conjecture.\n\nFor a nonnegative schedule h of length n, put S=sum h_i, D=1+S, and\n\n    V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-SD||g_n||^2.\n\nPrimitive means V_h>=0 on every actual convex differentiable 1-smooth\nGD run. All witnesses and certificates below use the physical steps.\n\n## 1. Exact projection witnesses give two necessary tests\n\nFor any compact interval C containing zero, the function\nF_C(x)=max_(v in C)(xv-v^2/2) is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth,\nwith derivative equal to the nearest-point projection onto C. This follows\nby completing the square; the projection normal inequality gives its\n1-Lipschitz property. At every actual sample, f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.\nThe squared-distance telescope therefore gives exactly\n\n    V_h=x_0^2-(x_n+Sg_n)^2.                           (1)\n\nChoose one checkpoint b=h_j>1. Put\nA=1+sum_(i<j)h_i, B=1+sum_(i>j)h_i, so D=A+B+b-1.\nChoose u>0, v=(b-1)u/B, C=[-v,u], and x_0=A u.\nEvery preceding ordinary step stays on the positive plateau, with\ngradient u, and the checkpoint input is exactly u. Its output is\n-(b-1)u=-Bv. Every following ordinary step stays on the negative plateau,\nwith gradient -v, and the final point is -v. All function values are\nthose of the ONE fixed projection function, so (1) gives\n\n    V_h=A^2u^2-D^2(b-1)^2u^2/B^2.\n\nThus every primitive schedule satisfies the exact cut conditions\n\n    D(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+sum_(i<j)h_i)(1+sum_(i>j)h_i).   (2)\n\nThere is a second projection witness: start the scalar unit quadratic\nat1 and let x_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t). A compact interval containing zero\nand ALL these finitely many points has projection gradient g_i=x_i at\nevery sample and the same values x_i^2/2. Therefore every primitive h has\n\n    D |product_(i<n)(1-h_i)| <=1.                    (3)\n\nCondition (3) tests curvature1 only, not all curvatures.\n\n## 2. Exact two-step capacity upper bound\n\nFor any primitive h=(h_0,h_1), (2) gives\n\n    h_0,h_1 <= M_D:=2D/(D+1),  D=1+h_0+h_1.          (4)\n\nIf a step is at most1 this is automatic; otherwise rearrange its own\ncut inequality D(h_i-1)<=D-h_i.\nLet D_two be the unique root larger than4 of\n\n    D_two^4-4D_two^3-1=0.                            (5)\n\nWe claim D<=D_two. There is nothing to prove if D<4. For D>=4,\n(4) and h_0+h_1=D-1 imply both steps lie in\n[L_D,M_D], where L_D=D-1-M_D>1. The function\n(x-1)(D-2-x) is concave and has the same value at these two endpoints.\nConsequently\n\n    (h_0-1)(h_1-1)\n      >= (M_D-1)(L_D-1)\n      = (D-1)(D^2-3D-2)/(D+1)^2.\n\nMultiplying by D and applying (3) yields\n\n    D(D-1)(D^2-3D-2)<=(D+1)^2,\n\nwhich is precisely D^4-4D^3-1<=0. The polynomial is strictly increasing\nfor D>3, negative at4 and positive at5, proving (5)'s uniqueness and\nthe claimed upper bound.\n\n## 3. An explicit full certificate attains that bound\n\nPut b=sqrt(2) and let a be the positive root of\n\n    a^2+ba=2+2b,\n    a=(-b+sqrt(10+8b))/2.\n\nThen 1<a<2. Set S=a+b and L_cert=S(2-b)>0. On the two-step schedule\n(a,b), use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2. The following\nis an identity on the FULL formal three-gradient space:\n\n    V_(a,b) = sum_(i!=j) lambda_ij (-Q_ij),          (6)\n\nwhere\n\n    lambda_01=0,\n    lambda_10=b L_cert,\n    lambda_02=(a-1)L_cert,\n    lambda_20=L_cert,\n    lambda_21=S(S+b),\n    lambda_12=(b-1)lambda_21+b L_cert.\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative, so accepted interpolation necessity\nQ_ij<=0 proves primitive validity in every finite dimension.\n\nHere is a full coefficient check. Write u=g_0,v=g_1,w=g_2 and use\nx_1=x_0-a u, x_2=x_0-a u-b v. The five residuals used in (6) are\n\n    Q_10=f_1-f_0+(||u||^2+||v||^2)/2+(a-1)<u,v>,\n    Q_02=f_0-f_2+(1/2-a)||u||^2+||w||^2/2\n                                      -b<u,v>-<u,w>,\n    Q_20=f_2-f_0+(||u||^2+||w||^2)/2\n                                      +(a-1)<u,w>+b<v,w>,\n    Q_12=f_1-f_2+(1/2-b)||v||^2+||w||^2/2-<v,w>,\n    Q_21=f_2-f_1+(||v||^2+||w||^2)/2+(b-1)<v,w>.\n\nThe three mixed-gradient coefficients cancel directly from the displayed\nlambdas. The function coefficients are 2a,2b,-2S: use\n(b+2-a)L_cert=2a and (2-b)S(S-b)=2b, both consequences of\naS=2+2b and b^2=2. The squared-gradient coefficients are\n\n    ||u||^2: (a-1)^2 L_cert-a=a(1-a),\n    ||v||^2: (2-3b/2)S(S+b)+b(b-1)L_cert=b(1-b),\n    ||w||^2: -[aL_cert+bS(S+b)+bL_cert]/2=-S(S+1).\n\nFor the first equality, expanding (a-1)^2 S(2-b) using aS=2+2b\ngives 2a-a^2. For the second, divide by S; the constant terms cancel\nand the result is a(2-3b/2), whose product with S is b-2=b(1-b).\nThe third uses b^2=2. This verifies every coefficient in (6).\n\nLet D=1+a+b. Then D(a-1)=1+b and\nD(a-1)(b-1)=b^2-1=1. Also a=2D/(D+1), so equality holds throughout\nthe bound in section2. Finally a>3-b because the defining polynomial\nat 3-b equals 7-5sqrt(2)<0. Thus D>4 and D=D_two. Explicitly,\n\n    D_two=[2+sqrt(2)+sqrt(10+8sqrt(2))]/2\n         =Omega(1,1+sqrt(2)).                        (7)\n\nThe last expression uses the exact primitive join notation from\naccepted fact b98bb807d514e80d. Equations (2)--(7) give an analytic\ncapacity bound and an attaining universal certificate, not an SDP result.\nThis is a two-step calibration, not a new all-length capacity claim.\n\n## 4. Cut tests plus curvature1 admit exponentially large false positives\n\nFix r_geom=3/2. For each integer k>=1 put\n\n    u_i=(r_geom-1)r_geom^i=(1/2)(3/2)^i, 0<=i<k,\n    h^(k)=(u_0,u_1,...,u_(k-1),1,u_(k-1),...,u_1,u_0).\n\nIts length is n=2k+1 and its exact physical capacity is\n\n    D=1+sum h^(k)=2 r_geom^k.                         (8)\n\nEvery cut condition (2) holds STRICTLY. For a left-half step, put\nA=r_geom^i, b=(r_geom-1)A. The other mass is B=D-r_geom A+1.\nSince A<=r_geom^(k-1)=D/(2r_geom),\n\n    AB-D(b-1)\n      =D[(2-r_geom)A+1]-r_geom A^2+A\n      >=D[(3/2-r_geom)A+1]+A=D+A>0.\n\nThe mirror cut has the same two masses interchanged. The center step1\nhas zero left side in (2); steps below1 also satisfy it trivially.\nCondition (3) holds with product ZERO because of that center step.\n\nNevertheless these schedules are not primitive for every k>=15. This\ncan be shown on the ONE fixed scalar quadratic f(x)=x^2/4, starting at1.\nIts curvature is lambda=1/2, and its final iterate is\n\n    x_n=(1/2)[product_(i=0)^(k-1)(1-r_geom^i/4)]^2.    (9)\n\nNone of these factors is zero. For i=0,...,5 the product of their\nabsolute values is exactly\n\n    C_6=(3/4)(5/8)(7/16)(5/32)(17/64)(115/128)\n       =1026375/134217728 > 1/256.\n\nFor i>=6, r_geom^i/4>=729/256>2, so\n\n    |1-r_geom^i/4|>=r_geom^i/8>=729/512>sqrt(2),\n\nwhere the last strict comparison is 729^2>2*512^2. Hence for k>=6,\n\n    x_n > (1/2)(1/256)^2 2^(k-6)=2^(k-23),\n    D |g_n|=r_geom^k x_n > 3^k/2^23.                 (10)\n\nFor k>=15 this exceeds1, since 3^15=14348907>8388608=2^23.\n\nTo check that (10) really forces primitive failure, for any legal\nsmooth-convex data with minimizer0 put\nI_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0. A direct telescope gives\n\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2-2sum h_i I_i\n                         -2S f_n-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nSmoothness and convexity give ||x_n||>=||g_n|| and\n2f_n>=||g_n||^2. Thus\n\n    V_h<=||x_0||^2-D^2||g_n||^2.                     (11)\n\nEquation (10) makes (11) negative for the explicit quadratic run.\nAll steps and function values are actual; the failure is not a relaxed\ngradient assignment. Capacity (8) grows exponentially with n, so (2)\nand (3) alone cannot establish ANY polynomial capacity upper bound.\n\nThis refutes only the cut-plus-curvature1 filter. The curvature1/2 test\nexplicitly detects the family. It makes no insufficiency claim about\nthe entire quadratic class, and does not propose a primitive schedule\nabove the silver-power threshold.\n\n## 5. These scalar defects also have finite simplex projection witnesses\n\nFor completeness, the negative energy just established can be placed\ninside the requested projection class by finite operations, without\nusing the unverified C27 reduction as a premise. This also explains why\nprojection witnesses can detect a failure not detected by (2) or (3).\n\nGiven this length n schedule and its negative quadratic energy -v_def,\ntake an auxiliary diagonal quadratic with eigenvalues\ntheta_l=(l+1)/(2(n+2)), l=0,...,n, started with all coordinates1.\nIts n+1 gradients are independent because the polynomials\nP_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) have distinct degrees0,...,n; evaluation\nat n+1 distinct theta_l, followed by nonzero row scaling theta_l, is\ninvertible. All distinct auxiliary Q_ij are strictly negative: they\nequal minus one half the A_aux(I-A_aux) squared norm of the sample\nposition difference, and the relevant polynomial difference cannot\nvanish at n+1 distinct nodes.\n\nLet E_aux be its finite energy. Add it in orthogonal coordinates with\nsquared scale epsilon=v_def/[2(v_def+|E_aux|)]. The product function\nis an actual 1-smooth convex function, its new energy is at most\n-v_def/2, all new gradients are independent, and all finite Q_ij<0.\nRename these mixed jets x_i,g_i,f_i and their positive definite gradient\nGram matrix A_gram. Choose\n\n    f_*=min_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2)-1,\n    r_i=f_*-f_i+<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n    z_*=sum_j(A_gram^(-1)r)_j g_j.\n\nThen X_i=x_i-z_* and F_i=f_i-f_* satisfy\nF_i=<g_i,X_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Every finite residual is unchanged and\nequals <X_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><0. The comparison with vertex zero is\n<X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-(F_i-||g_i||^2/2)<=-1. These are ALL normal\ninequalities for C=conv{0,g_0,...,g_n}, proving P_C(X_i)=g_i and\nF_C(X_i)=F_i. Translation and the common value shift leave energy\nunchanged. Projection onto the gradient span places this simplex\nand its trajectory in dimension n+1 with exactly n+2 vertices.\n\nThus every k>=15 in (8) has an explicit finite simplex projection\ncounterexample. This construction preserves the negative sign and\nretains every queried vertex, but not normalized objective error.\n\n## Scope and the next missing ingredient\n\nThe exact two-step optimum and the exponential false-positive family\nare quantitative consequences of actual geometric tests. They identify\nprecisely why extrapolating the two-step cut/product proof cannot work\nat arbitrary length. A general capacity argument must use additional\ncurvatures or additional coupled simplex normal inequalities. No\nunproved inequality with silver exponent is used anywhere above.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c28_projection_capacity_tests.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "plan_id": "C28_projection_capacity_calibration", "status": "Complete self-contained unverified candidate; no fact_submit or solver call.", "scope": "Exact two-step primitive capacity, plus explicit all-length failure of one cut/product filter. No silver-power theorem at general n.", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.97}, "glossary": {"D_two": "Unique root larger than4 of D^4-4D^3-1=0, equal to [2+sqrt(2)+sqrt(10+8sqrt(2))]/2.", "L_cert": "Positive certificate parameter (a+b)(2-b), with b=sqrt(2).", "r_geom": "The constant 3/2 used in the exponential cut-filter counterexample.", "u_i": "The positive ordinary step (1/2)(3/2)^i in that palindrome.", "C_6": "The exact product 1026375/134217728 of the first six absolute curvature1/2 factors."}}
{"id": "500d209094028aef", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:42:11.799619+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Complete global classification candidate for the entire fixed-middle-step family: for h=(u,2,w) with u,w>=0, universal nonnegativity of E_theta for ANY real theta forces u=w=sqrt(2). More generally every other member has an actual core-ended smooth-convex witness with all incoming-terminal Q zero and negative primitive energy, excluding even arbitrary signed terminal multipliers. Silver has an explicit nonnegative zero-remainder certificate. The middle step must remain exactly2; other three-step families and the asymptotic target remain open.", "evidence": "# Three-step silver is locally maximal even with arbitrary terminal credit\n\nStatus: complete self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical\nPEP evaluation or fact_submit. The only accepted predecessor is\n1252b8823bb5a290, used for finite smooth-convex interpolation of one explicitly\nchecked two-dimensional data array. This is a finite local obstruction, not\nthe unrestricted asymptotic minimax theorem.\n\n## 1. Statement and definitions\n\nFor a positive three-step schedule h=(u,v,w), put s=u+v+w and T=1+s.\nOn any actual convex differentiable 1-smooth GD trajectory define\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n  V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)+sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                               -sT||g_3||^2,\n  E_theta(h)=V_h-theta sum_(i<3)h_i Q_(i,3).\n\nLet t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, and h_S=(t,2,t), whose capacity is\nT_S=3+2t=lambda^2.\n\nThere exists epsilon_0>0 such that, for every positive h satisfying\n\n  0<||h-h_S||<epsilon_0,  T>=T_S,\n\none of four explicit legal witnesses below has V_h<0 and Q_(i,3)=0 for\nall i<3. In particular E_theta(h)<0 on that same witness for EVERY real\ntheta. This excludes not only the bounded terminal-credit state but every\nPSD intrinsic certificate with arbitrary signed incoming-terminal\nmultipliers and nonnegative multipliers on the other actual inequalities.\n\nFor each theta>=0 it follows that h_S is a strict local capacity\nmaximizer in the E_theta class, and also in the class of certificates\nallowing arbitrary signed incoming-terminal multipliers. No claim of\nglobal optimality among all three-step schedules or of the project's\nall-horizon result is made.\n\n## 2. Core-ended equality data and its energy\n\nSuppose a finite legal GD transcript has optimum (0,0,0), and\n\n  f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 for all i,  x_3=g_3.\n\nPut y_i=x_i-g_i. Direct expansion gives\n\n  Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>,  Q_(i,3)=0,\n  V_h=||x_0||^2-T^2||g_3||^2.                          (1)\n\nThe energy identity follows by telescoping squared distances and\nsubstituting the displayed value formula. All four witnesses below have\nthese properties, so all their incoming-terminal interpolation slacks\nvanish exactly.\n\nFor convenience put\n\n  a=u-1, A=u+1, r=v-1, D=w+1, eta=r/D.\n\nRestrict to a sufficiently small fixed open neighborhood of h_S in which\nu,v,w>1, eta a<1, and 0<r(w-1)<1. These strict conditions hold at h_S.\n\n## 3. Four actual witness inequalities\n\nIf V_h is nonnegative on every witness in this section, the following\nfour smooth scalar expressions must be nonnegative:\n\n  F_Q=1-T^2(u-1)^2(v-1)^2(w-1)^2,\n  F_B=A^2-T^2 eta^2,\n  F_C=A^2-T^2 r^2(w-1)^2,\n  F_N=a u F_B+(1+eta)u^2(2-u^2).                     (2)\n\nEach vanishes at h_S.\n\nQ witness. Use f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1. Its final gradient is\n-(u-1)(v-1)(w-1), and (1) gives V_h=F_Q.\n\nB witness. Integrate f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)) with f(0)=0, and start\nat x_0=A. The points and gradients are\n\n  x=(A,1,-r,-eta),  g=(1,1,-eta,-eta).\n\nThey obey the given three steps and the value/core conditions in (1),\nso V_h=F_B. The derivative is continuous, monotone, and 1-Lipschitz.\n\nC witness. Integrate f'(x)=min(x,1) with f(0)=0, and start at x_0=A.\nThe points and gradients are\n\n  x=(A,1,-r,r(w-1)),  g=(1,1,-r,r(w-1)).\n\nThe last point lies in (0,1) by the neighborhood restriction. The\nfunction is convex differentiable 1-smooth, minimized at 0, and obeys\n(1), so V_h=F_C.\n\nN witness. Start with the B data as a scalar base array. Define a second\nscalar array, not assumed interpolable by itself, by\n\n  x_aux=(1,-a,r a,eta a),  g_aux=(1,-a,eta a,eta a),\n  f_aux,i=g_aux,i*x_aux,i-g_aux,i^2/2.\n\nAll three GD equations hold. The first two auxiliary proximal points\nare zero, the third is w eta a, and the fourth is zero. Consequently\nthe only positive auxiliary finite residual is\n\n  Q_aux,(0,2)=(1-eta a)w eta a.\n\nIndeed all edges into queries 0,1,3 have zero target proximal point;\nthe edge 1->2 is negative and 3->2 is zero. Both optimum directions\nare also valid, since every g_aux,i*y_aux,i is nonnegative.\nFor the base array, the same edge has\nQ_base,(0,2)=-(1+eta)w eta.\n\nSet the explicit positive coefficient\n\n  epsilon_mix=(1+eta)/[a(1-eta a)]\n\nand combine the arrays orthogonally:\n\n  X_i=(x_base,i,sqrt(epsilon_mix)x_aux,i),\n  G_i=(g_base,i,sqrt(epsilon_mix)g_aux,i),\n  F_i=f_base,i+epsilon_mix f_aux,i.\n\nEvery residual is the corresponding base residual plus epsilon_mix\ntimes the auxiliary one. The sole potentially positive edge is exactly\ncanceled; every other finite edge is nonpositive. The optimum jet is\nzero, all incoming optimum residuals vanish, and all outgoing optimum\nresiduals are nonpositive by the preceding proximal-point check.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, proof section2, supplies one fixed globally\nconvex differentiable 1-smooth interpolating function. More explicitly,\nwith indices including the zero optimum, let\n\n  c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i,\n  H(z)=max_i[c_i+<X_i,z-G_i>+||z-G_i||^2/2],\n  F(X)=sup_z[<X,z>-H(z)].\n\nThe accepted interpolation construction gives F(X_i)=F_i,\ngrad F(X_i)=G_i and F(0)=0=min F. Thus this is a legal fixed function,\nnot merely an oracle data proposal. The combined array still obeys\nX_3=G_3 and the value formula in (1). Its energy is\n\n  V_mix=F_B+epsilon_mix(1-T^2 eta^2 a^2)\n       =F_N/[a(1-eta a)].                             (3)\n\nFor the last equality, multiply by a(1-eta a) and use\nA=a+2, u=a+1 and 1-A^2a^2=u^2(2-u^2). The resulting numerator is\na u(A^2-T^2 eta^2)+(1+eta)(1-A^2a^2), exactly F_N.\nIts denominator is positive. Thus F_N<0 gives the claimed legal\nnegative-energy witness, with all incoming-terminal Q zero.\n\n## 4. The first-order feasible cone has strictly negative capacity change\n\nLet d=(d_u,d_v,d_w) be a direction at h_S. Put\n\n  p=d_u+d_w, q=d_v, sigma=p+q.\n\nIf all four directional derivatives of the expressions in (2) are\nnonnegative, the Q and B derivatives imply, respectively,\n\n  q<=-(3+t)p/2,\n  q<=(t-1)p/2.                                      (4)\n\nHere is the calculation. Differentiating F_Q at its zero value gives\n\n  dF_Q=-2[lambda p+q+(p+q)/lambda^2],\n\nand (lambda+lambda^(-2))/(1+lambda^(-2))=(3+t)/2.\nDifferentiating F_B gives\n\n  dF_B=2[t p-(1+lambda^2)q],\n\nand t/(1+lambda^2)=(t-1)/2. These are precisely (4).\n\nSuppose also sigma>=0. If p>0, the first bound in (4) implies\nsigma<=-(1+t)p/2<0, a contradiction. If p<0, the second implies\nsigma<=(1+t)p/2<0, also a contradiction. Thus p=0 and q=0.\nWrite d_u=e,d_w=-e.\n\nThe C derivative in this remaining direction is\n\n  dF_C=2lambda(1+lambda^2)e,\n\nso nonnegativity forces e>=0. Since dF_B=0 and both F_B and\nu^2(2-u^2) vanish at h_S, differentiating F_N gives\n\n  dF_N=(1+eta_S)[-4t e]=-8e,\n\nwhere eta_S=1/lambda and 1+1/lambda=t. Hence e<=0 too. Consequently\nd=0. The only direction with all four derivatives nonnegative and\nnonnegative capacity change is the zero direction.\n\n## 5. Turning the cone calculation into an actual local theorem\n\nIf the stated neighborhood did not exist, there would be a sequence\nh_k!=h_S converging to h_S with capacity at least T_S and all four\nF_Q,F_B,F_C,F_N nonnegative. Define\n\n  d_k=(h_k-h_S)/||h_k-h_S||.\n\nA subsequence converges to a unit vector d. Differentiability of each\nF at h_S and F(h_S)=0 imply grad F(h_S) dot d>=0 after dividing by\n||h_k-h_S||. The capacity condition similarly gives\nd_u+d_v+d_w>=0. Section4 forces d=0, contradicting ||d||=1.\n\nTherefore some epsilon_0 exists. For any h in the asserted punctured\nneighborhood with capacity at least T_S, at least one of the four\nexpressions in (2) is negative. Select its corresponding witness in\nSection3. It is legal, has V_h<0, and has Q_(i,3)=0 for all i<3.\nThis proves the statement for every theta simultaneously.\n\nFinally, for a putative PSD intrinsic identity with arbitrary signed\ncoefficients only on incoming-terminal edges, evaluation on that\nwitness makes all signed terms zero. Every other coefficient is\nnonnegative and multiplies Q<=0. The PSD expression would therefore\nbe at most V_h<0, impossible. This verifies the stronger certificate\nscope directly, without a dual-attainment assertion.\n\nTo check that h_S itself belongs to the classes called locally maximal,\nthe following explicit nonnegative multipliers give a zero remainder:\n\n  lambda_01=2, lambda_10=2lambda,\n  lambda_21=2t lambda, lambda_31=lambda_13=2lambda,\n  lambda_23=2lambda^2+2t lambda, lambda_32=2lambda^3,\n\nand every unlisted multiplier is zero. Expansion using t^2=2 gives\nV_(h_S)+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0. Thus V_(h_S)>=0 on all legal trajectories;\nfor theta>=0, the nonpositive incoming-terminal sum gives\nE_theta(h_S)>=V_(h_S)>=0. No such membership assertion is needed or\nmade for negative theta.\n\n## 6. Global classification when the middle physical step equals 2\n\nThe same witnesses give a complete result on one entire three-step\nfamily, not merely near h_S. Let u,w>=0 and h=(u,2,w). If E_theta(h)\nis nonnegative on every legal trajectory for even one real theta, then\n\n  u=w=sqrt(2).                                      (5)\n\nThe same necessary conclusion holds for an intrinsic PSD certificate\nwith arbitrary signed incoming-terminal coefficients and nonnegative\ncoefficients on every other inequality. Conversely, at u=w=sqrt(2)\nthe explicit certificate above establishes E_theta>=0 for every\ntheta>=0. No sufficiency for negative theta is asserted.\n\nHere is a complete proof covering endpoints and all parameter ranges.\nPut T=u+w+3. A first-cut core-ended Huber witness, when u>1, has initial\npoint 1, prefix gradient 1, and negative plateau slope\n-(u-1)/(w+3) after the first step. Its two remaining physical steps\nare 2,w; it ends at its negative Huber threshold. Its energy is\n\n  1-T^2(u-1)^2/(w+3)^2.\n\nIts incoming-terminal residuals vanish, so validity forces\nT(u-1)<=w+3=T-u and hence u<=2T/(T+1)<2. If u<=1, u<2 holds already.\n\nA last-cut Huber witness starts at u+3 with positive plateau gradient\n1 through the first two steps, then the last step w>1 reaches its\nnegative threshold -(w-1). Its energy is\n\n  (u+3)^2-T^2(w-1)^2.\n\nThe same reasoning forces w<2; this is already true when w<=1.\nAll these are actual clipped-derivative functions of the same form as\nthe B witness, and their endpoints are core.\n\nThe B witness from Section3 is legal for every u,w>=0 with middle\nstep 2. Its nonnegativity says\n\n  (u+1)(w+1)>=u+w+3,  or uw>=2.                    (6)\n\nTogether with u,w<2 this forces 1<u,w<2. On this whole box the C and\nN constructions in Section3 are legal: w-1 is in (0,1), and\neta a=(u-1)/(w+1)<1. Thus all four expressions (2) must be nonnegative.\n\nWe first rule out u>t. If also w>=t, then\nT(u-1)(w-1)>T_S(t-1)^2=1, contradicting F_Q>=0.\nIf w<t, set D=1+w<lambda. Since r=1 and T=u+D+2, direct expansion gives\n\n  (D^2/u)F_N\n   =P_u(D):=u^2 D^2+(4-2u^2-u^3)D-(u-1)(u+2)^2.    (7)\n\nIts leading coefficient is positive and its constant term is negative,\nso P_u has one positive and one negative root and is negative between\nzero and its positive root. Using lambda^2=2lambda+1,\n\n  P_u(lambda)=-(lambda+1)u^3-2u^2+4(lambda+1).\n\nThis is zero at u=t and strictly decreasing for u>0. Therefore it is\nnegative for u>t. The positive root of P_u lies beyond lambda, and\nP_u(D)<0 for 0<D<=lambda. Equation(7) contradicts F_N>=0. We have\nproved u<=t.\n\nIf u<t, (6) gives w>=2/u>t. For 1<w<2, condition F_C>=0 is equivalent\nto\n\n  (u+w+3)(w-1)<=u+1.\n\nThe left side is strictly increasing in w. At the smallest allowed\nw=2/u, its difference from u+1 is\n\n  (u+2/u+3)(2/u-1)-(u+1)\n       =2(u+1)(2/u^2-1)>0.\n\nThis is a contradiction. Hence u=t, and (6) then forces w>=t.\nIf w>t, the quadratic witness is strictly negative. Therefore w=t,\nproving (5).\n\nEvery exclusion above uses a fixed legal witness with every Q_(i,3)=0.\nIt consequently works for arbitrary real theta, or for arbitrary\nindividual signed coefficients on the incoming-terminal edges. This\nglobal classification is restricted to the exact middle step 2.\n\n## Scope for the project\n\nThis blocks a local perturbation repair of the three-step silver tail\nby the new signed terminal-interpolation state, even with unbounded\ncredit. Section6 additionally classifies the exact-middle-step-2 family.\nIt does not exclude a distant three-step tail with a different middle\nstep, a longer tail, or another full value/moment state. It does not turn\nintrinsic energy into the original objective ratio; the changed\nnormalizations in these hard functions are used only to test V_h.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_terminal_credit_fixed_middle_classification", "artifact": "local_memory/terminal_credit_silver_local.md", "finding_ids": ["5b4359baa88bf3e4", "edbbe4af2a2ad5c2"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "scope": "all nonnegative endpoints; exactly middle step2 only", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "fact_submit_calls": 0, "numerical_pep_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"h_S": "Three-step silver schedule(sqrt(2),2,sqrt(2)).", "E_theta": "V_h-theta sum_(i<3)h_i Q_(i,3), with normalized interpolation residuals and intrinsic energy defined in the proof.", "P_u(D)": "Exact quadratic u^2 D^2+(4-2u^2-u^3)D-(u-1)(u+2)^2 in Section6.", "F_N": "Energy numerator of the explicit two-dimensional core-ended mixture from Section3.", "u,w": "Arbitrary nonnegative first and last physical step sizes; the middle step is fixed to2."}}
{"id": "9a118d3334b50f28", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:43:42.732696+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Sharp quantitative candidate for creating new proximal value from ANY fixed legal prefix: at X let v=grad F_seed(X) and delta=F_seed(X)-||v||²/2 for the maximal prefix interpolant. Every other legal interpolant has d_new+||g_new-v/2||²<=delta+||v||²/4 and value deficit >=||g_new-v||²/2. Raising height by b>0 requires b<=||v||²/4 and loses at least ||v||²[1-sqrt(1-4b/||v||²)]²/8. These are sharp on an actual Huber continuation. For any constant-gradient tail of mass T, the same prefix forces 2f_end<=2delta+||v||²/(2T+1)-(2T+1)||g-v/(2T+1)||². This makes the boundary-gradient budget indispensable.", "evidence": "# Height budgets and a two-checkpoint construction with an arbitrary tail\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No new verifier allocation and no\nsolver call. All new ingredients are proved below in the same document;\nunverified earlier findings are not premises. This does not establish the\nuniform comparison with the complete sequence B_n.\n\nVerified inputs: fact `1252b8823bb5a290` for smooth-convex interpolation and\nthe maximal finite-prefix interpolant; fact `1fca3e5dd555cb4a` for the ray\ncomparison in Section 5. The definitions of W_n, K_n and B_n are those in\nfact `b98bb807d514e80d`. Work at smoothness one, minimum zero.\n\n## 1. An invariant of one fixed maximal prefix interpolant\n\nTake finitely many legal jets (x_j,g_j,f_j), including (0,0,0). Define\n\n    y_j=x_j-g_j,       delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,\n    M_seed=max_j delta_j,\n    m_seed(v)=max_j(<y_j,v>-delta_j),\n    H_seed(v)=||v||^2/2+m_seed(v),\n    F_seed(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H_seed(v)).                       (1)\n\nThe accepted fact proves that F_seed is the maximal convex 1-smooth\ninterpolant of these jets. Each delta_j is nonnegative by interpolation\nwith the optimum. For every x, put v=grad F_seed(x). Strong conjugate\noptimality gives\n\n    x-v=sum_j alpha_j y_j,\n    alpha_j>=0, sum_j alpha_j=1,\n\nwhere positive alpha_j occur only among affine terms active in m_seed(v).\nTo justify the convex-hull subgradient assertion directly, the directional\nderivative of a finite maximum of affine functions is the maximum of the\nactive slopes in that direction. A vector outside their convex hull can\nbe strictly separated from that hull and cannot satisfy the subgradient\ninequality. Every convex combination of active slopes does satisfy it.\nUsing their common active value in (1) now gives the exact identity\n\n    F_seed(x)-||grad F_seed(x)||^2/2\n        =sum_j alpha_j delta_j in [0,M_seed].              (2)\n\nThus arbitrary later changes of gradient on this ONE fixed function do\nnot create a proximal value above the maximum seed value.\n\nThere is an equivalent finite convex-roof formula. Let K_seed be the\nconvex hull of all y_j, and define, for z in K_seed,\n\n    J_roof(z)=min {sum_j alpha_j delta_j:\n                  alpha>=0, sum alpha=1, sum alpha_j y_j=z},\n\nand J_roof=+infinity outside K_seed. The coefficient simplex is compact,\nso the minima exist; 0<=J_roof<=M_seed on K_seed. Then\n\n    F_seed(x)=min_alpha [sum alpha_j delta_j\n                         +||x-sum alpha_j y_j||^2/2].       (3)\n\nFor a direct proof, minimize the displayed continuous convex function\nover the coefficient simplex and set v=x-sum alpha_j y_j. Its directional\noptimality conditions on the simplex say that positive coordinates\nminimize delta_j-<y_j,v>. These are exactly the active terms of m_seed(v),\ngiving equality with (1). This is an identity, not a numerical program.\n\nIn particular, with K_zero=conv{y_j:delta_j=0},\n\n    argmin F_seed=K_zero,\n    dist(x,K_seed)^2/2 <= F_seed(x)\n                         <= dist(x,K_seed)^2/2+M_seed.      (4)\n\nThe minimizer identity follows from (3): its two nonnegative summands\nvanish exactly when x=sum alpha_j y_j with support on zero-height jets.\nIt supplies the actual closest-minimizer distance, without substituting\ndistance to the generally larger set K_seed.\n\nFor any GD step t>=0 from x, let x_new=x-t grad F_seed(x). The elementary\nsmoothness inequality and (2), with delta=F_seed(x)-||grad F_seed(x)||^2/2,\ngive\n\n    F_seed(x_new)<=delta+(1-t)^2||grad F_seed(x)||^2/2\n                  =(1-t)^2 F_seed(x)+[1-(1-t)^2]delta\n                  <=(1-t)^2 F_seed(x)\n                      +[1-(1-t)^2]_+ M_seed.              (5)\n\nThis holds along any nonnegative schedule, irrespective of the number\nof gradient changes. A unit step gives F_seed(x_new)<=M_seed. Every\nfollowing step in [0,2] preserves that value cap. Iterating (5) gives,\nfor a suffix t_0,...,t_(r-1), the explicit bound\n\n F_seed(x_r) <= product_(i<r)(1-t_i)^2 F_seed(x_0)\n   +M_seed sum_(j<r)[1-(1-t_j)^2]_+\n                         product_(j<i<r)(1-t_i)^2.          (6)\n\nEmpty products and sums have their usual meanings.\n\nGreedily adjoining jets of F_seed at new points and recomputing the\nmaximal interpolant leaves the entire function unchanged. Indeed the\nnew conjugate quadratic is <=H_seed everywhere, by interpolation of\nF_seed with its actual new jet, whereas all old quadratics remain.\nTheir maximum is therefore still H_seed. In particular such greedy\nextensions cannot increase M_seed, by (2).\n\nThe scope is essential. The bound does NOT hold for arbitrary other\ninterpolants of the seed. Seed jets (0,0,0) and (1,1,1/2) have M_seed=0\nand maximal interpolant F_seed(x)=x^2/2. The symmetric Huber function\n\n    f_H(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1, and |x|-1/2 for |x|>=1\n\nhas the same seed. Starting at1 with schedule (R+1,1), R>=2, the maximal\nquadratic ends at0, whereas f_H visits -R and -(R-1), with normalized\nterminal value 2R-3. Its new proximal height at -R is R-1>0. Hence\ncreating new height requires a nonmaximal choice of the future function;\nthe invariant is not an upper bound on the unrestricted W_n.\n\n## 6. Sharp quantitative cost of creating a new height\n\nReturn to an ARBITRARY fixed legal finite seed from Section1. At any proposed\nnew point X put\n\n    v=grad F_seed(X), delta=F_seed(X)-||v||^2/2.\n\nLet f_new be ANY legal convex 1-smooth interpolant of the same seed, and\nlet g=grad f_new(X), d_new=f_new(X)-||g||^2/2. It need not be maximal.\nThe retained outgoing interpolation inequalities imply\n\n    f_new(X)<=<X,g>-H_seed(g).\n\nThe quadratic strong-convexity bound on H_seed at v, whose subgradient\nis X, yields\n\n    F_seed(X)-f_new(X)>=||g-v||^2/2.                      (27)\n\nEquivalently, use convexity of m_seed with subgradient X-v at v to\nobtain the sharper useful arrangement\n\n    d_new+||g-v/2||^2<=delta+||v||^2/4\n                             <=M_seed+||v||^2/4.          (28)\n\nFor clarity, the expansion is\n\n d_new<=<X,g>-||g||^2-m_seed(g)\n      <=<v,g>-||g||^2+<X-v,v>-m_seed(v)\n       =delta+||v||^2/4-||g-v/2||^2.\n\nThus an increase b=d_new-delta>0 is impossible unless b<=||v||^2/4.\nWhen v!=0, (27)--(28) further give the explicit necessary value loss\n\n F_seed(X)-f_new(X)\n   >= ||v||^2/8 [1-sqrt(1-4b/||v||^2)]^2.               (29)\n\nIndeed (28) puts g in the ball with center v/2 and radius\nsqrt(||v||^2/4-b); its distance to v is at least ||v||/2 minus that\nradius. If v=0, no positive b is possible. The same lower bound applies\nwhen d_new>=M_seed+b, since delta<=M_seed. At b=||v||^2/4 the value\nloss is at least ||v||^2/8, a nonvanishing cost that cannot be hidden\nby dropping the boundary gradient from the state.\n\nThe inequalities are sharp for legal data, not merely abstract vectors.\nUse the seed (0,0,0),(1,1,1/2), so F_seed=x^2/2. Choose R>=2,\nX=-R, and the symmetric Huber function with threshold tR, 1/2<=t<=1.\nIt retains the seed because tR>=1. It has g=-tR,\nd_new=t(1-t)R^2, and value loss (1-t)^2R^2/2. Thus it attains\n(27)--(29), with t=(1+sqrt(1-4b/R^2))/2. The point X is reached from\nthe seed x_0=1 by the nonnegative step R+1; it is a valid next query.\n\nThere is also a general tail consequence. If one interpolant of the\nseed has the SAME gradient g at every actual sample of a prescribed\ntail starting at X, including its terminal sample, and its step sum\nis T, interpolation in both directions between equal-gradient jets\nforces f_end=f_new(X)-T||g||^2. Completing the square in (27) gives\n\n    2f_end <=2delta+||v||^2/(2T+1)\n                    -(2T+1)||g-v/(2T+1)||^2.             (30)\n\nThis is a necessary bound for every such constant-gradient continuation\nin any dimension. It separates the retained height from the geometric\ngradient budget, with an exact tail loss. It is not asserted for tails\nwhose gradients vary.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["b80fa725238cc84a", "1ed052539a27d4ff"]}, "glossary": {"F_seed": "Pointwise maximal smooth-convex interpolant of the finite legal seed.", "M_seed": "Maximum seed proximal height f_j-||g_j||²/2.", "d_new": "Proximal value f_new(X)-||grad f_new(X)||²/2 of any other legal prefix interpolant.", "b": "Positive increase d_new-delta in this height-budget statement.", "delta": "F_seed(X)-||grad F_seed(X)||²/2.", "v": "Actual gradient grad F_seed(X) at the selected point X."}}
{"id": "3b8e4508d416f978", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:43:42.772069+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact all-dimension constant-tail optimum candidate for the retained two-block prefix: with sigma=2T+1, D=(A-kappa)(A+2a)+a², and 0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon), the optimal tail score is [A kappa epsilon²-a²(kappa epsilon-sigma/M0)_+²/D]/sigma. The explicit fourth plane with gradient (epsilon d-tau e)/sigma, tau=a(kappa epsilon-sigma/M0)_+/D, attains the complete outgoing optimum AND all incoming old-plane constraints. Optimizing the angle is explicit: kappa_best=min(A/(1+epsilon), A(A+a)/(A+2a)+a sigma/[M0 epsilon(A+a)]). The interior retained-height term has a finite threshold in T, stated exactly; it cannot be treated as a tail-independent asymptotic gain.", "evidence": "# Height budgets and a two-checkpoint construction with an arbitrary tail\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No new verifier allocation and no\nsolver call. All new ingredients are proved below in the same document;\nunverified earlier findings are not premises. This does not establish the\nuniform comparison with the complete sequence B_n.\n\nVerified inputs: fact `1252b8823bb5a290` for smooth-convex interpolation and\nthe maximal finite-prefix interpolant; fact `1fca3e5dd555cb4a` for the ray\ncomparison in Section 5. The definitions of W_n, K_n and B_n are those in\nfact `b98bb807d514e80d`. Work at smoothness one, minimum zero.\n\n## 2. Fully explicit three-cell construction\n\nPrescribe arbitrary nonnegative lists B_0,B_1,B_2 of respective sums\nM_0-1, M_1-1, T, where M_0,M_1>=1 and T>=0. Put two checkpoints between\nthem:\n\n    h=(B_0, A M_0+1, B_1, 1+M_1 epsilon, B_2),\n    A>0, epsilon>0.                                      (7)\n\nTheir entries can be arbitrarily large individually; only their actual\npartial sums are used. Select any kappa satisfying\n\n    0<kappa<A,             kappa(1+epsilon)<=A.             (8)\n\nIn R^2, write\n\n    a=1-1/M_0,\n    nu=sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)),\n    p=(1,0)/M_0,              q=(-kappa,nu)/M_1,\n    X=(-A,0),                y=X-M_1 q=(kappa-A,-nu),\n    d=-M_1 q=(kappa,-nu),     e=(a,0)-y,\n    D=||e||^2=(A-kappa)(A+2a)+a^2>0,\n    w=d-(a kappa/D)e,\n    W_sq=||w||^2=A kappa-a^2 kappa^2/D,\n    lambda_cap=epsilon/(2T+1),\n    r_cap=lambda_cap w.                                  (9)\n\nThe following products are exact:\n\n    <d,y>=0, <d,e>=a kappa, <y,e>=-(A-kappa)(A+a),\n    <e,w>=0, <d,w>=W_sq, <q,w>=-W_sq/M_1,\n    w_1=kappa(A-kappa)(A+a)/D>0,\n    <y,w>=a kappa(A-kappa)(A+a)/D>=0.                     (10)\n\nHere w_1 denotes the first coordinate, not a time index. Define ONE\nfixed nonsmooth convex function and its Moreau envelope by\n\n    J_cap(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>,<r_cap,z-y>),\n    f_cap(x)=min_z[J_cap(z)+||x-z||^2/2],   x_init=(1,0).   (11)\n\nAll four planes are chosen before running the entire supplied schedule.\nThe fourth plane has intercept -<r_cap,y><=0; this intercept is retained.\n\nFor completeness, this envelope is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth.\nThe strictly convex coercive minimization has a unique proximal point\nz(x); optimality says g(x)=x-z(x) belongs to the subgradient of J_cap\nthere. Monotonicity of subgradients at two points gives\n<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'> >= ||g(x)-g(x')||^2. Thus g is 1-Lipschitz. The\ntwo minimizing inequalities show that it is the derivative of the\nenvelope, and joint convexity followed by minimization proves convexity.\nAn active affine-plane slope at z is a subgradient, so it suffices below\nto check activation at x=z+that slope.\n\n### First block and first checkpoint\n\nFor partial ordinary sum u in [0,M_0-1], propose gradient p and proximal\npoint z=(1-(u+1)/M_0,0). Its first coordinate lies in [0,a]. The p-plane\nis nonnegative, and the q-plane is nonpositive. Since <e,w>=0,\n\n    <r_cap,z-y>=lambda_cap (z_1-a)w_1<=0.\n\nThus p is active at every actual sample, including the first checkpoint\nquery at x=p. That checkpoint sends the iterate exactly to X=(-A,0).\n\n### Second block and second checkpoint\n\nFor t=u+1 in [1,M_1], propose gradient q and proximal point X-tq.\nIt equals y+(M_1-t)q. The q-height is\n\n    <q,X-tq>=(A kappa/M_1)(1-t/M_1)>=0.\n\nThe excess of q over p is affine in t, with endpoints controlled by\nits value at t=M_1:\n\n    <q-p,X-tq>=A kappa/M_1+A/M_0\n                         -t(A kappa/M_1^2+kappa/(M_0 M_1))\n                   >=(A-kappa)/M_0>0.\n\nThe fourth plane has value\n\n    <r_cap,X-tq-y>\n       =-lambda_cap(M_1-t)W_sq/M_1<=0.\n\nAll old and future plane comparisons therefore hold at every sample.\nThe position at the second checkpoint is y+q. Its step 1+M_1 epsilon\nsends the trajectory to\n\n    x_C=y+epsilon d.                                     (12)\n\n### Arbitrary final list\n\nFor every partial tail sum u in [0,T], propose gradient r_cap. Its\nproximal point is\n\n    Z(u)=y+epsilon d-(u+1)lambda_cap w.\n\nThe fourth plane there has nonnegative value\n\n    <r_cap,Z(u)-y>\n       =lambda_cap[epsilon-(u+1)lambda_cap]W_sq>=0,         (13)\n\nbecause (u+1)/(2T+1)<=1. The p-plane is nonpositive since\n\n    M_0<p,Z(u)>\n       =kappa-A+epsilon kappa-(u+1)lambda_cap w_1<=0\n\nby (8) and w_1>0. The q-plane is also nonpositive:\n\n    M_1<q,Z(u)>=-epsilon A kappa\n                         +(u+1)lambda_cap W_sq<=0,\n\nusing W_sq<=A kappa and (u+1)lambda_cap<=epsilon. Thus the fourth\nplane is active throughout the entire final list, including its endpoint.\nThis checks all directions of every old/new comparison using the same J_cap.\n\n### Exact value, boundary gradients and true normalization\n\nAt the terminal point x_C-T r_cap, adding half the squared gradient to\n(13) gives\n\n    2 f_cap(x_n)=epsilon^2 W_sq/(2T+1).                   (14)\n\nThe actual squared gradients are\n\n    ||p||^2=1/M_0^2,\n    ||q||^2=A kappa/M_1^2,\n    ||r_cap||^2=epsilon^2 W_sq/(2T+1)^2.                  (15)\n\nThe terminal proximal value is exactly\n\n    delta_end=T epsilon^2 W_sq/(2T+1)^2.                 (16)\n\nThe fresh component of q has squared norm kappa(A-kappa)/M_1^2; it\nhas not been dropped from any norm or scale calculation. The construction\nretains an explicitly positive value budget when T>0, in contrast to a\npure quadratic terminal completion followed by a unit-step reset.\n\nThe origin is a minimizer: its p- and q-planes are zero and its fourth\nplane is <=0 by (10). The minimum of J_cap and its envelope is zero.\nEvery minimizer z of J_cap satisfies <p,z><=0, hence z_1<=0. A minimizer\nof the envelope is exactly a minimizer of J_cap, since both terms in\nits defining minimum are nonnegative. Therefore the closest minimizer\nto x_init=(1,0) is genuinely the origin, at distance exactly one.\nEquation (14) is consequently the required normalized objective, not\nan evaluation using an arbitrarily distant optimum.\n\nAll endpoints M_0=1, M_1=1 and T=0 are included. In particular a=0\ncauses no singularity because 0<kappa<A makes D>0. Zero steps duplicate\nqueries and have no effect on the proof. No parameter limit is hidden.\n\n## 3. A schedule functional with no feasibility oracle\n\nFor any nonnegative schedule h of length n and any i<j with h_i>1,\nh_j>1, define\n\n    P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t,\n    S_ij=sum_(i<t<j) h_t,\n    T_j=sum_(j<t<n) h_t,\n    A_ij=(h_i-1)/(1+P_i),\n    epsilon_ij=(h_j-1)/(1+S_ij).\n\nChoose explicitly\n\n    kappa=A_ij/[2 max(1,epsilon_ij)].                      (17)\n\nIt is positive, at most A_ij/2, and satisfies (8), because\n1+epsilon<=2max(1,epsilon). From D>=a^2 one has\n\n    W_sq>=A kappa-kappa^2>=A kappa/2.\n\nThus (14) yields, for EVERY eligible pair,\n\n W_n(h) >= A_ij^2 min(epsilon_ij^2,epsilon_ij)\n                                      /[4(1+2T_j)].       (18)\n\nDefine C_n^tail(h) to be the maximum of the right side over all eligible\npairs, with zero when there are none. The construction proves the new\ncandidate universal lower bound\n\n    W_n(h)>=max(K_n(h), C_n^tail(h)).                      (19)\n\nThe pair selection is adversary selection before the run; there are no\nunproved existence parameters and no changes to the supplied schedule.\nUnlike a product term tied to h_(n-1), C_n^tail need not vanish when the\nlast step is one. Arbitrarily long, nonperiodic final lists are covered\nwith their exact mass loss 1+2T_j. No assertion that (19) matches 1/B_n\nuniformly is made.\n\n## 4. Exact optimization within the displayed cap directions\n\nFor fixed A>0, a>=0, epsilon>0, T>=0, all directions satisfying (8)\nare admissible in the preceding proof. The exact score is proportional\nto\n\n    W_sq(kappa)=A kappa-a^2 kappa^2/\n                         [(A-kappa)(A+2a)+a^2].            (20)\n\nIf a=0, this is A kappa, so its maximum on the admissible interval is\nat kappa=A/(1+epsilon). If a>0, differentiation with the positive\ndenominator gives\n\n    W_sq'(kappa)=A-a^2 kappa[2D+(A+2a)kappa]/D^2.\n\nPut b=A+2a and C=(A+a)^2, so D=C-b kappa. Expanding its numerator\nfactorizes as\n\n    D^2 W_sq'(kappa)\n        =(A+a)^2 [A(A+a)-b kappa]\n                       [(A+a)-kappa].                    (21)\n\nThe second bracket is positive for kappa<A. Hence the unique unconstrained\nmaximizer in (0,A) is\n\n    kappa_0=A(A+a)/(A+2a).\n\nIts squared direction norm is W_sq(kappa_0)=kappa_0^2. The exact optimum\nwithin the explicitly checked cap family is consequently\n\n    kappa_opt=min(kappa_0,A/(1+epsilon)) for a>0,\n    kappa_opt=A/(1+epsilon) for a=0.                       (22)\n\nAt the feasibility endpoint, kappa(1+epsilon)=A, the old p-plane is\nstill dominated, by the nonnegative subtraction in its comparison. The\noptimization therefore has no open-boundary loss. This is an optimum\nonly within this displayed family of cap planes, not over all prefixes\nor all legal interpolants.\n\n## 5. A strict comparison with the entire verified ray functional\n\nThe following family establishes that (18) contains information absent\nfrom F_ray, although the schedules are already above the target scale\nby the single-cut witness and hence do not solve the hard regime.\n\nLet an integer M>=16 be given, A=M^(1/8), epsilon=M^(-1/4), and choose\n\n    h=(1 repeated M-1 times, A M+1,\n       1 repeated M-1 times, 1+M epsilon, 1).              (23)\n\nOnly its two large checkpoints have entries >1, so the verified ray\nconstruction can select at most those two: a selected first checkpoint\nmust exceed1, and every later selected checkpoint must also exceed1\nbecause positive new gradient magnitude requires (b-1)m>O>=0.\nIf both are chosen, its exact z_term is\n\n    z_term=A epsilon-(M-1)/M\n          =M^(-1/8)-1+1/M<0.                             (24)\n\nIndeed M^(-1/8)<=1/sqrt(2)<3/4 and 1/M<=1/16. Thus this pair is\ninfeasible even before its terminal old-slope constraint. Every feasible\nray witness has at most one cut, and fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives exactly\n\n    F_ray_n(h)=K_n(h).\n\nThe global Huber term is <=1/M. The first cut contributes\nA^2/[1+2(M-1+1+M epsilon+1)]<=A^2/(2M). At the second cut,\none plus the prefix mass is (A+2)M and the tail has sum1, so its value is\nepsilon^2/[3(A+2)^2]<=M^(-3/4)/3. Thus\n\n    F_ray_n(h)=K_n(h)<=M^(-3/4).                          (25)\n\nThe new cap, with T=1, has from (18)\n\n    2f_cap(x_n)>=A^2 epsilon^2/12=M^(-1/4)/12.             (26)\n\nIts ratio to the complete ray functional is at least sqrt(M)/12 and\ntends to infinity, with a genuinely unit final step and exact distance1.\nThis comparison is deliberately scoped: n=2M+1, so (25) itself does\nnot establish that F_ray is below the project benchmark. No such claim\nis used to market this family as a new solution of the all-schedule gap.\n\n## 6. Sharp quantitative cost of creating a new height\n\nReturn to an ARBITRARY fixed legal finite seed from Section1. At any proposed\nnew point X put\n\n    v=grad F_seed(X), delta=F_seed(X)-||v||^2/2.\n\nLet f_new be ANY legal convex 1-smooth interpolant of the same seed, and\nlet g=grad f_new(X), d_new=f_new(X)-||g||^2/2. It need not be maximal.\nThe retained outgoing interpolation inequalities imply\n\n    f_new(X)<=<X,g>-H_seed(g).\n\nThe quadratic strong-convexity bound on H_seed at v, whose subgradient\nis X, yields\n\n    F_seed(X)-f_new(X)>=||g-v||^2/2.                      (27)\n\nEquivalently, use convexity of m_seed with subgradient X-v at v to\nobtain the sharper useful arrangement\n\n    d_new+||g-v/2||^2<=delta+||v||^2/4\n                             <=M_seed+||v||^2/4.          (28)\n\nFor clarity, the expansion is\n\n d_new<=<X,g>-||g||^2-m_seed(g)\n      <=<v,g>-||g||^2+<X-v,v>-m_seed(v)\n       =delta+||v||^2/4-||g-v/2||^2.\n\nThus an increase b=d_new-delta>0 is impossible unless b<=||v||^2/4.\nWhen v!=0, (27)--(28) further give the explicit necessary value loss\n\n F_seed(X)-f_new(X)\n   >= ||v||^2/8 [1-sqrt(1-4b/||v||^2)]^2.               (29)\n\nIndeed (28) puts g in the ball with center v/2 and radius\nsqrt(||v||^2/4-b); its distance to v is at least ||v||/2 minus that\nradius. If v=0, no positive b is possible. The same lower bound applies\nwhen d_new>=M_seed+b, since delta<=M_seed. At b=||v||^2/4 the value\nloss is at least ||v||^2/8, a nonvanishing cost that cannot be hidden\nby dropping the boundary gradient from the state.\n\nThe inequalities are sharp for legal data, not merely abstract vectors.\nUse the seed (0,0,0),(1,1,1/2), so F_seed=x^2/2. Choose R>=2,\nX=-R, and the symmetric Huber function with threshold tR, 1/2<=t<=1.\nIt retains the seed because tR>=1. It has g=-tR,\nd_new=t(1-t)R^2, and value loss (1-t)^2R^2/2. Thus it attains\n(27)--(29), with t=(1+sqrt(1-4b/R^2))/2. The point X is reached from\nthe seed x_0=1 by the nonnegative step R+1; it is a valid next query.\n\nThere is also a general tail consequence. If one interpolant of the\nseed has the SAME gradient g at every actual sample of a prescribed\ntail starting at X, including its terminal sample, and its step sum\nis T, interpolation in both directions between equal-gradient jets\nforces f_end=f_new(X)-T||g||^2. Completing the square in (27) gives\n\n    2f_end <=2delta+||v||^2/(2T+1)\n                    -(2T+1)||g-v/(2T+1)||^2.             (30)\n\nThis is a necessary bound for every such constant-gradient continuation\nin any dimension. It separates the retained height from the geometric\ngradient budget, with an exact tail loss. It is not asserted for tails\nwhose gradients vary.\n\n## 7. Exact optimal constant-gradient tail for each displayed prefix\n\nSection2's perpendicular direction gave a simple explicit guarantee. The\nfull outgoing optimum for the SAME retained first two blocks can in fact\nbe attained with a slightly different fourth plane. Keep all definitions\nthrough e,D in (9), retain the feasibility range (8), and put\n\n    sigma=2T+1,\n    tau=a(kappa epsilon-sigma/M_0)_+/D,\n    r_exact=(epsilon d-tau e)/sigma.                      (31)\n\nIf a=0 set tau=0, as the formula already does. In all cases\n0<=tau<1: when a>0, (8) and D>=(A-kappa)(A+2a) imply\ntau<=a/(A+2a)<1/2. Replace the fourth plane in (11) by\n\n    <r_exact,z-y>.\n\nThe exact score is\n\n V_exact(kappa)= [A kappa epsilon^2\n               -a^2(kappa epsilon-sigma/M_0)_+^2/D]/sigma. (32)\n\nThis score is the maximum over ALL legal constant-gradient tails with\nthe fixed first- and second-block jets at this kappa, including arbitrary\naffine intercepts and extra dimensions. This assertion concerns the\ngiven prefix, not every possible two-block prefix or varying-gradient\ntail. The range (8) remains explicit.\n\n### Full attainment checks\n\nOne has\n\n    <y,r_exact>=-tau<y,e>/sigma>=0,\n    0<=<e,r_exact><=a/M_0,\n    <q,r_exact>=(-epsilon A kappa+tau a kappa)/(sigma M_1)<0.\n\nThe last sign follows from tau<=a kappa epsilon/D and\nA kappa-a^2 kappa^2/D>0. Also the first coordinate of r_exact is positive:\nit is at least epsilon w_1/sigma, since tau<=a kappa epsilon/D.\n\nAt every first-block proximal point z=(t,0), 0<=t<=a, the difference\nbetween the fourth plane and the p-plane is affine in t. At t=0 it\nis -<r_exact,y><=0. At t=a it is <r_exact,e>-a/M_0<=0. Hence the\nfourth plane preserves every first jet. At every second-block proximal\npoint y+(M_1-u)q it has value (M_1-u)<r_exact,q><=0 and so preserves\nevery second jet. The origin is still a minimizer.\n\nFor partial tail mass u in [0,T], let Z=y+epsilon d-(u+1)r_exact.\nThe p-plane is <=0 because its first-coordinate numerator is bounded\nby kappa(1+epsilon)-A<=0. The q-plane is <=0 because\n\n M_1<q,Z>=-epsilon A kappa\n       +(u+1)(epsilon A kappa-tau a kappa)/sigma<=0.\n\nThe new plane is nonnegative: from epsilon d=sigma r_exact+tau e,\n\n    <r_exact,Z-y>\n       =(\n          sigma-u-1\n         )||r_exact||^2+tau<r_exact,e>\n       >=T||r_exact||^2>=0.\n\nThus this one fixed four-plane envelope gives the entire desired tail\nwith actual gradient r_exact. Its terminal score is\n\n    2epsilon<d,r_exact>-sigma||r_exact||^2\n      =[epsilon^2||d||^2-tau^2 D]/sigma,\n\nwhich is (32). The actual closest minimizer remains the origin by the\nsame p-plane argument as Section2. The terminal proximal value is\n\n    T||r_exact||^2+tau<r_exact,e>,                         (33)\n\nwith <r_exact,e>=a/M_0 when tau>0. Both sources of retained value are\nexplicit; neither is discarded.\n\n### Optimality for the entire fixed-prefix constant-tail class\n\nThe seed conjugate maximum, including the optimum and all first two\nblock queries, is exactly\n\n m_seed(r)=max(0, <(a,0),r>-a/M_0,\n                 <y,r>,\n                 <y,r>+(M_1-1)(<q,r>-||q||^2)).           (34)\n\nAll intermediate terms are convex combinations of the two endpoints\non each block; every endpoint is an actual retained query. The formula\nalso includes empty blocks, when the corresponding endpoints coincide.\nFor any legal tail with gradient r, its start value is bounded by the\noutgoing seed inequalities, and the equal-gradient values decrease by\nT||r||^2. Hence its terminal value is at most\n\n    sup_r [<y+epsilon d,r>-sigma||r||^2/2-m_seed(r)].        (35)\n\nAt r_exact the y-term in (34) dominates zero, and it dominates the last\nterm because <q,r_exact><0. It also dominates the (a,0)-term since\n<e,r_exact><=a/M_0. If tau>0 those two terms tie; if tau=0 the\ny-term alone can be used. Thus y+tau e is a convex combination of\nactive slopes in (34). The exact identity\n\n    y+epsilon d=sigma r_exact+y+tau e\n\nis the global optimality condition for the strictly concave expression\nin (35). This proves its maximum is V_exact(kappa)/2. The constructed\nenvelope attains it while satisfying ALL incoming inequalities too.\nFor extra orthogonal dimensions, the old slopes and the start position\nhave zero components, and the term -sigma||r||^2/2 strictly penalizes\neach new orthogonal component. Thus they cannot improve (35).\n\n## 8. Explicit optimum over the admissible angle and its finite budget\n\nSet\n\n    kappa_lim=A/(1+epsilon),\n    kappa_0=A(A+a)/(A+2a),\n    kappa_free=kappa_0+a sigma/[M_0 epsilon(A+a)].\n\nThe exact maximizer of (32) over 0<kappa<=kappa_lim is\n\n    kappa_best=min(kappa_lim,kappa_free).                 (36)\n\nThis includes a=0, when kappa_free=A>kappa_lim and tau=0. To prove\nthe nonzero-a formula, (32) increases linearly while\nkappa<=sigma/(M_0 epsilon). Above this threshold put\n\n    c=sigma/M_0, b=A+2a, C=(A+a)^2,\n    u=kappa-c/epsilon, C_t=C-bc/epsilon, D=C_t-bu.\n\nWhen this active region meets (0,A), C_t>a^2>0. Apart from the positive\nfactor epsilon^2/(sigma D^2), the derivative is\n\n    A C_t^2-2 C C_t u+b C u^2.\n\nIts smaller root gives exactly kappa_free, and its larger root gives\nA+a-a c/[epsilon(A+a)]>A. Hence the derivative changes from positive\nto negative only at kappa_free in the admissible region. If the positive\npart never activates before kappa_lim, the score is increasing and\nkappa_free>=kappa_lim; indeed either c/epsilon<A and\nkappa_free>c/epsilon, or c/epsilon>=A and kappa_free>=A.\nThis proves (36) in every case, including equality at a breakpoint.\n\nWhen the optimum is interior, its parameters and score simplify to\n\n    tau_best=epsilon A/(A+2a),\n    V_best=epsilon^2 kappa_0^2/(2T+1)\n                      +2epsilon A a/[M_0(A+2a)].          (37)\n\nThe second term is a retained old height, but it is NOT available for\narbitrarily long tails. Interiority requires kappa_free<kappa_lim,\nwhich is equivalent, for a>0, to\n\n    2T+1 < [M_0 epsilon(A+a)/a](kappa_lim-kappa_0).         (38)\n\nIn particular the right side must be positive. Once the threshold is\nreached, (36) fixes kappa_best=kappa_lim and one must use the exact\nboundary formula (32). If 2T+1>=M_0 epsilon kappa_lim, tau=0 there\nand the score is precisely epsilon^2 A kappa_lim/(2T+1), which decays\nwith the tail mass. This identifies the finite scale budget behind the\nseemingly tail-independent term in (37), without an o(1) shortcut.\n\n## Remaining interface\n\nThe invariant (2) identifies a structural limitation of repeatedly using\none maximal interpolant. The new fourth plane explicitly creates the\nproximal value needed to survive an arbitrary tail, and (18) gives its\nquantitative schedule cost without unspecified feasible parameters.\nThe missing target-level step is still to compare a sufficiently rich\nlegal construction uniformly against the complete sequence B_n.\n\nMatlas searches supplied no external theorem used here. The previously\ndownloaded exact text of Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666v6,\nRemark2 and its preceding interpolation proof, was read for context;\nall identities used above are proved from the accepted project fact and\nthe displayed elementary constructions, not from an external black box.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["1ed052539a27d4ff"]}, "glossary": {"sigma": "Exact tail mass denominator 2T+1.", "r_exact": "Gradient (epsilon d-tau e)/sigma on every sample of the optimized constant-gradient tail.", "tau": "a(kappa epsilon-sigma/M_0)_+/D, the active mixture coefficient of the two prefix conjugate planes.", "V_exact": "Exact normalized optimum for this fixed-prefix constant-tail class, formula(32) in the evidence.", "kappa_lim": "A/(1+epsilon), the explicit sufficient range retaining old-plane domination.", "kappa_free": "A(A+a)/(A+2a)+a sigma/[M_0 epsilon(A+a)], the unrestricted critical angle of formula(32).", "kappa_best": "Minimum of kappa_lim and kappa_free.", "M_0": "One plus ordinary prefix mass before the first checkpoint.", "M_1": "One plus mass between the two checkpoints.", "D": "Squared norm of e=(a,0)-y, equal to (A-kappa)(A+2a)+a²."}}
{"id": "a3fd26438333d51c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:01:01.690920+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C31 complete energy-preserving affine-gradient reduction: every primitive witness with q distinct gradients has a realization in dimension<=q-1, including a minimizer, with EVERY finite Q and V unchanged. Any two-gradient witness is therefore scalar after this transformation. More sharply, for an arbitrary-dimensional trajectory with just one transition between two constant-gradient phases at step b, universal V>=0 is EQUIVALENT to the existing single-cut condition D(b-1)_+<=(1+P)(1+Q). All values and all remote cross pairs are retained; merely adding dimension or changing the two angles cannot strengthen that test.", "evidence": "# Affine gradient dimension and the exact limit of two constant-gradient phases\n\nStatus: self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit. The only accepted input is finite smooth-convex interpolation\nnecessity from fact 1252b8823bb5a290; the realizing conjugate construction\nis also proved below. Prior unverified projection findings are not used.\n\nFor a nonnegative schedule h of length n, let P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and\nS=P_n. On an actual 1-smooth convex GD trajectory define\n\n    V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n        +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n        -S(S+1)||g_n||^2.\n\n## 1. Exact removal of the common orthogonal gradient component\n\nLet E=span{g_i-g_0:0<=i<=n}. There is a constant vector c in E^perp\nsuch that every g_i=v_i+c, with v_i in E. Define\n\n    y_i=projection_E x_i,   f'_i=f_i-<c,x_i>.\n\nThen y_(i+1)=y_i-h_i v_i. Moreover, every finite interpolation residual\nis unchanged:\n\n    f'_i-f'_j+<v_i,y_j-y_i>+||v_i-v_j||^2/2\n      =f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.    (1)\n\nIndeed the component of x_j-x_i in E^perp is -(P_j-P_i)c; the\ncorresponding inner product is exactly canceled by the value correction.\nThis proves (1) for EVERY ordered pair, not just consecutive queries.\n\nThese data are realizable in E by an everywhere finite differentiable\nconvex function with gradient Lipschitz constant one and a minimizer.\nTo see this directly, put\n\n    a_i=<v_i,y_i>-f'_i,\n    H(z)=max_i {a_i+<y_i,z-v_i>+||z-v_i||^2/2},\n    F(y)=sup_z {<y,z>-H(z)}.\n\nThe inequalities (1) imply H(v_i)=a_i and y_i in partial H(v_i).\nThe function H is finite and 1-strongly convex, and grows quadratically.\nThus for each y its conjugate maximization has a unique point z(y).\nStrong monotonicity of partial H gives\n||z(y)-z(w)||<=||y-w||; comparison of the two maximizing expressions\nshows that F is differentiable with gradient z(y). This proves all\nclaimed regularity and interpolation F(y_i)=f'_i, grad F(y_i)=v_i.\nAn active quadratic of H at zero has a gradient y_* in partial H(0).\nConsequently grad F(y_*)=0 and F(y_*)=-H(0), so a minimizer exists.\nNo preassigned optimum or preservation of its location was assumed.\n\nThe primitive energy is EXACTLY unchanged. Write C=||c||^2. Then\n\n    f'_i-f'_n=(f_i-f_n)-(S-P_i)C,\n    ||v_i||^2=||g_i||^2-C.\n\nUsing 2sum_i h_i P_i=S^2-sum_i h_i^2, the change in V equals\n\n    C[-2sum_i h_i(S-P_i)-sum_i h_i(1-h_i)+S(S+1)]=0. (2)\n\nTherefore a trajectory with q distinct gradients has a realization with\nthe SAME primitive energy in dimension at most q-1. If all gradients\nare equal, V=0 and the transformed function can be taken constant.\nFor a schedule with m positive steps, every negative-energy witness can\nbe realized in dimension at most m, since there are at most m+1 distinct\nsampled gradients. Zero steps simply repeat points and gradients.\n\nIn particular ANY two-gradient witness, including one that revisits its\ntwo gradients many times, has a scalar realization with identical V.\nThis does not assert that its scalar realization is a projection onto\nan interval. The conjugate construction may add other gradients away\nfrom the finite queried trajectory. Normalized objective error is not\nasserted to survive the value correction or the new minimizer.\n\n## 2. One change of gradient: the exact arbitrary-dimensional test\n\nFix one actual checkpoint index j and write\n\n    P=sum_(i<j)h_i, b=h_j, Q=sum_(i>j)h_i,\n    S=P+b+Q, D=1+S, A=P+b.\n\nConsider EVERY legal trajectory, in any finite dimension, satisfying\n\n    g_i=u for 0<=i<=j,\n    g_i=v for j+1<=i<=n.                            (3)\n\nAll function values remain those of the same actual function. Then\n\n    V_h>=0 for all trajectories (3)\n\nholds if and only if\n\n    D(b-1)_+ <= (P+1)(Q+1).                         (4)\n\nThus arbitrary dimension and arbitrary legal values within two constant-\ngradient phases do not strengthen the usual single-cut test at all.\nFor (3) with u!=v, a quantitative version when b>1 is\n\n    V_h >= [(P+b)t/(Q+1)]\n                [(P+1)(Q+1)-D(b-1)],\n    t=||u||^2-<u,v>>0.                             (5)\n\n### Every cross-history constraint is retained\n\nIf u=v then V_h=0, as follows from affine values and the same summation\nas (2). If u!=v then b>0, because a zero step repeats its point. Put\n\n    Delta=f_j-f_(j+1), d=||u-v||^2.\n\nTwo queries with the same gradient must have exactly their affine value\ndifference: apply both interpolation inequalities and combine them.\nFor a query before j at remaining ordinary sum s in [0,P] and a query\nafter j at elapsed sum q in [0,Q], their point displacement is\n(b+s)u+q v and their value difference is\n\n    Delta+s||u||^2+q||v||^2.\n\nConsequently their two ordered interpolation inequalities are exactly\n\n    Delta >= b<u,v>+d/2-s t,\n    Delta <= b||u||^2-d/2-q(d-t).                   (6)\n\nThe adjacent pair gives b t>=d>0, so t>0. The strongest lower bound in\n(6) is at s=0. The strongest upper bound is at q=Q if d>=t, and at\nq=0 otherwise. The queried history contains both those endpoints.\nHence ALL the finite interpolation constraints in (3) reduce exactly to\n\n    b<u,v>+d/2 <= Delta\n       <=b||u||^2-d/2-Q(d-t)_+ .                  (7)\n\nSame-gradient pairs already have equality. The initial ordinary steps\ndo not disappear by assumption: their effect is the monotone term -s t\nin (6). Equation (7) implies, for r=d/t,\n\n    r+Q(r-1)_+ <= b,\n    r <= b                          if 0<b<=1,\n    r <= (b+Q)/(1+Q)                if b>1.         (8)\n\n### The full primitive energy\n\nSumming the actual affine values in the two phases gives\n\n    V_h=A[2Delta+(P-b+1)||u||^2-(A+1)||v||^2].      (9)\n\nFor example, the prefix contribution uses\nsum_(i<=j)h_i P_i=(A^2-sum_(i<=j)h_i^2)/2, and the suffix\ncontribution uses the identical formula for its elapsed sums. No\nreplacement step or surrogate endpoint value appears in (9).\n\nSubstitute the lower bound on Delta from (7), and use\n||v||^2=||u||^2-2t+d. This yields\n\n    V_h >= A[2(P+1)t-A d].                         (10)\n\nIf 0<b<=1 then (8) implies the bracket divided by t is at least\n2(P+1)-(P+b)b>=P+1>0. If b>1, use the other bound in (8) and the\nidentity\n\n    2(P+1)(Q+1)-(P+b)(Q+b)\n       =(P+1)(Q+1)-D(b-1).\n\nThis proves (5) and the sufficient direction of (4), including b<=1.\nThe case b=0 has only u=v and was already treated.\n\n### Necessity is attained by one fixed interval projection\n\nWhen (4) fails, necessarily b>1. Set w=(b-1)/(Q+1)>0, and let\nC=[-w,1]. The fixed function\n\n    F_C(x)=max_(z in C)(xz-z^2/2)\n\nis convex differentiable 1-smooth, has gradient clip(x,-w,1), and\nminimum zero at zero. Start at x_0=P+1. Every prefix gradient up to\ncheckpoint j is 1, because x_j=1 and preceding radii are at least1.\nThe checkpoint lands at 1-b=-(Q+1)w. Every suffix gradient is -w,\nand the terminal point is -w. Every query satisfies\nF_C(x_i)=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. A squared-distance telescope therefore gives\n\n    V_h=(P+1)^2-D^2 w^2<0.                        (11)\n\nThis proves necessity with an actual scalar projection and all original\nsteps retained. It also shows that the sign threshold in (5) is sharp.\n\n## 3. Consequence for the geometric program\n\nSingle-cut tests already cover the ENTIRE arbitrary-dimensional class\n(3), not just a preselected pair of opposite scalar gradients. Enlarging\nthat class by changing angles, ambient dimension, values, or the common\northogonal gradient component cannot supply a new primitive-capacity\ninequality. A new geometric test must have at least two gradient changes.\nTwo gradient values with repeated changes still remain scalar by Section1;\nto exploit higher-dimensional gradient geometry requires at least three\ndistinct gradient values and affine gradient rank at least two.\n\nThese are exact scope limits of witness classes. They do not bound all\nprimitive capacities or solve the unrestricted objective question.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/c31_affine_gradient_dimension_and_two_phase_limit.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "related_unverified": ["c58273e20409955e", "846c8ad5e763dfb7"], "scope": "Exact reduction and witness-class characterization; not universal capacity or normalized-objective preservation", "status": "Unverified; no solver or verifier call"}, "glossary": {"E": "Span of all gradient differences g_i-g_0.", "c": "Common component of all sampled gradients orthogonal to E, removed with an explicit value correction.", "V_h": "Intrinsic primitive energy defined in the attached proof.", "P": "Total ordinary steps before the selected transition step b.", "Q": "Total ordinary steps after b.", "D": "One plus the total step sum.", "t": "||u||^2-<u,v>, positive for a genuine one-transition witness.", "d": "||u-v||^2."}}
{"id": "102610bc6e0e464e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:05:09.953419+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Assigned near-critical ruler is a genuine constant-level gap for the tested mechanisms. For every m>=128 at the original appended b=1+a_m, ALL quadratic curvatures and ALL single-cut witnesses are dominated by K=1/(2 sum H_m+1). Every retained two-block prefix in the explicit cap-angle range, followed by ANY legal constant-gradient tail (all dimensions, affine offsets and positive values), also has normalized objective <=K. The joint proof derives B_(2^m-1)<=1+4(2^m-1)^log_2(1+sqrt2), hence limsup K B<=2/e<1. It also gives exact full-spectrum and first/last-occurrence K formulas for EVERY b>=0, and the exact large-b threshold where the old last-cut witness already matches B. This is a mechanism obstruction on the assigned family, not an upper bound on unrestricted W and not primitive rejection.", "evidence": "# Near-critical ruler: complete quadratic screen and a quantitative cap obstruction\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No solver, numerical sweep, or\nfact_submit was used. All new estimates are proved here; prior unverified\nfindings are not premises. Accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the\ndefinitions and validity of K_n and the exact benchmark recursions; accepted\nfact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies smooth-convex interpolation. This does not\nupper-bound the unrestricted W_n and does not settle the project target.\n\n## 1. Exact schedule and all quadratic curvatures, including arbitrary b\n\nFix an integer m>=2. Within this document write\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThe parameters c,a remain fixed for this entire horizon. They are not\nrecomputed when j changes. The symbol H_j here names a schedule, not the\nconjugate jet quadratic from the project glossary. Put r=c-2 and A_mass=a/r.\nCounting the occurrences of each level gives, for 0<=j<=m,\n\n    S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2),\n    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                       (1)\n\nThere are 2^(m-j-1) occurrences of d_j in H_m. Its scalar multiplier at\ncurvature t in [0,1] is exactly\n\n    P_m(t)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^(2^(m-j-1)).     (2)\n\nLet p_m be H_m without its final d_0 and let h^(m,b)=(p_m,b), b>=0. Then\n\n    sum h^(m,b)=S_m-d_0+b,\n    R_(m,b)(t)=(1-bt) product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^N_j,\n    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1) for 1<=j<m.           (3)\n\nThe exact worst normalized quadratic objective over ALL finite dimensions\nis\n\n    Q_m(b)=max_(0<=t<=1) t R_(m,b)(t)^2.                (4)\n\nIndeed diagonalizing a positive semidefinite Hessian bounded by the identity\nmakes the normalized error a weighted average of the displayed scalar\nquantities. A scalar quadratic attains the maximum. Zero eigenvalues have\nzero objective contribution; affine translations to a minimizer change\nneither calculation. Thus this is a full quadratic-class computation.\n\nFor an exact finite characterization of (4), subdivide [0,1] at all its\ndistinct interior roots 1/d_j and, when b>0, 1/b. On each root-free open\ninterval the logarithmic derivative of the nonzero objective is\n\n    L(t)=1/t-2b/(1-bt)-2sum_j N_j d_j/(1-d_j t),\n    L'(t)=-1/t^2-2b^2/(1-bt)^2\n                    -2sum_j N_j d_j^2/(1-d_j t)^2<0.   (5)\n\nConsequently there is exactly one interior maximizer between consecutive\nroots, also between zero and the first root. On the last interval the\nmaximum is either its unique root of L or t=1. Values at roots are zero.\nCoincident roots are combined, and b=0 simply removes its term. Equations\n(3)--(5) specify the complete spectrum without a numerical sweep.\n\nThe unit-curvature values alone are\n\n    P_m(1)=-(ac)^(2^m-1)c^(-m),\n    R_(m,b)(1)=(1-b)(ac)^(2^m-2)c^(1-m).                (6)\n\nThey are not used as a replacement for (4).\n\n## 2. Exact single-cut screen for every appended b\n\nFor a cut with step d, preceding mass P and following mass T, the accepted\ncut value is (d-1)_+^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2T)]. For a fixed d and total schedule\nmass S, its reciprocal denominator is\n\n    D(P)=(1+P)^2[1+2(S-d-P)].\n\nIts derivative has the sign of 2(S-d)-3P. Therefore its minimum on any\nclosed interval is attained at an endpoint. Among all occurrences of a\ngiven level it suffices to check the first and last occurrence.\n\nFor h^(m,b), all levels 1<=j<m remain present. Their first and last\npreceding masses are, respectively,\n\n    P_j^first=S_j, P_j^last=S_m-d_j-S_j.                (7)\n\nTheir following masses are S_m-d_0+b-d_j-P. At level zero, after deleting\nthe original last occurrence, the endpoint preceding masses are\n\n    P_0^first=0, P_0^last=S_m-2d_0-d_1,                 (8)\n\nwith following masses S_m-2d_0+b and d_1+b. For m=2 the two occurrences\nin (8) refer to the same remaining index. The appended b contributes\n\n    (b-1)_+^2/(1+S_m-d_0)^2.                           (9)\n\nTaking the maximum of the cuts in (7)--(9) and\n1/[1+2(S_m-d_0+b)] is EXACTLY K_n(h^(m,b)). No omitted internal cut can\nbe larger, by the denominator argument. In particular the accepted final\ncut already proves W_n(h^(m,b))>=1/B_n whenever\n\n    b>=1+(1+S_m-d_0)/sqrt(B_n).                        (10)\n\nThe global Huber term proves the same comparison whenever\nb<=(B_n-1)/2-(S_m-d_0), if that right side is nonnegative. These tests use\nthe actual B_n, not a guessed dyadic constant.\n\n## 3. An exact all-cut result at the original final step\n\nFor the remainder take m>=128 unless indicated, and first set b=d_0, so\nthe actual schedule is H_m. The following rational bounds will be used:\n\n    12/5<lambda<c<49/20<5/2,\n    3/8<a<lambda-2<1/2,\n    ac>=127/128>7/8,\n    0<a<c-2=r<=9/20.                                   (11)\n\nFor example lambda<17/7 gives\nc<2193/896<49/20 for m>=128; a=(1-1/m)/c then exceeds3/8.\nThe upper bound a<lambda-2 follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda].\nConsequently 0<A_mass<1, and (1) gives\n\n    M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j,\n    a c^j<=r M_j.                                      (12)\n\nLet M=M_(m-1). For a first occurrence of any level j, P=S_j and its\nfollowing mass is at least S_(m-1). Its cut is at most\nr^2/(2M-1). For a last occurrence, its preceding mass is at least\nS_(m-1), and its following mass is S_j. Since x^2/(2x-1) is increasing\nfor x>=1 and M_j<=M, its cut is also at most\n\n    r^2 M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)]<=r^2/(2M-1).              (13)\n\nThese statements include the unique central occurrence, for which the\nfirst and last positions coincide. Using c<=5/2, r<=1/2 and M>=1,\n\n    r^2(2M_m-1)<= (5M-1)/4 <= 2M-1.\n\nThus every single-cut term is no larger than the global Huber term:\n\n    K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                               (14)\n\n## 4. Full-spectrum quadratic bound at b=d_0\n\nThis argument controls every t in [0,1], not only t=1. Write\nv_j=a c^j t and, for the partial ruler H_j,\n\n    Y_j=c v_j |P_j(t)|.\n\nIf no level 0,...,m-1 satisfies d_j t>=1, all factors in (2) are\nnonnegative, and 1-u<=exp(-u) gives\n\n    t P_m(t)^2<=t exp(-2S_m t)<=1/(2e S_m).             (15)\n\nThe last maximum is over all nonnegative t, so it also bounds the actual\nrestricted interval. Suppose instead that j is the first level with\nd_j t>=1. If j=0, Y_0=ac t<=1. If j>=1, all factors in P_j are\nnonnegative and t<=1/(1+a)<=8/11. Equations (1), (11) give\n\n    t S_j>=v_j/r-t,\n    Y_j<=c v_j exp(-v_j/r+t)<=cr exp(t-1)<1.            (16)\n\nHere the maximum of v exp(-v/r) is r/e, cr<5/4, and\nexp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4. For every subsequent level l>=j the\nfactor is on its nonpositive side, and the exact recursion is\n\n    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]Y_l^2<=Y_l^2<=1.             (17)\n\nThe bracket lies in [0,1]. If an earlier factor is zero the conclusion\nholds directly as well. Therefore\n\n    t P_m(t)^2<=1/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)t]\n                      <=d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)),      (18)\n\nusing t>=1/d_(m-1). Equations (15),(18) prove the explicit full-spectrum\nestimate\n\n    Q_m(d_0)<=max{1/(2e S_m),\n                 (1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))}.      (19)\n\nBoth entries are strictly below (14). For the first, S_m>=1 gives\n(2S_m+1)/(2e S_m)<=3/(2e)<3/4. For the second, using (12) and ac>7/8,\n\n    (2S_m+1)(1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))\n       <=2/[(ac)^2 c^m]+2/[c^2 ac]\n       <=16/49+25/63=2233/3087<3/4.                   (20)\n\nFor the first term of this last bound it already suffices that m>=3\nand c>2. Thus, with considerable slack,\n\n    Q_m(d_0)<(3/4)K_n(H_m).                            (21)\n\n## 5. Exact benchmark comparison, with no fitted constant\n\nLet rho=log_2(lambda). The accepted benchmark defines the primitive mass\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1 with join\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n\nand B_n with join G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. We prove directly\nfrom these recursions that\n\n    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                     (22)\n\nPut F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, t>0. Direct differentiation, with t=exp(z), gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp z)\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe strict inequality follows by integrating the positive derivative of\n(1+t)^rho-1-rho t for t>0. Convexity at z and -z gives\nF(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. If A=t^rho B and\nD=(1+t)^rho B, this says (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, or\nD^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. Hence\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.\n\nZero arguments follow by continuity. Induction in the primitive\nrecursion proves the first bound in (22). Since G(A,D)<=4A+D,\ninduction in the dominant recursion, with a split k and l=n-k-1,\ngives B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho. The last inequality follows\nby integrating the increasing derivative of x^rho, including l=0.\n\nIn our family (1) implies\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n       =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m] -> e,        (23)\n\nsince A_mass->1 and 2/c is bounded below1. Thus the EXACT finite bound\n\n    K_n(H_m) B_n\n      <=[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2M_m-1)                     (24)\n\nhas limit 2/e<1. This uses an upper bound derived for the actual B_n\nat every integer horizon; it assumes no asymptotic phase or decimal\nvalue for B_n/lambda^m. In particular, the full quadratic and all-cut\nbaseline is strictly below U_n^ZJ for every sufficiently large m.\n\n## 6. Retained two-block geometry and all constant tails in its cap range\n\nHere is the precise mechanism tested next. For ANY two selected cuts i<j\nof an arbitrary schedule, let P be the mass before i, S the mass strictly\nbetween i and j, T the mass after j, and define\n\n    M_0=1+P, M_1=1+S,\n    A=(h_i-1)/M_0, epsilon=(h_j-1)/M_1, sigma=2T+1.\n\nAssume h_i,h_j>1 and choose the cap-range angle\n\n    0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon).                            (25)\n\nUse the retained prefix gradients and proximal point\n\n    p=(1,0)/M_0,\n    q=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)))/M_1,\n    X=(-A,0), y=X-M_1 q, d=-M_1 q.                    (26)\n\nThese prefix data are actual jets of the single Moreau envelope of\nJ(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>), starting at (1,0). Before the first checkpoint\nthe proximal points are (1-(u+1)/M_0,0), 0<=u<=P, so the p-plane is\nactive. That checkpoint, of size1+A M_0, sends its input p to X.
