{"id": "43bba983fbe9b3a9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:44:52.572623+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Quantitative physical-length consequence of the complete three-step credit capacity proof: any schedule assembled by the stated Omega/phi primitive joins from ordinary benchmark-capped leaves and arbitrary three-step E_theta-valid leaves, then by the stated psi dominant joins, satisfies W_N(H)>=U_N^ZJ at EVERY horizon. All bridge steps and initial-distance normalization are retained. This rules out an improving asymptotic family in that precise construction class, not the unrestricted target.", "evidence": "# Three-step capacity with arbitrary signed terminal credit\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No SDP/PEP or\nfact_submit call. This closes the proposed three-step discovery domain if\nthe candidate survives verification. It does not settle the general signed\ncone question or the original all-horizon normalized objective target.\n\n## 1. Statement and definitions\n\nLet h=(u,v,w) be a nonnegative three-step schedule, let T=1+u+v+w, and put\nt=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, T_0=lambda^2=3+2t. Along gradient descent on a convex\ndifferentiable function f with 1-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer, write\nx_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)+sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                             -(T-1)T||g_3||^2,\n J_h=sum_(i<3)h_i Q_(i,3), E_theta=V_h-theta J_h.\n\nClaim. If T>=T_0 and h differs from (t,2,t), there is a fixed, finite-dimensional\nconvex differentiable 1-smooth function, with a minimizer, whose full h-run has\n\n              V_h<0 and Q_(i,3)=0 for every i<3.       (1)\n\nConsequently, for EVERY real theta, universal validity of E_theta>=0 with\nT>=T_0 forces h=(t,2,t). The same necessary conclusion holds for any intrinsic\ncertificate V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij whose values cancel, whose remainder is PSD\non the complete independent formal gradient space, and whose multipliers are\nnonnegative except for arbitrary signs on incoming-terminal finite pairs.\nNo validity-versus-certifiability equivalence is assumed.\n\nAt h=(t,2,t), an explicit ordinary primitive certificate is given in Section 7.\nTherefore, for each theta>=0, the exact maximum three-step capacity in the\nE_theta-valid class and in its nonnegative-certificate class is T_0, uniquely\nattained by Silver. The arbitrary-signed-certificate class has that same\nmaximum. In particular the conclusion applies throughout 0<=theta<1.\n\nThe proof includes the required domain reductions directly. C32 and earlier\nmax4 findings motivated them, but are not used as unverified premises.\n\n## 2. Legal projection witnesses and the first four constraints\n\nHere is a self-contained way to turn each finite array below into a fixed\nlegal function. Suppose its GD equations hold, its terminal point satisfies\nx_3=g_3, and its assigned values are\n\n f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nSet y_i=x_i-g_i. Expansion gives\n\n Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_(i,3)=0,\n V_h=||x_0||^2-T^2||g_3||^2.                         (2)\n\nIf every finite Q_ij<=0 and <g_i,y_i>>=0, take K=conv{0,g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3}.\nThose inequalities say precisely <y_j,z-g_j><=0 for every vertex z of K and\nhence for every z in K. Thus g_j=P_K(x_j). The function\n\n F_K(x)=max_(z in K)[<x,z>-||z||^2/2]\n\ninterpolates all assigned values and gradients. The projection normal\ninequalities imply <P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'>>=||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2, so P_K is\n1-Lipschitz. The maximizing formula gives\n0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),d><=<P_K(x+d)-P_K(x),d><=||d||^2,\nwhich proves differentiability with gradient P_K. Thus F_K is globally convex\nand 1-smooth; it has minimum F_K(0)=0. No unverified interpolation theorem is\nneeded for these arrays.\n\nAt any selected step b>1, with preceding and following sums P,Q, use derivative\nclip(x,-(b-1)/(Q+1),1) and start at P+1. The preceding gradients are 1 and the\nfollowing gradients are -(b-1)/(Q+1), ending at that negative threshold.\nThe function obtained by integration has all the properties in (2). Its\nenergy yields the necessary cut inequality\n\n              T(b-1)_+ <= (P+1)(Q+1).               (3)\n\nFor b<=1 this inequality is automatic. This includes schedules with zero\nsteps. The first and last cuts give\n\n              u,w <= M(T):=2T/(T+1)<2.              (4)\n\nFor u,v,w>1 write a=u-1, A=u+1, r=v-1, D=w+1, eta=r/D.\nThe following additional inequalities are necessary if no witness (1) exists:\n\n F_Q=1-T^2 a^2 r^2(w-1)^2 >=0,                     (5)\n F_B=A^2-T^2 eta^2 >=0,                            (6)\n F_C=A^2-T^2 r^2(w-1)^2 >=0,                       (7)\n F_N=a u F_B+(1+eta)u^2(2-u^2) >=0.                (8)\n\nFor (5), use f(x)=x^2/2 and x_0=1. For (6), use the middle cut in (3),\nwhose x=(A,1,-r,-eta), g=(1,1,-eta,-eta). For (7), provided r(w-1)<=1,\nuse derivative min(x,1), start at A, and obtain\nx=(A,1,-r,r(w-1)), g=(1,1,-r,r(w-1)). It is a fixed convex 1-smooth\nfunction with minimum at0, and its terminal point is inside its quadratic\npart. Each of these functions satisfies (2).\n\nFor (8), provided eta a<1, combine the middle-cut base from (6) with the\nauxiliary array\n\n x_aux=(1,-a,ra,eta a), g_aux=(1,-a,eta a,eta a),\n f_aux,i=x_aux,i*g_aux,i-g_aux,i^2/2.\n\nThe auxiliary normals are (0,0,w eta a,0). Its only positive finite residual\nis Q_aux,(0,2)=(1-eta a)w eta a. Every outgoing optimum inequality holds,\nsince each g_aux,i*y_aux,i is nonnegative. In the base array,\nQ_base,(0,2)=-(1+eta)w eta, and all finite residuals are nonpositive.\nTake their orthogonal sum with auxiliary scale sqrt(epsilon), where\n\n                   epsilon=(1+eta)/[a(1-eta a)].\n\nResiduals add, the sole positive auxiliary edge is canceled exactly, every\nother finite edge is nonpositive, and every optimum condition holds.\nThe projection construction above realizes one fixed legal function. Its\nenergy is F_B+epsilon(1-T^2 eta^2 a^2)=F_N/[a(1-eta a)]; the denominator\nis positive. The equality follows by 1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2).\nThe terminal point stays core, so every incoming-terminal residual is zero.\n\n## 3. Complete reduction to the asymmetric region\n\nAssume T>=T_0 and assume, for contradiction, that every applicable witness\nabove has nonnegative energy. First v>1: otherwise T<=2+2M(T) gives\nT^2-5T-2<=0, whereas at T_0 this polynomial equals 2t>0 and it increases\nthereafter. Thus r>0 and the middle cut applies, initially with A=u+1,D=w+1.\nSince T=A+D+r, it gives\n\n T(T-A-D)<=AD<=(A+D)^2/4,\n T<=lambda(A+D)/2<=lambda(M(T)+1).\n\nHence T^2+(1-3lambda)T-lambda<=0. The polynomial increases for T>=T_0,\nand its value at7 is 56-22lambda>0 because lambda<5/2. Thus T<7, and\nu,w<M(7)=7/4. Both endpoints exceed1: if u<=1, then A<=2,D<11/4 and\nr>T-19/4, so Tr>T(T-19/4)>=T_0(T_0-19/4)=11/4+5t/2>11/2>AD,\na contradiction. The proof for w is identical.\n\nThe extra hypotheses needed in Section 2 now hold everywhere in this box:\n\n r(w-1)<=A(w^2-1)/T<363/(64T_0)<1,\n eta a<=Aa/T=(u^2-1)/T<33/(16T_0)<1.                (9)\n\nFor the first numerical inequality, 363<64T_0 follows from\n171<128sqrt(2), whose square is 29241<32768. Also\n\n                   u+w>=2T/lambda-2>=2t.            (10)\n\nIf u<=t, then w>=t. The left-minus-right difference in the necessary\ninequality Tr(w-1)<=A from (7) is at least\nT_0(T_0-t-w-2)(w-1)-(t+1)=T_0(w-t)(2-w)>=0.\nIt is positive except when u=w=t,T=T_0; equality then gives v=2.\nThus every nonexceptional candidate has u>t.\n\nIf w>=t as well, consider q_T(u,w)=T(u-1)(w-1)(T-u-w-2) on\n[t,M(T)]^2. It is strictly concave in each endpoint separately. Its minimum\nis at a corner; strict concavity excludes equality at an interior endpoint\nwhen corner minima have already been identified. At T_0, since\nM(T_0)=1+t/2, the four corner values are 1,lambda/2,lambda/2,lambda/2,\nwith the value1 only at (t,t). All four corner values strictly increase with\nT>=T_0: their factors are positive and increasing, including\nT-t-M(T)-2 and T-2M(T)-2, whose derivatives are respectively\n1-2/(T+1)^2 and 1-4/(T+1)^2. Thus (5) excludes this case except Silver.\nEvery other candidate has u>t>w.\n\nFor such endpoints, F_N strictly decreases as r>0 increases, with u,w fixed:\nthe negative squared term in F_B decreases and u^2(2-u^2)<0. At r=1,\n\n D^2 F_N/u=u^2 D^2+(4-2u^2-u^3)D-(u-1)(u+2)^2=:P_u(D).\n\nThis quadratic has one positive and one negative root. At D=lambda,\nP_u(lambda)=-(lambda+1)u^3-2u^2+4(lambda+1)<0 for u>t: it vanishes\nat t and has negative derivative in u>0. Its positive root is therefore\nbeyond lambda. Since D<lambda, F_N<0 when r=1, and then also for r>=1.\nNecessarily r<1. We have proved, with every witness hypothesis checked,\n\n       t<u<7/4, 1<v<2, 1<w<t, T_0<=T<7,\n                       u+w>=2T/lambda-2.             (11)\n\n## 4. The additional two-cut witness forces u<8/5\n\nHere the different letters R=u-1, k=v-1, C=w+1, B=v+w+1=C+k+1 are used\nto avoid confusing the first and second bridge. Thus T=B+R+1.\nSet eta_1=R/B, zeta=k/C, p=-1/R. Consider the two scalar arrays\n\n x_base=(1,-R,-C eta_1,-eta_1),\n g_base=(1,-eta_1,-eta_1,-eta_1),\n x_aux=(p,1,-k,-zeta), g_aux=(p,1,-zeta,-zeta).\n\nBoth obey the same three GD steps; assign values by f=g*x-g^2/2.\nTheir normal arrays are respectively\n(0,-(B-1)eta_1,-w eta_1,0) and (0,0,-w zeta,0).\nThe base is an actual clipped-derivative trajectory. Within (11),\nR zeta<3/8<1. The auxiliary array's only positive residual is\nQ_aux,(0,2)=w zeta(1/R-zeta); all its other finite residuals are zero\nexcept Q_aux,(1,2)<0. Every optimum inequality holds in both arrays.\nThe base residual Q_base,(0,2)=-(1+eta_1)w eta_1 cancels that positive\nedge when the orthogonal auxiliary scale is sqrt(epsilon_1), where\n\n             epsilon_1=eta_1(1+eta_1)/[zeta(1/R-zeta)].\n\nAll other finite inequalities remain nonpositive. The projection realization\nin Section 2 supplies a fixed globally legal function; its terminal point is\ncore and every incoming-terminal Q is zero. Its energy is\n\n V_mix=1-T^2 eta_1^2+epsilon_1(R^(-2)-T^2 zeta^2)\n      =(C+kB)(B+R)/[B^2 k(C-Rk)]\n                     *{C-Rk[1+R(T+1)]}.             (12)\n\nThe prefactor is positive. For an algebra audit, substitute\nepsilon_1=R^2 C^2(B+R)/[B^2 k(C-Rk)], put the two energy terms over\nthat denominator, and use B=C+k+1,T=B+R+1. Thus nonnegative energy forces\n\n                    R k[1+R(T+1)]<=C.              (13)\n\nIn (11), w<t and T>=T_0 imply\nk=T-2-u-w>1+t-u=t-R, C<lambda. Therefore (13) implies\n\n            R(t-R)[1+(T_0+1)R]<lambda.              (14)\n\nThe function H(R)=R(t-R)(1+(T_0+1)R) strictly increases on\nt-1<R<3/4. Indeed its derivative is\nt-2R+(T_0+1)R(2t-3R); here t-2R>-1/10, T_0+1>6,\nR>2/5 and 2t-3R>1/2, so H'(R)>11/10>0. These bounds follow from\n7/5<t<3/2. At R=3/5,\n\n           H(3/5)=(201t+27)/125>1+t,\n\nbecause the difference is (76t-98)/125>0. Thus (14) forces R<3/5,\nor u<8/5. This is the extra information absent from the four-test screen.\n\n## 5. The quadratic witness forces a small imbalance\n\nFix the u,w now under consideration and reduce the formal capacity to T_0,\nthat is, put v_0=T_0-1-u-w. Write\n\n q=t-1, delta=u-t, z=t-w, d=delta-z=u+w-2t.\n\nBy (10), (11), and Section 4,\n\n       0<delta<8/5-t<1/5, 0<z<=delta,\n                   0<=d<delta, v_0=2-d>1.           (15)\n\nFor fixed u,w, the product T(u-1)(w-1)(T-u-w-2) increases with T when\nT-u-w-2>0. Thus (5) at the actual capacity implies its version at T_0.\nSince T_0 q^2=1, that version is\n\n             (q+delta)(q-delta+d)(1-d)<=q^2.\n\nExpanding and rearranging gives\n\n d[(q+delta)(1-d)-q^2+delta^2]<=delta^2.\n\nBecause d<=delta, the bracket is at least (1-q)(q+delta)>0. Hence\n\n d/delta <= delta/[(1-q)(q+delta)] < 4/7 < 3/5.     (16)\n\nFor the strict bound, the fraction increases with delta, so delta<1/5\nreduces it to 1/[(1-q)(5q+1)]. Since 2/5<q<1/2,\n(1-q)(5q+1)>=7/4: subtracting 7/4 gives\n(1/2-q)(5q-3/2)>=0. This proves (16) by rational bounds alone.\n\n## 6. The mixed witness contradicts that small imbalance\n\nFor fixed u,w and u>t, write eta=(T-u-w-2)/(w+1). An exact expression is\n\n F_N/u = u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-(u-1)T^2 eta^2.       (17)\n\nIt strictly decreases with T in the relevant range, because eta increases,\nu(2-u^2)<0, u-1>0, and eta>0. Thus F_N>=0 at the actual T would imply\nF_N>=0 at T_0. At T_0, hold u=t+delta fixed and vary d; then\nw=t-delta+d and eta=(1-d)/(lambda-delta+d). This eta strictly decreases\nwith d, while the right side of (17) strictly decreases with eta. Thus\nF_N/u at T_0 strictly increases with d.\n\nBy (16), it suffices to show negativity at the LARGER comparison value\nd=3delta/5, that is, w=t-2delta/5 and v_0=2-3delta/5.\nLet D=w+1,A=u+1,r=v_0-1 at this comparison point. The numerator\n\n N(delta)=D^2 F_N/u\n         =(u-1)(A^2 D^2-T_0^2 r^2)\n                              +(T_0-A)D u(2-u^2)\n\nexpands exactly to\n\n N(delta)=delta[ -4-18t/5\n          +(376+294t)delta/25\n          -(128+70t)delta^2/25\n          +(29-3t)delta^3/25\n          -6delta^4/25 ].                           (18)\n\nFor 0<delta<1/5, discard the two negative higher-order terms and use\ndelta<=1/5, delta^3<=1/125, and 29-3t<29. The bracket is at most\n\n -4-18t/5+(376+294t)/125+29/3125\n                   =-(3071+3900t)/3125<0.           (19)\n\nThus N(delta)<0, contradicting the required nonnegative F_N at T_0.\nThis eliminates every candidate in (11). Consequently the original assumption\nthat all the listed legal witnesses have nonnegative energy is impossible\nfor T>=T_0 outside Silver. A negative witness from this finite explicit list\ntherefore supplies (1), with its whole trajectory and all terminal slacks.\n\n## 7. Attainment and precise consequences\n\nAt h=(t,2,t), use the following nonnegative finite-pair multipliers:\n\n lambda_01=2, lambda_10=2lambda,\n lambda_21=2t lambda, lambda_31=lambda_13=2lambda,\n lambda_23=2lambda^2+2t lambda, lambda_32=2lambda^3,\n\nwith every unlisted entry zero. Expansion of the four value coefficients\nand ten independent gradient monomials, using t^2=2, gives\n\n                       V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0.\n\nHence V_h>=0 on every legal trajectory. For theta>=0, adding theta h_i\nto every incoming-terminal multiplier gives a nonnegative certificate for\nE_theta with the same zero remainder. This proves all the attainment claims.\n\nIf a putative arbitrary-signed incoming-terminal certificate exists for a\nnon-Silver h with T>=T_0, evaluate it on (1): the signed terms vanish, all\nother multiplier terms are nonpositive, and the PSD remainder would be\nat most V_h<0. This is a contradiction on the full formal matrix.\n\nThe result is an exact three-step capacity statement, not a claim that\nE_theta-validity always forces ordinary primitivity below this capacity or\nfor longer schedules. It supplies no new upper schedule; the restricted\nobjective comparison below keeps the construction assumptions explicit.\nAll new mathematics in this note remains unverified.\n\n## 8. Physical lengths and an all-horizon comparison for the specified joins\n\nThere is a precise restricted comparison with the original objective profile.\nThis section uses the accepted definitions and global Huber lower bound in\nfact b98bb807d514e80d. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, where\nS_circle and S_bullet are the exact Zhang--Jiang recursions in that fact.\nTheir capacity forms, including C_0=B_0=1, are\n\n C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n Omega(A,D)=[A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD)]/2,\n B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.                      (20)\n\nBoth operations increase in each positive argument. Directly,\nC_1=lambda, C_2=Omega(1,lambda), and C_3=T_0. To check the last equality,\nOmega(lambda,lambda)=lambda^2=T_0. The other possible split has\nOmega(1,C_2)<T_0. Indeed C_2 is the positive root of\nX^2-(2+t)X-(1+t)=0, and the value of this polynomial at\nX=T_0/t=2+3t/2 is1/2>0. Thus C_2<T_0/t; using\nT_0(T_0-1)/(T_0+1)=T_0/t proves the required strict split comparison by\nthe defining quadratic for Omega(1,C_2).\n\nConsider physical schedules constructed as follows. A primitive input leaf\nof length k is either an ordinary supplied block with sum<=S_circle(k),\nor a three-step block for which E_theta>=0 universally for some real theta.\nThe latter leaf has capacity<=C_3 by the COMPLETE proof in Sections 1--7;\nthis reference is within the same candidate proof, not adoption of an\nunverified external finding. Allow any finite number of primitive joins\n\n              [a,1+r,b], r>0, r(A+D+r)=AD,\n                    A=1+sum a, D=1+sum b.\n\nTheir capacities are exactly Omega(A,D), and their physical lengths are\nlen(a)+1+len(b). Induction on the construction and (20) therefore shows that\nevery resulting length-k primitive-type block has capacity<=C_k. This\nargument includes arbitrarily many prepended bridges, arbitrary association\ntrees and a three-step credited leaf whose parameters depend on the horizon.\n\nFor dominant-type schedules start with the empty schedule, of mass1, and\nallow the physical joins\n\n                       [a,psi(sum a,sum b),b],\n\nwhere a is a block from the preceding class and b is a previously constructed\ndominant-type schedule. The physical length is again len(a)+1+len(b), and\nits mass 1+2sum of all steps is G(1+sum a,1+2sum b), exactly as in the\naccepted recurrence. Induction gives\n\n              1+2sum H <= B_N for every such length-N H.\n\nThe global Huber bound from fact b98bb807d514e80d then yields the ACTUAL\nnormalized objective comparison\n\n                  W_N(H)>=1/(1+2sum H)>=1/B_N=U_N^ZJ.       (21)\n\nHere W_N is the supremum of 2(f(x_N)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all\nfinite-dimensional convex L-smooth functions with a minimizer and all\nnonoptimal initial points, following that exact predetermined schedule.\nThus the true initial-distance normalization is retained. The terminal\nobjective conversion obtained by appending psi(sum a,0) is a special case.\n\nEquation (21) rules out improvement at EVERY horizon within this specified\nconstruction class using three-step credited leaves, with all bridge steps\nand physical lengths counted. It is stronger than saying one calibration is\ndominated. It does not cover arbitrary longer E_theta leaves, primitive\ninputs violating the stated sum caps, different joining rules, or arbitrary\nnonnegative schedules. Accordingly it does not settle the project's\nunrestricted asymptotic minimax problem. The only accepted predecessor used\nin this additional comparison is b98bb807d514e80d; the new three-step theorem\nand this combined candidate have not been submitted to the verifier.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_three_step_credit_physical_comparison", "finding_ids": ["3891570aa172ca1d", "6306bdd00914df38"], "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "research/three_step_credit_capacity.md", "status": "complete_combined_candidate_unverified", "scope": "All horizons for the stated Omega/psi construction with3-step credited leaves and benchmark-capped ordinary leaves only.", "no_changed_optimum_normalization": true, "new_pep_evaluations": 0, "fact_submit_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"C_n": "Exact primitive capacity S_circle(n)+1 from accepted factb98bb807d514e80d.", "B_n": "Exact dominant mass2S_bullet(n)+1 from accepted factb98bb807d514e80d.", "Omega": "Positive capacity join [A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD)]/2.", "G": "Dominant mass join2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.", "E_theta": "Intrinsic V_h minus theta times its actual weighted incoming-terminal sum; defined fully in the proof.", "H": "A physical schedule in the exactly specified primitive/dominant construction class of Section8."}}
{"id": "7e35b105762a95f0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:50:44.960941+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Whole varying-gradient mechanism obstruction on the ACTUAL near-critical ruler: every legal ascending first-occurrence Markov relay with consecutive checkpoint levels u,...,v, zero proximal heights at its relay endpoints, arbitrary nonstationary correlations and a freely optimized constant final cell has 2f_end/K<=Gamma_0=3159/4000 for k=v-u=0, and <=Gamma_1*eta^(k-1) for k>=1, where Gamma_1=614365479/640000000<1 and eta=972405/1120000<1. One retained old query forces beta_l/theta_l<=5/7 at each interior transfer. Thus the entire family stays below Huber and its exact-B ratio tends to zero when the number of transfers grows. This does not bound non-Markov or positive-interior-height tails.", "evidence": "# A uniform obstruction for the ascending Markov ruler relay\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate, with no solver or fact_submit.\nThis screens an explicit varying-gradient family on the ACTUAL assigned\nruler. Actual partition and the full candidate data family\n\nFix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, and\n\n    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a c^l,H_l), n=2^m-1,\n    M_l=1+sum H_l, r_l=a c^l, theta_l=r_l/M_l, r=c-2.\n\nEvery parameter is fixed throughout the horizon. Choose ANY integers\n0<=u<=v<=m-1. Select the FIRST occurrence of each consecutive level\nu,u+1,...,v as checkpoints. The initial ordinary block is exactly H_u;\nbetween the first occurrences of level l and l+1 the ordinary block is\nexactly H_l. Let T be the total mass strictly after the selected level v.\nThen T>=M_(m-1)-1, since this first occurrence is in or at the end of\nthe left half of H_m. The entire right copy H_(m-1) follows it.\n\nThe construction uses one constant gradient omega_l on the ordinary\nblock ending at checkpoint l. Its ordinary capacity is\n\n    C_u=M_u, C_l=M_(l-1) for u<l<=v.\n\nChoose arbitrary parameters 0<beta_l<=theta_l for u<=l<v, and an\northonormal sequence e_u,...,e_v in an otherwise unrestricted ambient\nspace. Define actual Euclidean vectors by\n\n    alpha_u=1/M_u, zeta_u=e_u,\n    alpha_(l+1)^2=theta_l beta_l alpha_l^2,\n    a_l=sqrt(beta_l/theta_l),\n    zeta_(l+1)=-a_l zeta_l+sqrt(1-a_l^2)e_(l+1),\n    omega_l=alpha_l zeta_l.                            (1)\n\nStart at x_0=e_u. Let X_l be the start of block l, and put\n\n    Z_l=X_l-C_l omega_l,\n    X_(l+1)=Z_l-r_l omega_l,\n    Z_(l+1)=Z_l-r_l omega_l-M_l omega_(l+1).           (2)\n\nAt the actual ending query of block l, prescribe the gradient omega_l\nand value ||omega_l||^2/2: its proximal height is zero. Within that\nordinary block the gradient remains omega_l at each actual query.\nAfter checkpoint v, allow ANY single common terminal-cell gradient,\narbitrary heights and intercepts, and additional dimensions.\n\nThe family under consideration consists of ALL legal realizations of\nthese data by one fixed convex differentiable 1-smooth function f,\nwith f(0)=min f=0. We do NOT claim every parameter choice in (1) is\nrealizable. The theorem below upper-bounds every legal choice, using\nnecessary old/new interpolation inequalities. Zero beta degeneracies\ncan be included by continuity, or directly: a zero gradient at a zero\nheight endpoint is a minimizer and all subsequent prescribed Markov\ngradients and the actual trajectory are zero.\n\nThe target of the screen is whether beta_l/theta_l can stay close to 1\nover many levels, so that the apparent amplification c r>1 survives.\n\n## 2. Old-query constraints force a fixed loss at every interior transfer\n\nFrom (1), every zeta_l is unit. For l<j, taking successive inner\nproducts gives\n\n    <omega_l,omega_j>\n       =(-1)^(j-l) alpha_l^2 product_(k=l)^(j-1) beta_k.\n\nAlso M_l alpha_(l+1)=r_l alpha_l a_l. Hence the increment in (2)\nis -r_l alpha_l(zeta_l+a_l zeta_(l+1)), orthogonal to every later\nzeta_j, j>=l+1. Since Z_u=0, it follows that\n\n    <omega_l,Z_l>=0.\n\nDefine the adjacent residual R_l=r_l-M_l beta_l. For l+2<=v, (2)\nand the inner products above give the exact identities\n\n    <omega_l,Z_(l+2)>\n       =-alpha_l^2[R_l-beta_l R_(l+1)],\n    <omega_l,omega_(l+2)>=alpha_l^2 beta_l beta_(l+1),\n    alpha_(l+2)^2\n       =alpha_l^2 theta_l theta_(l+1) beta_l beta_(l+1).\n\nThe first actual query in block l+2 has proximal point\nZ_(l+2)+(M_(l+1)-1)omega_(l+2). Its height equals\n(M_(l+1)-1)alpha_(l+2)^2. To see the latter equality, both directions\nof interpolation between constant-gradient queries force their values\nto differ by the common gradient dotted with their position difference;\nthe ending height of this block is zero by assumption.\n\nIn proximal notation y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\nthe accepted interpolation inequality is precisely\n\n    delta_j>=delta_i+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.\n\nApply it from the ending query of block l to the first query in block\nl+2. The first is an old ACTUAL sample, not a discarded auxiliary point.\nUsing the displayed identities yields the necessary condition\n\n    r_l-beta_l(M_l+r_(l+1))\n                   +D_l beta_l beta_(l+1)>=0,         (3)\n    D_l=1+(M_(l+1)-1)theta_l theta_(l+1).\n\nThus no choice of further planes, offsets, ambient dimension, or final\ncompletion can evade (3) while retaining the stated actual data.\n\nFor the assigned parameters the exact mass formula from the joint audit\nis M_l=A_mass c^l+(1-A_mass)2^l, with A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1).\nIn particular\n\n    a<=theta_l<=r<=9/20,\n    c<=49/20, ac>=127/128,\n    M_(l+1)/M_l=2+theta_l.\n\nThese identities give\n\n    D_l/M_l\n       =(1-theta_l theta_(l+1))/M_l\n                         +(2+theta_l)theta_l theta_(l+1)\n       <=1+(1+r)r^2.\n\nSince beta_(l+1)<=theta_(l+1)<=r and c theta_l>=ac,\n\n    1+c theta_l-(D_l/M_l)beta_(l+1)\n       >=1+127/128-[r+(1+r)r^3]\n       >=225609/160000 >7/5.                          (4)\n\nHere the last equality bound substitutes r=9/20 into the increasing\npolynomial r+r^3+r^4; the difference from 7/5 is 1609/160000.\nDivide (3) by M_l and use (4). For EVERY l with l+2<=v,\n\n    beta_l <= (5/7)theta_l,\n    a_l^2 <=5/7.                                     (5)\n\nThis is a fixed loss, not a small perturbation of full antialignment.\nIn this Markov realization the fresh squared component 1-a_l^2 is\nat least 2/7 at every such transfer. The same requirement arises from\njust one retained old query; all remaining inequalities can only\nfurther restrict the family.\n\n## 3. The loss dominates the near-critical amplification\n\nLet k=v-u be the number of Markov transfers. From (1),(5), with the\nlast beta_(v-1) using only beta_(v-1)<=theta_(v-1),\n\n    alpha_v^2 <= M_u^(-2) product_(l=u)^(v-1) theta_l^2\n                                    (5/7)^max(k-1,0).\n\nSince r_v/M_u=c^k theta_u and all theta_l<=r,\n\n    r_v^2 alpha_v^2\n       <=r^2(c r)^(2k)(5/7)^max(k-1,0).               (6)\n\nThe ending query at checkpoint v has zero proximal height. Smoothness\nalong its unit negative-gradient step gives f(Z_v)<=0, so Z_v is an\nactual minimizer of the SAME f, with f(Z_v)=0. The checkpoint sends the\niterate to Z_v-r_v omega_v. If the terminal-cell gradient is g and its\nordinary mass is T, the terminal point is Z_v-r_v omega_v-Tg.\nInterpolation to the actual minimizer Z_v gives\n\n    2f_end<=-2r_v<omega_v,g>-(2T+1)||g||^2\n             <=r_v^2 alpha_v^2/(2T+1).               (7)\n\nThis allows any constant terminal direction, any affine offset, and any\npositive terminal height. The original closest-minimizer normalization\nis still 1: the first checkpoint query is omega_u=e_u/M_u with value\n||omega_u||^2/2, forcing every minimizer z to satisfy <e_u,z><=0.\nOrigin is present, so its distance from x_0=e_u is exactly 1.\n\nSet K=1/(2M_m-1), the global Huber score on H_m. The tail-mass bound\nand M_m<=c M_(m-1) imply\n\n    (2M_m-1)/(2T+1)<=2c-1<=39/10.\n\nPut the explicit rational constants\n\n    eta=(5/7)(441/400)^2=972405/1120000<1,\n    Gamma_0=(9/20)^2(39/10)=3159/4000,\n    Gamma_1=Gamma_0(441/400)^2\n                          =614365479/640000000<1.\n\nHere c r<=441/400. Combining (6),(7), uniformly over EVERY legal\nparameter family and EVERY chosen u,v, yields\n\n    2f_end/K <= Gamma_0                         if k=0,\n    2f_end/K <= Gamma_1 eta^(k-1)                if k>=1. (8)\n\nThus the whole ascending Markov relay, even with a freely optimized\nconstant terminal cell, stays uniformly below the global Huber value.\nIf the number of transfers k tends to infinity, its relative value\ntends to zero geometrically, uniformly in how m,u,v and all beta vary.\nNo dimension, amplitude, or initial-distance factor has been omitted.\n\n## 4. Exact benchmark and the required next change\n\nFor the actual benchmark B_n from accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`, the\nindependent recursion calculation in the joint audit gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 lambda),\n    M_m/lambda^m -> e,\n    limsup B_(2^m-1) K<=2/e.\n\nConsequently (8) gives, for the entire class,\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1) 2f_end<=Gamma_1*2/e<1,\n\nand gives a limit of zero whenever k->infinity. This is an all-horizon\nobstruction for this explicit family, not a finite calibration.\n\nThe tempting choice beta_l/theta_l=1-O(1/m) is impossible at every\ninterior transfer by (5). The initial profile excess c(c-2)>1 cannot\noffset the fixed retained-query loss. A successful near-critical\nvarying-tail construction must therefore leave this first-order Markov\nnormal recursion, retain positive heights at interior relay endpoints,\nuse a different checkpoint organization, or genuinely vary gradients\ninside the ordinary gaps. Correlated changes by themselves do not\nremove the old-query obligation.\n\nThis does not close non-Markov correlations, nonconsecutive checkpoint\npartitions, positive-height intermediate relays, or unrestricted W.\nIt supplies no full target theorem and adds no verifier-accepted fact.\n# Near-critical ruler: complete quadratic screen and a quantitative cap obstruction\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No solver, numerical sweep, or\nfact_submit was used. All new estimates are proved here; prior unverified\nfindings are not premises. Accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the\ndefinitions and validity of K_n and the exact benchmark recursions; accepted\nfact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies smooth-convex interpolation. This does not\nupper-bound the unrestricted W_n and does not settle the project target.\n\n## 1. Exact schedule and all quadratic curvatures, including arbitrary b\n\nFix an integer m>=2. Within this document write\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThe parameters c,a remain fixed for this entire horizon. They are not\nrecomputed when j changes. The symbol H_j here names a schedule, not the\nconjugate jet quadratic from the project glossary. Put r=c-2 and A_mass=a/r.\nCounting the occurrences of each level gives, for 0<=j<=m,\n\n    S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2),\n    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                       (1)\n\nThere are 2^(m-j-1) occurrences of d_j in H_m. Its scalar multiplier at\ncurvature t in [0,1] is exactly\n\n    P_m(t)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^(2^(m-j-1)).     (2)\n\nLet p_m be H_m without its final d_0 and let h^(m,b)=(p_m,b), b>=0. Then\n\n    sum h^(m,b)=S_m-d_0+b,\n    R_(m,b)(t)=(1-bt) product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^N_j,\n    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1) for 1<=j<m.           (3)\n\nThe exact worst normalized quadratic objective over ALL finite dimensions\nis\n\n    Q_m(b)=max_(0<=t<=1) t R_(m,b)(t)^2.                (4)\n\nIndeed diagonalizing a positive semidefinite Hessian bounded by the identity\nmakes the normalized error a weighted average of the displayed scalar\nquantities. A scalar quadratic attains the maximum. Zero eigenvalues have\nzero objective contribution; affine translations to a minimizer change\nneither calculation. Thus this is a full quadratic-class computation.\n\nFor an exact finite characterization of (4), subdivide [0,1] at all its\ndistinct interior roots 1/d_j and, when b>0, 1/b. On each root-free open\ninterval the logarithmic derivative of the nonzero objective is\n\n    L(t)=1/t-2b/(1-bt)-2sum_j N_j d_j/(1-d_j t),\n    L'(t)=-1/t^2-2b^2/(1-bt)^2\n                    -2sum_j N_j d_j^2/(1-d_j t)^2<0.   (5)\n\nConsequently there is exactly one interior maximizer between consecutive\nroots, also between zero and the first root. On the last interval the\nmaximum is either its unique root of L or t=1. Values at roots are zero.\nCoincident roots are combined, and b=0 simply removes its term. Equations\n(3)--(5) specify the complete spectrum without a numerical sweep.\n\nThe unit-curvature values alone are\n\n    P_m(1)=-(ac)^(2^m-1)c^(-m),\n    R_(m,b)(1)=(1-b)(ac)^(2^m-2)c^(1-m).                (6)\n\nThey are not used as a replacement for (4).\n\n## 2. Exact single-cut screen for every appended b\n\nFor a cut with step d, preceding mass P and following mass T, the accepted\ncut value is (d-1)_+^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2T)]. For a fixed d and total schedule\nmass S, its reciprocal denominator is\n\n    D(P)=(1+P)^2[1+2(S-d-P)].\n\nIts derivative has the sign of 2(S-d)-3P. Therefore its minimum on any\nclosed interval is attained at an endpoint. Among all occurrences of a\ngiven level it suffices to check the first and last occurrence.\n\nFor h^(m,b), all levels 1<=j<m remain present. Their first and last\npreceding masses are, respectively,\n\n    P_j^first=S_j, P_j^last=S_m-d_j-S_j.                (7)\n\nTheir following masses are S_m-d_0+b-d_j-P. At level zero, after deleting\nthe original last occurrence, the endpoint preceding masses are\n\n    P_0^first=0, P_0^last=S_m-2d_0-d_1,                 (8)\n\nwith following masses S_m-2d_0+b and d_1+b. For m=2 the two occurrences\nin (8) refer to the same remaining index. The appended b contributes\n\n    (b-1)_+^2/(1+S_m-d_0)^2.                           (9)\n\nTaking the maximum of the cuts in (7)--(9) and\n1/[1+2(S_m-d_0+b)] is EXACTLY K_n(h^(m,b)). No omitted internal cut can\nbe larger, by the denominator argument. In particular the accepted final\ncut already proves W_n(h^(m,b))>=1/B_n whenever\n\n    b>=1+(1+S_m-d_0)/sqrt(B_n).                        (10)\n\nThe global Huber term proves the same comparison whenever\nb<=(B_n-1)/2-(S_m-d_0), if that right side is nonnegative. These tests use\nthe actual B_n, not a guessed dyadic constant.\n\n## 3. An exact all-cut result at the original final step\n\nFor the remainder take m>=128 unless indicated, and first set b=d_0, so\nthe actual schedule is H_m. The following rational bounds will be used:\n\n    12/5<lambda<c<49/20<5/2,\n    3/8<a<lambda-2<1/2,\n    ac>=127/128>7/8,\n    0<a<c-2=r<=9/20.                                   (11)\n\nFor example lambda<17/7 gives\nc<2193/896<49/20 for m>=128; a=(1-1/m)/c then exceeds3/8.\nThe upper bound a<lambda-2 follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda].\nConsequently 0<A_mass<1, and (1) gives\n\n    M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j,\n    a c^j<=r M_j.                                      (12)\n\nLet M=M_(m-1). For a first occurrence of any level j, P=S_j and its\nfollowing mass is at least S_(m-1). Its cut is at most\nr^2/(2M-1). For a last occurrence, its preceding mass is at least\nS_(m-1), and its following mass is S_j. Since x^2/(2x-1) is increasing\nfor x>=1 and M_j<=M, its cut is also at most\n\n    r^2 M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)]<=r^2/(2M-1).              (13)\n\nThese statements include the unique central occurrence, for which the\nfirst and last positions coincide. Using c<=5/2, r<=1/2 and M>=1,\n\n    r^2(2M_m-1)<= (5M-1)/4 <= 2M-1.\n\nThus every single-cut term is no larger than the global Huber term:\n\n    K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                               (14)\n\n## 4. Full-spectrum quadratic bound at b=d_0\n\nThis argument controls every t in [0,1], not only t=1. Write\nv_j=a c^j t and, for the partial ruler H_j,\n\n    Y_j=c v_j |P_j(t)|.\n\nIf no level 0,...,m-1 satisfies d_j t>=1, all factors in (2) are\nnonnegative, and 1-u<=exp(-u) gives\n\n    t P_m(t)^2<=t exp(-2S_m t)<=1/(2e S_m).             (15)\n\nThe last maximum is over all nonnegative t, so it also bounds the actual\nrestricted interval. Suppose instead that j is the first level with\nd_j t>=1. If j=0, Y_0=ac t<=1. If j>=1, all factors in P_j are\nnonnegative and t<=1/(1+a)<=8/11. Equations (1), (11) give\n\n    t S_j>=v_j/r-t,\n    Y_j<=c v_j exp(-v_j/r+t)<=cr exp(t-1)<1.            (16)\n\nHere the maximum of v exp(-v/r) is r/e, cr<5/4, and\nexp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4. For every subsequent level l>=j the\nfactor is on its nonpositive side, and the exact recursion is\n\n    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]Y_l^2<=Y_l^2<=1.             (17)\n\nThe bracket lies in [0,1]. If an earlier factor is zero the conclusion\nholds directly as well. Therefore\n\n    t P_m(t)^2<=1/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)t]\n                      <=d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)),      (18)\n\nusing t>=1/d_(m-1). Equations (15),(18) prove the explicit full-spectrum\nestimate\n\n    Q_m(d_0)<=max{1/(2e S_m),\n                 (1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))}.      (19)\n\nBoth entries are strictly below (14). For the first, S_m>=1 gives\n(2S_m+1)/(2e S_m)<=3/(2e)<3/4. For the second, using (12) and ac>7/8,\n\n    (2S_m+1)(1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))\n       <=2/[(ac)^2 c^m]+2/[c^2 ac]\n       <=16/49+25/63=2233/3087<3/4.                   (20)\n\nFor the first term of this last bound it already suffices that m>=3\nand c>2. Thus, with considerable slack,\n\n    Q_m(d_0)<(3/4)K_n(H_m).                            (21)\n\n## 5. Exact benchmark comparison, with no fitted constant\n\nLet rho=log_2(lambda). The accepted benchmark defines the primitive mass\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1 with join\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n\nand B_n with join G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. We prove directly\nfrom these recursions that\n\n    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                     (22)\n\nPut F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, t>0. Direct differentiation, with t=exp(z), gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp z)\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe strict inequality follows by integrating the positive derivative of\n(1+t)^rho-1-rho t for t>0. Convexity at z and -z gives\nF(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. If A=t^rho B and\nD=(1+t)^rho B, this says (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, or\nD^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. Hence\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.\n\nZero arguments follow by continuity. Induction in the primitive\nrecursion proves the first bound in (22). Since G(A,D)<=4A+D,\ninduction in the dominant recursion, with a split k and l=n-k-1,\ngives B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho. The last inequality follows\nby integrating the increasing derivative of x^rho, including l=0.\n\nIn our family (1) implies\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n       =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m] -> e,        (23)\n\nsince A_mass->1 and 2/c is bounded below1. Thus the EXACT finite bound\n\n    K_n(H_m) B_n\n      <=[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2M_m-1)                     (24)\n\nhas limit 2/e<1. This uses an upper bound derived for the actual B_n\nat every integer horizon; it assumes no asymptotic phase or decimal\nvalue for B_n/lambda^m. In particular, the full quadratic and all-cut\nbaseline is strictly below U_n^ZJ for every sufficiently large m.\n\n## 6. Retained two-block geometry and all constant tails in its cap range\n\nHere is the precise mechanism tested next. For ANY two selected cuts i<j\nof an arbitrary schedule, let P be the mass before i, S the mass strictly\nbetween i and j, T the mass after j, and define\n\n    M_0=1+P, M_1=1+S,\n    A=(h_i-1)/M_0, epsilon=(h_j-1)/M_1, sigma=2T+1.\n\nAssume h_i,h_j>1 and choose the cap-range angle\n\n    0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon).                            (25)\n\nUse the retained prefix gradients and proximal point\n\n    p=(1,0)/M_0,\n    q=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)))/M_1,\n    X=(-A,0), y=X-M_1 q, d=-M_1 q.                    (26)\n\nThese prefix data are actual jets of the single Moreau envelope of\nJ(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>), starting at (1,0). Before the first checkpoint\nthe proximal points are (1-(u+1)/M_0,0), 0<=u<=P, so the p-plane is\nactive. That checkpoint, of size1+A M_0, sends its input p to X. In\nthe second block, the proposed proximal points X-tq, 1<=t<=M_1, have\n\n    <q,X-tq>=(A kappa/M_1)(1-t/M_1)>=0,\n    <q-p,X-tq>\n      =A kappa/M_1+A/M_0\n          -t[A kappa/M_1^2+kappa/(M_0 M_1)]\n      >=(A-kappa)/M_0>0.\n\nThis verifies every actual old query, even if individual ordinary steps\nare large. At the second checkpoint its jet is\n\n    x_j=y+q, g_j=q, f_j=||q||^2/2.\n\nFor completeness, the envelope minimizes J(z)+||x-z||^2/2. This has a\nunique minimizer, and an active-plane slope g at z proves optimality at\nx=z+g. At two proximal points, monotonicity of the subgradients gives\n||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, hence a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The two minimizing\ninequalities identify the derivative as g; joint convexity proves\nconvexity of the envelope. Thus the claimed function has the required\nglobal regularity, not only valid formal data.\n\nThe second checkpoint, of size1+M_1 epsilon, sends the iterate to\nX_tail=y+epsilon d. The optimum jet is (0,0,0). Every interpolant of\nthis prefix has origin as an actual closest minimizer to (1,0): the\nretained first checkpoint jet (p,p,||p||^2/2), interpolated with any\nminimizer z, forces <p,z><=0. Its squared distance from (1,0) is\ntherefore at least1, attained at origin. This normalization persists\nfor all extensions below.\n\nAllow ANY legal continuation with a single common gradient g at every\ntail query, including the terminal sample; allow affine intercepts,\npositive values and extra dimensions, and retain every prefix jet.\nIts endpoint is y+epsilon d-Tg. The accepted interpolation inequality\nfrom this endpoint to the old jet (y+q,q,||q||^2/2) gives\n\n    2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g>-sigma||g||^2\n                         <=epsilon^2||d||^2/sigma\n                         =A kappa epsilon^2/sigma.\n\nThis uses a retained old query, not a reset to a new problem. From (25),\nEVERY such constant-tail continuation obeys the exact universal cap\n\n    2f_end<=U_ij:=A^2 epsilon^2/[(1+epsilon)(1+2T)].     (27)\n\nIn particular it covers all angles and all optimized tail planes of the\nprevious explicit two-checkpoint cap policy. It does not cover angles\nkappa>A/(1+epsilon), different two-block prefixes, or variable-gradient\ntails. No claim that (27) is attained for every pair is needed below.\n\n## 7. The complete cap-range mechanism is dominated by K on H_m\n\nReturn to the full H_m, m>=128. For a selected pair i<j let their levels\nbe u,v; write d=a c^v=h_j-1, x=1+S, and epsilon=d/x. The individual\naccepted cut terms at i and j are K_i,K_j. Direct cancellation gives\n\n    U_ij/K_i=epsilon^2/(1+epsilon)\n                          +2d epsilon/(1+2T),          (28)\n    U_ij/K_j=(h_i-1)^2(1+P+h_i+S)^2\n                          /[(1+P)^2 x(x+d)].           (29)\n\nEvery occurrence of level v has a full H_v immediately after it, so\nT>=S_v. If v>=2 then M_v>=M_2=4+2a+ac>5, and by (11),(12),\n\n    2d/(1+2T)<=2r M_v/(2M_v-1)<=10r/9<=1/2.\n\nConsequently, for epsilon<=1, (28) is at most1.\n\nIf epsilon>1, then x<d. The first selected cut must lie INSIDE the\ncopy of H_v immediately preceding j and its level satisfies u<v.\nOtherwise that copy lies between the cuts, giving S>=S_v and\nx>=M_v>d. Inside this copy put P_loc for its mass before i. The\nsubcopies of H_u immediately before and after i yield\n\n    P>=P_loc, x>=M_u,\n    1+P_loc=M_v-(h_i-1)-x.\n\nAlso h_i-1<=d/c<=r M_v/c and x<d<=r M_v. For fixed x, the factor\n(1+P+h_i+S)/(1+P) decreases with P, so substituting P_loc in (29)\ngives\n\n    U_ij/K_j\n      <=(h_i-1)^2 M_v^2/\n          [(M_v-(h_i-1)-x)^2 x(x+d)]\n      <r^2/[2(1-r-r/c)^2]\n      <=648/841<1.                                    (30)\n\nThe penultimate step uses h_i-1<=r M_u<=r x and x/(x+d)<1/2.\nFor the last, r<=9/20 and c>=12/5 imply 1-r-r/c>=29/80.\n\nIt remains to include the two small levels. If v=0, then d=a<1/2 and\nepsilon<=d. Equation (28) is at most d^2/(1+d)+2d^2<=2/3. If v=1,\nthen d=ac<1. Unless i is the one level-zero step immediately before j,\nthe intervening mass includes H_1, so epsilon<=1/2 and T>=S_1>=1;\n(28) is then at most1/6+1/3=1/2. For that one adjacent pair x=1 and\nh_i-1=a. Equation (29) is bounded by\n\n    a^2(2+a)^2/(1+ac)<=1/(1+ac)<1,\n\nbecause a<=sqrt(2)-1 implies a(2+a)<=1. This covers EVERY ordered pair,\nincluding pairs near the end and repeated occurrences of either level.\n\nCombining (14),(27)--(30), every member of the complete constant-tail\nmechanism in Section6 has normalized error at most K_n(H_m). Therefore\n\n    max{Q_m(d_0), K_n(H_m),\n        supremum of the Section6 constant-tail scores}\n      =1/(2S_m+1),\n    limsup B_n times this maximum <=2/e<1.             (31)\n\nThis is a quantitative failure of a concrete retained-history mechanism\non the ASSIGNED constant-level family. Its tail bound allows all\ndimensions, intercepts and positive values once its prefix and angle\nrange are fixed. Thus retuning only its two cuts, its angle inside (25),\nor its final constant-gradient plane cannot produce the target comparison\nfor all appended b: b=d_0 already defeats that possibility.\n\nThe unrestricted W_n may be larger. In particular, (31) gives no upper\ncertificate for it, no primitive-validity conclusion, and no decision of\nthe original all-schedule/all-horizon problem. The remaining exact branch\nis to leave (25), change more than two prefix cells, or allow genuinely\nvarying gradients within the remaining ruler block while retaining all\nqueried jets and the closest-minimizer normalization.\n\n## 8. The entire accepted alternating-ray family also collapses to K\n\nWe now invoke the exact ray recurrence from accepted fact\n`1fca3e5dd555cb4a`. No unverified ray exclusion is used. For any two\nindices i<j in H_m, write P,S for their preceding and intervening masses,\nand let u,v be their levels. Put r_u=a c^u and r_v=a c^v. We claim\n\n    r_u r_v<(1+P)(1+S).                               (32)\n\nIf u>=v, or if i lies before the full H_v immediately preceding j,\nthe local copies give 1+P>=M_u and 1+S>=M_v. Thus (32) follows from\nr_u<=r M_u, r_v<=r M_v and r<1.\n\nOtherwise u<v and i lies inside that preceding H_v. Define P_loc there.\nThe local H_u copies on both sides of i give\n\n    1+P_loc>=M_u, 1+S>=M_u,\n    (1+P_loc)+(1+S)=M_v-r_u.\n\nExpanding [(1+P_loc)-M_u][(1+S)-M_u]>=0 shows\n\n    (1+P)(1+S)>=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u).\n\nFor fixed u define D_v=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u)-r_u r_v.\nAt v=u+1, the identity M_(u+1)=2M_u+r_u gives\n\n    D_(u+1)=M_u^2-c r_u^2\n               >=(1-cr^2)M_u^2>0,\n\nsince cr^2<=3969/8000<1. At successive levels,\n\n    D_(v+1)-D_v=M_u M_v+[M_u-(c-1)r_u]r_v>0,\n\nbecause (c-1)r<=261/400<1. This proves (32) for every pair, including\nthe small levels with empty local blocks.\n\nSuppose a ray construction selected at least two checkpoints i,j. Its\nfirst two gradients and first old offset are\n\n    m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=r_u m_0/(1+S), O_1=P m_0.\n\nBy (32), the second-return budget satisfies\n\n    r_v m_1-O_1\n       =m_0[r_u r_v/(1+S)-P]<m_0.                    (33)\n\nWith exactly two checkpoints, the terminal old-slope constraint requires\nthis quantity to be at least (1+T)m_0>=m_0. With three or more, the next\nnonterminal gradient is this quantity divided by1 plus its block mass,\nand hence is strictly below m_0, violating same-ray slope monotonicity.\nThus no accepted ray construction has two or more checkpoints. The\nzero- and one-checkpoint cases recover K exactly, so\n\n    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                  (34)\n\n## 9. The complete universal complex lens is also below Huber\n\nThis concerns the accepted Lens_n and maximal path-value Gap_h from fact\n`dfde2fa326c908bf`, not the larger schedule-dependent domain D_h. The\naccepted bound is\n\n    Gap_(H_m)(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|P_m(z)|^2,\n    z=alpha+i beta,\n    kappa_lens=alpha-|z|^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2)).        (35)\n\nAll chronological prefix jets and their interpolation cycles remain those\nof the accepted definition. We only bound their actual terminal polynomial.\nWe prove, for m>=128,\n\n    sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(H_m)(z)<1/(2S_m+1).         (36)\n\nThe endpoints z=0 and z=1 follow directly: the former has zero error,\nand at the latter the upper bound in (35) is P_m(1)^2, covered by (21).\nEvery other lens point has 0<alpha<1 and kappa_lens>0. Put\n\n    delta=tan(pi/(n+2))<=1/100,\n    tau_z=|1-z|^2/(2kappa_lens),\n    q_decay=99/100.\n\nThe delta bound follows from tan x<=2x for 0<=x<=1/2 and pi<4,\nsince n=2^m-1. The tangent inequality follows from sin x<=x and\ncos x>=1-x^2/2>=7/8 on that interval. From the lens inequality,\n\n    |beta|<=delta alpha(1-alpha),\n    kappa_lens>=alpha(1-alpha)(1-delta^2/4),\n    tau_z<=F_delta(1-alpha)/(2alpha),\n    F_delta=(1+delta^2)/(1-delta^2/4)<101/100.           (37)\n\nConsider a partial ruler H_j all of whose levels l<j satisfy\na c^l<tau_z, with j>=1. In particular tau_z>a>3/8, and (37) gives\nalpha<101/176<3/5. Each step d_l of H_j satisfies\n\n    d_l alpha<(1+tau_z)alpha\n       <=alpha+F_delta(1-alpha)/2<81/100.              (38)\n\nIts real factor is therefore positive, at least19/100. The inequalities\nlog(1-u)<=-u and log(1+u)<=u imply\n\n    log|1-d_l z|\n      <=-d_l alpha+d_l^2 beta^2/[2(19/100)^2].\n\nSum with the exact multiplicities of the partial ruler. Its sum of\nsquared steps is at most its largest step times S_j, and (38) bounds\nthat largest step by81/(100alpha). Hence\n\n    |P_j(z)|<=exp[-(1-12delta^2)alpha S_j]\n                       <=exp(-q_decay alpha S_j).    (39)\n\nFor the numerical coefficient here,\n(81/100)/[2(19/100)^2]=4050/361<12, and\n1-12/10000>99/100. This is a symbolic bound; no spectral sampling occurs.\n\nIf no level l<m reaches a c^l>=tau_z, (39) applies to H_m. From (35),\n\n    Gap_(H_m)(z)<=2alpha exp(-2q_decay S_m alpha)\n                        <=1/(q_decay e S_m).\n\nSince S_m>=2 and e>8/3, its ratio to Huber is strictly less than\n\n    (5/2)/(q_decay(8/3))=125/132<1.                   (40)\n\nOtherwise let j<m be the first level with a c^j>=tau_z. Put\nY_j=c a c^j |z| |P_j(z)|. If j=0, Y_0=ac|z|<=ac<1.\nIf j>=1, (39) and S_j>=a c^j/r-1 give, by maximizing\nv exp(-q_decay v/r) over v>=0,\n\n    Y_j<= [cr sqrt(1+delta^2)/q_decay]\n                              exp(q_decay alpha-1)\n       <=(9/8)exp(-2/5)<1.                           (41)\n\nIndeed cr<=441/400, sqrt(1+delta^2)<=10001/10000,\nand dividing their product by99/100 gives a number below9/8.\nAlso alpha<3/5 and exp(2/5)>1+2/5>9/8.\n\nFor every level l>=j, the definition of tau_z gives exactly\n\n    |1-d_l z|^2\n      =a^2 c^(2l)|z|^2+|1-z|^2-2a c^l kappa_lens\n      <=a^2 c^(2l)|z|^2.\n\nConsequently the ruler recursion implies Y_(l+1)<=Y_l^2, so Y_m<=1.\nIf alpha>1/16, (35) and |z|>=alpha imply that the ratio of its bound\nto Huber is at most\n\n    (2S_m+1) 32/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)]\n      <=64/[(ac)^2 c^m]<128/2^m<1.                   (42)\n\nIf alpha<=1/16, then tau_z>=(1-alpha)/(2alpha)>=15/2>a,\nso j>=1. The same estimate before (41) now gives\n\n    Y_j<=(9/8)exp(1/16)/e\n                  <(9/8)(16/15)(3/8)=9/20<1/2.\n\nHere exp(t)<=1/(1-t) for 0<=t<1 follows termwise from its series,\nand e>8/3 follows by retaining its terms through degree four.\nThere is at least one update after index j, hence Y_m<1/4. The fact\nthat tau_z<=a c^(m-1), together with\n|1-z|^2>=1-2alpha and kappa_lens<=alpha, yields\n\n    alpha>=1/[2(1+a c^(m-1))]=1/(2d_(m-1)).\n\nEquation (35) is therefore at most\n\n    1/[8c^2 a^2 c^(2m)alpha]\n       <=d_(m-1)/[4c^2 a^2 c^(2m)].                  (43)\n\nBy (20), its ratio to Huber is less than3/16. Equations (40)--(43)\ncover every lens point and prove (36). Consequently\n\n    V_lens(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=F_ray_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).      (44)\n\nCombining (21), (31), (34), (44) gives the same exact 2/e limit-superior\nobstruction even after including ALL accepted single-cut, alternating-ray,\nand universal-lens baselines, as well as the complete quadratic class and\nthe retained two-block constant-tail mechanism from Section6. The full\nschedule-dependent complex domain, general multidimensional nonlinear\norbits, and variable-gradient tails remain outside this statement.\n\n## 10. Remove the angle restriction: every constant tail of this two-block prefix\n\nThe cap-angle condition (25) is not needed for the following stronger\nobstruction. Allow EVERY 0<=kappa<=A in the two-block prefix (26), and\nretain all its actual jets and the optimum jet. The prefix legality\ncalculation in Section6 still holds: the only strict comparison there\nbecomes nonnegative at kappa=A. At kappa=0 the second block is already\nat a minimum. The argument proving origin is the closest minimizer is\nunchanged. For ANY legal constant-gradient tail, (27)'s derivation before\nusing the cap range gives\n\n    2f_end<=A kappa epsilon^2/(1+2T)\n                  <=A^2 epsilon^2/(1+2T).             (45)\n\nNo claim that this outgoing upper bound is attainable is made. We show\nits right side is itself smaller than the old Huber value on H_m.\n\nFirst strengthen (32). Define q_pair=c r^2<1/2, r=c-2 as before.\nFor ANY selected i<j, with their levels u,v and preceding/intervening\nmasses P,S, one has\n\n    R_pair:=(h_i-1)(h_j-1)/[(1+P)(1+S)]<=q_pair.       (46)\n\nIn the outside-copy case of Section8 the bound r^2 already suffices.\nIn the inside-copy case its denominator is at least\nM_u(M_v-r_u-M_u). Write theta=r_u/M_u<=r and z=M_v/M_u.\nHere z>=M_(u+1)/M_u=2+theta and r_v<=r M_v. Consequently\n\n    R_pair<=theta r z/(z-1-theta)\n              <=theta r(2+theta)<=r^2(2+r)=q_pair.    (47)\n\nThe middle inequality follows because z/(z-1-theta) decreases for\nz>1+theta. This includes u=0. It proves (46) with an explicit uniform\nmargin, not just the strict inequality used for the ray exclusion.\n\nLet p=1+P, x=1+S, t=1+T and let r_i=h_i-1, r_j=h_j-1.\nHere p,x,t are positive scalars, not the vector p from Section6. The\npalindrome H_m gives the same (46) in the reverse chronological order:\n\n    r_i r_j/(t x)<=q_pair.\n\nEvery cut has its own H_level on both sides. Thus r_i<=r p and\nr_j<=r t. Combining these three bounds yields\n\n    R_pair<=q_pair,\n    p<=q_pair t/R_pair,\n    x<=r^2 t/R_pair.                                  (48)\n\nAll quantities divided by are positive. The exact total mass is\n\n    M_m=p+x+t+r_i+r_j\n           <=(1+r)(p+t)+x.\n\nSubstituting (48), and then R_pair<=q_pair, gives\n\n    R_pair M_m/t\n       <=(1+r)R_pair+(1+r)q_pair+r^2\n       <=D_pair:=2(1+r)q_pair+r^2.\n\nTherefore\n\n    R_pair<=min(q_pair,D_pair t/M_m).                 (49)\n\nThis retains both the true preceding mass and the true remaining tail;\nneither is absorbed into a new initialization distance.\n\nWrite M=M_m, q=q_pair and D=D_pair temporarily. Since M>=8 and\nD/q=2(1+r)+1/c<=199/60<4, the crossover t_0=qM/D exceeds1.\nThe function t^2/(2t-1) is increasing for t>=1 and 1/(2t-1) is\ndecreasing. Maximizing the bound from (49) on the two sides of t_0\ntherefore gives the exact elementary envelope\n\n    R_pair^2/(2t-1)\n       <=q^2/(2qM/D-1)=qD/(2M-D/q).                  (50)\n\nFor the permitted parameters,\n\n    qD<=661/800,\n    D/q<=199/60.\n\nIndeed q<1/2, r<=9/20 give\nD<=29/20+81/400=661/400, and the displayed formula for D/q gives\nits bound using c>=12/5. Consequently, since M>=8,\n\n    [R_pair^2/(2t-1)] / [1/(2M-1)]\n       <=(661/800)(2M-1)/(2M-199/60)\n       <=5949/6088<1.                                (51)\n\nThe ratio in the middle decreases with M because199/60>1; its value\nat M=8 is5949/6088. Equations (45),(51) prove that EVERY legal\nconstant-gradient extension of ANY angle in (26) satisfies\n\n    2f_end<=(5949/6088)/(2S_m+1).                     (52)\n\nThis covers all choices of the two checkpoints, all ordinary block\ncontents in their actual order, both endpoint angles, affine offsets,\npositive values, additional dimensions, and arbitrary interpolation\nchoices for the tail, provided that its gradient is constant at all\nactual tail queries and the terminal point. If no such tail exists the\nstatement is vacuous. No tail feasibility or direction optimization is\nsilently assumed. At kappa=0 its error is zero as noted above.\n\nIn particular, removing only the cap-angle restriction is NOT an escape\nfrom the quantitative obstruction. Sections1--10 give the single joint\nconclusion, for m>=128 and the original appended b=d_0:\n\n    max{all quadratic scores, F_ray_n(H_m), V_lens(H_m),\n        all Section10 two-block constant-tail scores}\n       =K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1),\n    limsup of B_n times this maximum <=2/e<1.          (53)\n\nThe original all-schedule target remains unresolved. The remaining\ngeometric change must alter the retained two-block prefix itself or use\nvarying gradients inside the remaining ruler tail. The full\nschedule-dependent complex domain and the other unrestricted mechanisms\nidentified in Section9 are also still open.\n\n## 11. A quantitative requirement for a genuinely varying tail\n\nKeep ANY angle 0<=kappa<=A in the exact retained prefix (26), but now\npermit a varying tail. Denote its physical steps by t_l>=0, its gradients\nat those queries by g_l, its terminal gradient by g_end, and put\n\n    T=sum_l t_l, sigma=2T+1,\n    Delta_tail=sum_l t_l(g_end-g_l).\n\nThis notation changes neither the prescribed steps nor the retained jets.\nIts terminal point is y+epsilon d-sum_l t_l g_l. The outgoing\ninterpolation inequality to the retained jet (y+q,q,||q||^2/2) is\n\n    2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g_end>-sigma||g_end||^2\n                                  +2<g_end,Delta_tail>.\n\nCompleting the square gives the more useful exact bound\n\n    2f_end<=A kappa epsilon^2/sigma\n       +2<g_end,Delta_tail>\n       -sigma||g_end-epsilon d/sigma||^2.             (54)\n\nFor the H_m geometry, the first term is bounded by gamma K, where\ngamma=5949/6088 and K=1/(2S_m+1), by the complete calculation (45)--(51).\nTherefore any such witness reaching 2f_end>=1/B_n must satisfy\n\n    2<g_end,Delta_tail>\n       >=1/B_n-gamma K\n                   +sigma||g_end-epsilon d/sigma||^2. (55)\n\nThe true closest-minimizer normalization is still1 by the first retained\ncheckpoint jet. In particular, (24) implies the quantitative necessary\ncondition\n\n    liminf B_n * 2<g_end,Delta_tail> >=1-2gamma/e>0     (56)\n\nfor any unbounded sequence of successful witnesses of this form.\nThis requirement keeps every parameter loss. Merely introducing new\ngradient directions is insufficient: if\n<g_end,g_l>>=||g_end||^2 at every positive tail step, the left side\nof (55) is nonpositive. It includes all collinear contracting tails\ng_l=c_l g_end with c_l>=1, and all changes g_l=g_end+w_l with\n<g_end,w_l>=0. Neither class can escape the obstruction.\n\nWhen T>0 and g_end!=0, Cauchy--Schwarz additionally gives\n\n    sum_l t_l||g_l-g_end||^2\n      >= [ (1/B_n-gamma K)_+ ]^2/[4T||g_end||^2]        (57)\n\nfor any successful witness. Equation (55) is stronger because it\nretains the sign of the required terminal correlation and the square\nloss. No sufficient construction with the required positive correlation\nhas been established. This is a necessary quantitative interface, not a\nnew lower bound on W_n and not an assertion of feasibility.\n\n## 12. A different positive-height prefix, and its first failed shortcut\n\nThe preceding obstruction fixes the zero height at the second checkpoint.\nOne can change that geometry while retaining a legal function and exact\nnormalization. Fix M_0,M_1>=1, A>0, epsilon>0, choose\n\n    0<=kappa<=A, 0<=nu^2<=kappa(A-kappa),\n    v= kappa^2+nu^2,\n    p=(1,0)/M_0, q=(-kappa,nu)/M_1,\n    X=(-A,0), d=-M_1q, y=X+d,\n    delta_second=(A kappa-v)/M_1>=0.                  (58)\n\nUse the same actual ordinary blocks and checkpoints of sizes1+A M_0\nand1+M_1 epsilon. The one fixed envelope of max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>)\nrealizes the first two blocks: the first p-plane comparison is unchanged;\nin the second block, for 1<=t<=M_1,\n\n    <q,X-tq>=A kappa/M_1-tv/M_1^2>=delta_second>=0,\n    <p,X-tq>=(-A+t kappa/M_1)/M_0<=0.\n\nEvery query is thus legal. The second checkpoint jet has proximal point\ny and value f_j=delta_second+||q||^2/2. The origin is the true closest\nminimizer by the same saturated first checkpoint jet, without assuming\nanything about the subsequent interpolant.\n\nFor ANY legal constant-gradient tail of total mass T, write sigma=2T+1.\nThe retained second-checkpoint inequality and the optimum inequality,\ncompleted separately as squares, give\n\n    2f_end<=min {\n      2delta_second+epsilon^2 v/sigma,\n      [A^2-2A(1+epsilon)kappa+(1+epsilon)^2v]/sigma\n    }.                                                (59)\n\nThese are simultaneous necessary conditions, not an attained formula.\nAll incoming interpolation inequalities to the old positive-height\nqueries must still be imposed to produce a witness. They are not removed\nby the accepted unqueried-terminal reduction: these are constraints on\nthe queried tail itself.\n\nThe tempting shortcut of treating the first term of (59) as an attainable\nretained-height payoff is false. Take epsilon=1, kappa=A/2 and nu=0.\nThen delta_second=A^2/(4M_1)>0, but\n\n    X_tail=y+epsilon d=(-A,0)+2(A/2,0)=0.\n\nThis is the retained minimizer, so every subsequent gradient and every\nterminal error are zero, for EVERY nonnegative tail. The first term\nof (59) is A^2/(2M_1)+A^2/(4sigma)>0, whereas the optimum term is zero.\nThis is an explicit legal fixed-function counterexample to that shortcut,\nnot merely inconsistent formal data. It also shows why raising the\ncheckpoint height cannot be evaluated independently of its location.\n\nThe screened next tasks are consequently precise: either construct an\nactual varying tail satisfying (55) with every old-query inequality, or\nsolve the joint positive-height prefix/tail constraints behind (58)--(59)\nand obtain a value at least1/B_n for all appended b. No such payoff\ncomparison is claimed here. arbitrary growing dimension.", "new_mechanism": "Lag-two retained interpolation forces a uniform macroscopic alignment loss; this dominates c(c-2)>1 after true normalization.", "next": "Allow positive interior relay height, non-Markov correlated innovations, a different checkpoint partition, or within-gap varying gradients."}, "glossary": {"theta_l": "r_l/M_l for the actual fixed-parameter ruler, where r_l=a c^l and M_l=1+sum H_l.", "beta_l": "Variable Markov transfer parameter in[0,theta_l].", "alpha_l": "Norm of the l-th block gradient, with alpha_u=1/M_u and alpha_(l+1)^2=theta_l beta_l alpha_l².", "zeta_l": "Unit Markov normal satisfying zeta_(l+1)=-sqrt(beta_l/theta_l)zeta_l+sqrt(1-beta_l/theta_l)e_(l+1).", "omega_l": "Actual proposed constant gradient alpha_l zeta_l on the block ending at the first occurrence of level l.", "Z_l": "Proximal point at the retained zero-height ending query of block l.", "D_l": "1+(M_(l+1)-1)theta_l theta_(l+1), the coefficient in the exact old-query necessary inequality.", "k_relay": "v-u, the number of Markov transfers, written k.", "eta_relay": "972405/1120000, the geometric upper factor, written eta.", "Gamma_0": "3159/4000, the uniform one-checkpoint ratio upper bound.", "Gamma_1": "614365479/640000000, the uniform multiple-checkpoint ratio upper bound."}}
{"id": "3b5baff6751d091e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:56:31.595813+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C36 erratum to1d9af6b7bf48b5e3: delete the spurious intermediate expression M[(2+q)^2+(c-2)q]. The correct exact four-block mass is M_m=M[4+q(c+2)]<=M c^2. The all-normal merge deletion and quantitative bound are unchanged.", "evidence": "Count the actual four ordinary blocks of mass M-1 and three checkpoints of masses 1+Mq,1+Mcq,1+Mq. Then 1+sum h=1+4(M-1)+3+Mq(2+c)=M[4+q(c+2)]. Since q<=c-2, 4+q(c+2)<=4+(c-2)(c+2)=c^2.
