Two arbitrary queried cells; three queried cells after the specified saturated first block; explicit ascending Markov relay.", "completion_identity": "Exact all-anchor mixture; constant-cell interiors compressed only because their anchors are exact convex combinations of the two queried endpoints.", "finite_loss": "The general two-queried-cell upper bound has multiplicative loss1/(1-d0²K)=1+O(K); no unsupported claim of sharp finite equality.", "next": "Genuine multiple queried-gradient changes with non-Markov correlations or positive interior relay heights."}, "glossary": {"F_max": "The actual accepted maximal-prefix interpolant F(x)=sup_v[x.v-.5||v||²-max_i(y_i.v-delta_i)].", "K_free": "K/(1-d0² K), an explicit rigorous upper bound for all two-queried-cell free-terminal completions on H_m, not claimed sharp.", "sigma_b": "1+2T-b² in the free-completion upper bound, with actual final physical step b in[0,2].", "b_star": "The actual ruler final step d0=1+a, in(1,3/2).", "K_i_free": "(h_i-1)²/[(1+P_i)²(1+2T_i-b_star²)], with actual prefix and suffix masses.", "R0_tail": "Actual tail-start distance from a retained proximal point proved to be a minimizer, written R_0.", "alpha_mix": "All-anchor simplex weights in the exact formula F_max(x)=min_alpha[sum alpha_i delta_i+.5||x-sum alpha_i y_i||²]."}}
{"id": "f6f1159119ef54eb", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:02:04.007126+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C37 normalization sharpened: ANY convex truncation D subset of cone C containing0 has exactly the SAME true initial distance whenever P_D(x0)=P_C(x0). Thus every truncation leaving all trajectory projections unchanged is quadratic-bounded, without requiring all cone extreme rays to be retained.", "evidence": "For F_D(x)=max_(p in D)(<x,p>-||p||²/2), grad F_D=P_D is1-Lipschitz and its minimizer set is D^polar. Since D subset C, C^polar subset D^polar; hence dist(x0,D^polar)<=dist(x0,C^polar)=||P_C(x0)||. Conversely P_D(z)=0 at every z in D^polar, so the Lipschitz bound implies dist(x0,D^polar)>=||P_D(x0)||. Under the stated initial equality the two bounds coincide. If every later cone projection also belongs to D, all projections, function values and GD positions agree, so the product bound proved in ad323c2b7c7c75aa applies with the exact closest-minimizer denominator. This full argument is now in the local C37 artifact.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["ad323c2b7c7c75aa"], "artifact": "research/c37_squared_distance_cone_obstruction.md"}, "glossary": {"D": "Convex polytope containing0 and contained in closed convex cone C.", "F_D": "Projection objective max_(p in D)(<x,p>-||p||²/2)."}}
{"id": "62bd541a94134051", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:15:12.559399+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Whole positive-INTERIOR-height Markov mechanism obstruction on the actual near-critical ruler, with FREE terminal completion: every legal consecutive first-occurrence relay in the stated homogeneous-plane family, arbitrary margins w_l in[0,r_l], nonstationary fresh-direction correlations, and any common QUERIED final-tail gradient satisfies 2f_end <=(31360/38557)K <(5/6)K. The complete actual-location identity ||X||²=A_u² alpha_u²+sum(A_i²-tau_i²)alpha_i² and one retained old-query constraint give the uniform exact-benchmark limsup <=2/[e(1+sqrt2)]<1. All initial/interior heights, all old pairs, original closest distance1, and last displacement are retained. This is NOT an unrestricted W upper bound.", "evidence": "# Positive interior heights do not rescue the consecutive Markov ruler relay\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No solver or fact_submit allocation.\nThis is a whole-mechanism obstruction with an explicit data domain, including\nall positive interior margins, actual old queries, actual displacement, and\nfree unqueried terminal gradient. It is NOT an upper bound on unrestricted\nW_n. Mathematical premises are accepted interpolation fact\n`1252b8823bb5a290` and the exact benchmark definitions in\n`b98bb807d514e80d`. All additional calculations are proved in this document.\n\n## 1. Schedule, complete family, and claimed comparison\n\nFor an integer m>=128, fix throughout the horizon\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a c^l,H_l), n=2^m-1,\n    M_l=1+sum H_l, r_l=a c^l, r=c-2,\n    K=1/(2M_m-1).\n\nSelect the FIRST occurrences of consecutive checkpoint levels u,...,v,\nwhere 0<=u<=v<=m-1. Write k=v-u. The initial ordinary block is H_u;\nbetween the first occurrences of levels l and l+1 the ordinary block is\nH_l. Thus its capacities (one plus its actual mass) are\n\n    C_u=M_u, C_l=M_(l-1) for u<l<=v.\n\nChoose ANY endpoint margins w_l in [0,r_l]. Define\n\n    tau_l=C_l+w_l, A_l=r_l-w_l.\n\nFor each u<=l<v with positive A_l choose\n\n    0<beta_l<=A_l/tau_(l+1), theta_l=A_l/tau_(l+1).\n\nLet e_u,...,e_v be orthonormal. The ACTUAL Euclidean vectors are\n\n    g_u=e_u/tau_u, alpha_u=1/tau_u,\n    g_(l+1)=-beta_l g_l+z_(l+1),\n    z_(l+1) orthogonal to all earlier vectors,\n    ||z_(l+1)||^2=alpha_l^2 beta_l(theta_l-beta_l),\n    alpha_(l+1)^2=theta_l beta_l alpha_l^2.\n\nZero degeneracies are interpreted by the same recurrence without division\nby a zero norm. A zero prescribed gradient has prescribed value zero, so\nin a legal realization its actual point is a minimizer and every later\niterate remains there. Such cases have zero final error and require no\nseparate positive-gradient estimates below.\n\nStart at X_u=e_u. Let X_l be the actual start of ordinary block l and\n\n    Y_l=X_l-tau_l g_l,\n    X_(l+1)=Y_l-A_l g_l,\n    Y_(l+1)=Y_l-A_l g_l-tau_(l+1)g_(l+1).       (1)\n\nThese are precisely the actual GD displacements: an ordinary block of\nmass C_l-1 followed by checkpoint 1+r_l has mass C_l+r_l, and\ntau_l+A_l=C_l+r_l.\n\nAt EVERY actual query in block l, including its checkpoint query, let t\nbe the ordinary cumulative mass already executed. Prescribe\n\n    x=Y_l+(tau_l-t)g_l,\n    grad f(x)=g_l,\n    f(x)=(tau_l-t-1/2)alpha_l^2,  0<=t<=C_l-1. (2)\n\nThe endpoint proximal height is w_l alpha_l^2. No height is discarded or\nassigned independently of a position and a value. Include the optimum\nsample f(0)=0, grad f(0)=0. Consider EVERY single convex differentiable\n1-smooth function realizing these data, in arbitrary finite dimension.\nIts values or gradients away from these samples are not fixed.\n\nExecute checkpoint v and then the whole remaining actual ruler tail,\nof total mass T. Its start is\n\n    X=Y_v-A_v g_v.                                  (3)\n\nRequire one arbitrary common gradient q at the QUERIED samples of this\nremaining tail. The terminal gradient is free. Allow all terminal-cell\nvalues, offsets, and extra dimensions. Equivalently complete this entire\nqueried prefix by the maximal interpolant of accepted 1252b8823bb5a290,\nwith every old anchor retained; other legal completions are also covered.\n\nEvery legal member of this entire family has true initial distance one\nand satisfies\n\n    2 f(x_n) <= Gamma K, Gamma=31360/38557<5/6.       (4)\n\nThe uniform asymptotic comparison to the ACTUAL exact benchmark is\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) 2 f(x_n)\n                <= 2/[e(1+sqrt(2))] < 1.            (5)\n\nBoth bounds are uniform over u,v, every margin and correlation, every\nlegal interpolant, and every allowed terminal cell. Thus repeated\npositive interior heights in this explicit Markov family do not supply\na benchmark-level witness.\n\n## 2. All-query legality, with no omitted remote plane\n\nThe accepted interpolation inequality in proximal notation is\n\n    delta_j >= delta_i + <g_i,y_j-y_i>,\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.             (6)\n\nFor the data (2) put d=tau_l-t-1. Then\n\n    y=Y_l+d g_l, delta=d alpha_l^2,\n    w_l<=d<=tau_l-1.\n\nFor i<j define\n\n    P_ij=product_(l=i)^(j-1) beta_l,\n    Qprod_ij=product_(l=i)^(j-1) theta_l,\n    R_l=A_l-tau_(l+1) beta_l>=0,\n    L_ij=sum_(l=i)^(j-1) (-1)^(l-i) R_l P_il,\n\nwith empty product one. Directly from (1) and the vector recurrence,\n\n    <g_i,g_j>=(-1)^(j-i) alpha_i^2 P_ij,\n    alpha_j^2=alpha_i^2 P_ij Qprod_ij,\n    <g_i,Y_j>=-alpha_i^2 L_ij for i<j,\n    <g_i,Y_j>=0 for i>=j.                            (7)\n\nTo verify the last identity, each increment\nDeltaY_l=Y_(l+1)-Y_l=-A_l g_l-tau_(l+1)g_(l+1)\nis orthogonal to g_(l+1) because\n<g_l,g_(l+1)>=-beta_l alpha_l^2 and\ntau_(l+1)alpha_(l+1)^2=A_l beta_l alpha_l^2.\nEvery later gradient is a scalar multiple of g_(l+1) plus fresh\northogonal directions, so DeltaY_l is orthogonal to all those gradients.\nSince Y_u=0, summation proves the claimed identity. Taking the earlier\ngradient inner products of the same increments proves the formula for\nL_ij, including its signs.\n\nWe will prove theta_l<=r<1 below; hence beta_l<1 and Qprod_ij<1. By (7),\nevery proposed future supporting plane g_j has value at an old proximal\nquery Y_i+d g_i at most its current value d alpha_i^2. The comparison\nholds at odd lag by its negative sign and at even lag because P_ij<=1.\nFor an old plane i at a later query in block j, its value minus the\ncurrent plane is exactly\n\n    alpha_i^2 [-L_ij+d P_ij((-1)^(j-i)-Qprod_ij)].        (8)\n\nFor even lag the exact strongest condition is\n\n    L_ij >= (tau_j-1)P_ij(1-Qprod_ij),  j-i even.        (9)\n\nFor odd lag no extra condition is needed once (9) holds for all even\nlags: at lag one L=R_i>=0; at larger odd lag,\nL_ij=L_(i,j-1)+P_(i,j-1) R_(j-1)>=0 by (9), and the\nd coefficient in (8) is negative. Thus (9), for all even pairs, is a\nNECESSARY AND SUFFICIENT complete legality test for the prescribed\nrelay data. Queries inside a block require no additional comparisons:\ntheir d coordinates lie between the two actual endpoint queries.\n\nFor sufficiency construct the ONE fixed function\n\n    J(z)=max(0,<g_u,z>,...,<g_v,z>),\n    f_min(x)=min_z [J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                (10)\n\nAll comparisons just proved say that the selected g_l is a subgradient\nof J at the proposed y. Its subgradient inequality plus the quadratic\nterm proves y is the unique minimizer in (10), with value and gradient\ngiven by (2). To recall the elementary smoothness argument, adding two\nproximal subgradient inequalities gives\n||g(x)-g(x')||^2<=<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>. The two minimizing\ninequalities then identify the envelope derivative as g and prove its\n1-Lipschitz property; joint convexity proves convexity. Thus (10) is a\ngenuine realization whenever (9) holds. Necessity of (9) follows from\n(6) for the actual endpoint query in block i and first query in block j.\nThis also shows that no other interpolant can evade (9).\n\nThe bound below is for ALL interpolants of (2), not only (10). In\nparticular it never assumes a virtual Y_l is a minimizer of another\ninterpolant. That unsupported inference would lose the positive-height\nand location distinction that this calculation is intended to retain.\n\nFor any legal interpolant, the first checkpoint sample is\nx=(w_u+1)g_u, f=(w_u+1/2)alpha_u^2. Apply interpolation\nwith ANY minimizer z. Since its minimum is zero, (6) gives\n<g_u,z><=0. Therefore <e_u,z><=0, and\n||e_u-z||^2=1+||z||^2-2<e_u,z>>=1.\nOrigin is a minimizer. The actual closest-minimizer distance is exactly\none for every legal completion, including the maximal one.\n\n## 3. One retained old query still forces a fixed correlation loss\n\nUse (9) at j=l+2. With P=beta_l beta_(l+1) its exact form is\n\n    A_l-beta_l(tau_(l+1)+A_(l+1))\n                 +D_l beta_l beta_(l+1)>=0,\n    D_l=1+(tau_(l+2)-1)theta_l theta_(l+1).           (11)\n\nThis compares an old ACTUAL query against a later ACTUAL query. Positive\nmargins cancel in its middle coefficient:\n\n    tau_(l+1)+A_(l+1)=M_l+r_(l+1).\n\nHere are the schedule estimates needed to bound (11). The exact recursion\nis M_(l+1)=2M_l+r_l, M_0=1. With A_mass=a/(c-2),\n\n    M_l=A_mass c^l+(1-A_mass)2^l,\n    0<A_mass<1,\n    a<=r_l/M_l<=r, M_(l+1)<=c M_l.                  (12)\n\nIndeed a<1/lambda=lambda-2<r, and M_l/c^l decreases from one to\nA_mass. Further, lambda<58/24 implies\nc<=lambda(129/128)<(58/24)(129/128)=1247/512<39/16.\nThus, using m>=128,\n\n    c<=39/16, r<=7/16, ac>=127/128,\n    theta_l=A_l/tau_(l+1)<=r_l/M_l<=r.              (13)\n\nThe elementary lambda bound follows from sqrt(2)<17/12, whose square\nis checked by 2<289/144. The displayed rational c bound has slack.\n\nFor the coefficient D_l in (11),\n\n    D_l=1+(1-1/tau_(l+2)) A_l A_(l+1)/tau_(l+1),\n    D_l/M_l <= 1/M_l+c(r_l/M_l)^2 <=1+c r^2.\n\nSince beta_(l+1)<=r and c r_l/M_l>=ca>=127/128,\n\n    (M_l+r_(l+1)-D_l beta_(l+1))/M_l\n       >=1+127/128-r(1+c r^2)\n       >=88511/65536 >4/3.                          (14)\n\nThe middle fraction is obtained by substituting c=39/16 and r=7/16.\nConsequently every interior transfer l+2<=v satisfies\n\n    beta_l <= (3/4) A_l/M_l <= (3/4) r_l/M_l.        (15)\n\nThis holds with arbitrary margins. They do not buy back the lost\ncorrelation. The final transfer l=v-1 only uses beta_l<=r.\n\n## 4. Exact location energy, including every retained height\n\nThe increments DeltaY_l defined after (7) are mutually orthogonal. For\nl<j, DeltaY_j is a linear combination of g_j,g_(j+1), both orthogonal to\nDeltaY_l. They are also orthogonal to g_v. Their exact squared norms are\n\n    ||DeltaY_l||^2=A_l^2 alpha_l^2-tau_(l+1)^2 alpha_(l+1)^2.\n\nHence the ACTUAL post-checkpoint point (3) obeys the telescoping identity\n\n    ||X||^2\n      = A_u^2 alpha_u^2\n            +sum_(i=u+1)^v (A_i^2-tau_i^2)alpha_i^2. (16)\n\nAll transverse displacement from the starting point is present in this\nidentity. Positive heights enter via\n\n    A_i^2-tau_i^2\n      =(r_i+C_i)(r_i-C_i-2w_i) <= r_i^2-C_i^2.      (17)\n\nDefine s=c r>1, rho=3/4 and eta=rho s^2. The inequality s>1 follows\nfrom c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1. By (13),\n\n    s<=273/256, eta<=223587/262144<1.\n\nThe first term of (16) is at most r^2. For i=u+j, 1<=j<=k,\nC_i=M_(u+j-1)<=c^(j-1) M_u. The norm recurrence and (15) give\n\n    C_i^2 alpha_i^2\n       <= r^2 rho eta^(j-1) if 1<=j<k,\n       <= r^2 eta^(k-1)     if j=k.                  (18)\n\nThis counts the loss on every transfer except the last, exactly once.\nFor k>=1 the sum of (18) is at most r^2 rho/(1-eta): the exact\nexpression is r^2[rho(1-eta^(k-1))/(1-eta)+eta^(k-1)],\nand rho/(1-eta)>=1 since s>1. Also r_i/C_i<=c r=s for i>u.\nCombining (16)--(18) yields the parameter-sensitive bound\n\n    ||X||^2 <= r^2+(s^2-1)r^2 rho/(1-rho s^2)\n             = r^2/(4-3c^2 r^2)=:Xi_m.              (19)\n\nFor k=0, ||X||^2<=r^2<=Xi_m gives the same result. Since the\nright side increases with c,r on the stated range,\n\n    Xi_m <= (49/256)/(4-3(273/256)^2)\n          =12544/38557 <1/3.                        (20)\n\nNo positive height is added as an independent payoff; it has already\naffected both the incoming amplitude and actual outgoing location.\n\n## 5. Actual terminal displacement and free-gradient completion\n\nThe entire right copy H_(m-1) lies after every selected first occurrence,\nso T>=M_(m-1)-1. Its final step is b=1+a<3/2. Set M=M_(m-1).\nThere is at least one queried tail sample, and the last queried position\nis X-(T-b)q. Optimum interpolation and smoothness on the ACTUAL final\nstep give, for every legal completion,\n\n    2f(x_n)\n      <=2<q,X>-(2T+1-b^2)||q||^2\n      <=||X||^2/(2T+1-b^2).                         (21)\n\nThe denominator is positive because M>=2^(m-1)>57. This argument\ndoes not prescribe the terminal gradient and does not replace the\nfull maximal interpolant by a reduced-anchor function. It is a necessary\nbound on that same full interpolant, using its retained last query.\n\nSince M_m<=c M, (13) and b^2<=9/4 imply\n\n    (2M_m-1)/(2T+1-b^2)\n       <=(2c M-1)/(2M-13/4) <5/2.                  (22)\n\nTo check the last inequality, its right side minus its left numerator\nafter multiplication by the positive denominator is at least\n(M-57)/8>0. Equations (19)--(22) prove (4), with the exact rational\nGamma=(5/2)(12544/38557)=31360/38557<5/6.\n\nThis finite estimate covers ANY number of selected relay transitions,\nincluding zero and one, and arbitrary positive interior heights. Its\nconstant is not obtained by fitting a numerical experiment.\n\n## 6. Exact benchmark comparison, derived from its actual recursion\n\nFor completeness rederive the needed bound for the exact benchmark,\ninstead of treating a previous unverified ruler calculation as a fact.\nLet p=log_2(lambda)>1 and write C_n^bench=S_circle(n)+1. The accepted\nrecursions in b98bb807d514e80d become the mass joins\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nFor F(t)=(1+t)^p-1 and t=exp(z), direct differentiation gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp z)\n       =p t(1+t)^(p-2)[(1+t)^p-1-p t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe positive bracket follows by integrating its positive derivative for\nt>0. Convexity at z and -z yields\nF(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. Set A=t^p B and\nD=(1+t)^p B. Then (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB and therefore\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/p)+B^(1/p))^p.\n\nInduction over the exact primitive splits gives C_n^bench<=(n+1)^p.\nSince sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A for nonnegative A,D, the dominant join\nsatisfies G(A,D)<=4A+D. Inducting over a split with n=k+l+1 gives\n\n    B_n <=1+4[(k+1)^p+l^p]<=1+4n^p.                 (23)\n\nThe last inequality is the superadditivity of x^p, obtained by\nintegrating its increasing derivative on nonnegative arguments.\n\nThe exact mass formula (12) gives\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n      =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m] -> e,\n\nbecause A_mass->1 and 2/c is uniformly below one. Thus (23) entails\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1) K <=2/e.                        (24)\n\nFor the sharper geometric asymptotic, Xi_m in (19) tends to\n(lambda-2)^2=1/lambda^2. The ratio in (22), before its coarse 5/2\nbound, has limsup at most lambda, uniformly in all selected checkpoints\nbecause T>=M_(m-1)-1 and M_(m-1) tends to infinity. Combining\n(19),(21),(24) gives (5): 2/(e lambda). No asymptotic equality for\nB_n was assumed and no other benchmark was substituted.\n\n## 7. {"id": "c035d2edbaffb2c0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:22:43.091948+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Strengthened full-class closure: on the actual near-critical ruler, every legal consecutive first-occurrence relay with an initially radial separating jet and successive gradients g_(l+1)=-beta_l g_l+fresh (beta_l>0, fresh orthogonal to ALL earlier vectors), at least one such transition, obeys 2f_end<=(31360/38557)K and exact-B limsup<=2/[e(1+sqrt2)]. Innovation norms, ALL later values/affine intercepts and positive interior heights are free; the final geometric margin may be arbitrarily large; the common QUERIED terminal cell has FREE last gradient. Legality itself forces the former norm-transfer parameterization. Sum of both old/new inequalities removes the intercepts and yields the same fixed correlation loss. This closes negative-intercept and arbitrary-fresh-norm retuning, not non-Markov, positive adjacent coefficients, other checkpoints or variable queried gaps.", "evidence": "# Positive interior heights do not rescue the consecutive Markov ruler relay\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No solver or fact_submit allocation.\nThis is a whole-mechanism obstruction with an explicit data domain, including\nall positive interior margins, actual old queries, actual displacement, and\nfree unqueried terminal gradient. It is NOT an upper bound on unrestricted\nW_n. Mathematical premises are accepted interpolation fact\n`1252b8823bb5a290` and the exact benchmark definitions in\n`b98bb807d514e80d`. All additional calculations are proved in this document.\n\n## 1. Schedule, complete family, and claimed comparison\n\nFor an integer m>=128, fix throughout the horizon\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a c^l,H_l), n=2^m-1,\n    M_l=1+sum H_l, r_l=a c^l, r=c-2,\n    K=1/(2M_m-1).\n\nSelect the FIRST occurrences of consecutive checkpoint levels u,...,v,\nwhere 0<=u<=v<=m-1. Write k=v-u. The initial ordinary block is H_u;\nbetween the first occurrences of levels l and l+1 the ordinary block is\nH_l. Thus its capacities (one plus its actual mass) are\n\n    C_u=M_u, C_l=M_(l-1) for u<l<=v.\n\nChoose ANY endpoint margins w_l in [0,r_l]. Define\n\n    tau_l=C_l+w_l, A_l=r_l-w_l.\n\nFor each u<=l<v with positive A_l choose\n\n    0<beta_l<=A_l/tau_(l+1), theta_l=A_l/tau_(l+1).\n\nLet e_u,...,e_v be orthonormal. The ACTUAL Euclidean vectors are\n\n    g_u=e_u/tau_u, alpha_u=1/tau_u,\n    g_(l+1)=-beta_l g_l+z_(l+1),\n    z_(l+1) orthogonal to all earlier vectors,\n    ||z_(l+1)||^2=alpha_l^2 beta_l(theta_l-beta_l),\n    alpha_(l+1)^2=theta_l beta_l alpha_l^2.\n\nZero degeneracies are interpreted by the same recurrence without division\nby a zero norm. A zero prescribed gradient has prescribed value zero, so\nin a legal realization its actual point is a minimizer and every later\niterate remains there. Such cases have zero final error and require no\nseparate positive-gradient estimates below.\n\nStart at X_u=e_u. Let X_l be the actual start of ordinary block l and\n\n    Y_l=X_l-tau_l g_l,\n    X_(l+1)=Y_l-A_l g_l,\n    Y_(l+1)=Y_l-A_l g_l-tau_(l+1)g_(l+1).       (1)\n\nThese are precisely the actual GD displacements: an ordinary block of\nmass C_l-1 followed by checkpoint 1+r_l has mass C_l+r_l, and\ntau_l+A_l=C_l+r_l.\n\nAt EVERY actual query in block l, including its checkpoint query, let t\nbe the ordinary cumulative mass already executed. Prescribe\n\n    x=Y_l+(tau_l-t)g_l,\n    grad f(x)=g_l,\n    f(x)=(tau_l-t-1/2)alpha_l^2,  0<=t<=C_l-1. (2)\n\nThe endpoint proximal height is w_l alpha_l^2. No height is discarded or\nassigned independently of a position and a value. Include the optimum\nsample f(0)=0, grad f(0)=0. Consider EVERY single convex differentiable\n1-smooth function realizing these data, in arbitrary finite dimension.\nIts values or gradients away from these samples are not fixed.\n\nExecute checkpoint v and then the whole remaining actual ruler tail,\nof total mass T. Its start is\n\n    X=Y_v-A_v g_v.                                  (3)\n\nRequire one arbitrary common gradient q at the QUERIED samples of this\nremaining tail. The terminal gradient is free. Allow all terminal-cell\nvalues, offsets, and extra dimensions. Equivalently complete this entire\nqueried prefix by the maximal interpolant of accepted 1252b8823bb5a290,\nwith every old anchor retained; other legal completions are also covered.\n\nEvery legal member of this entire family has true initial distance one\nand satisfies\n\n    2 f(x_n) <= Gamma K, Gamma=31360/38557<5/6.       (4)\n\nThe uniform asymptotic comparison to the ACTUAL exact benchmark is\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) 2 f(x_n)\n                <= 2/[e(1+sqrt(2))] < 1.            (5)\n\nBoth bounds are uniform over u,v, every margin and correlation, every\nlegal interpolant, and every allowed terminal cell. Thus repeated\npositive interior heights in this explicit Markov family do not supply\na benchmark-level witness.\n\n## 2. All-query legality, with no omitted remote plane\n\nThe accepted interpolation inequality in proximal notation is\n\n    delta_j >= delta_i + <g_i,y_j-y_i>,\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.             (6)\n\nFor the data (2) put d=tau_l-t-1. Then\n\n    y=Y_l+d g_l, delta=d alpha_l^2,\n    w_l<=d<=tau_l-1.\n\nFor i<j define\n\n    P_ij=product_(l=i)^(j-1) beta_l,\n    Qprod_ij=product_(l=i)^(j-1) theta_l,\n    R_l=A_l-tau_(l+1) beta_l>=0,\n    L_ij=sum_(l=i)^(j-1) (-1)^(l-i) R_l P_il,\n\nwith empty product one. Directly from (1) and the vector recurrence,\n\n    <g_i,g_j>=(-1)^(j-i) alpha_i^2 P_ij,\n    alpha_j^2=alpha_i^2 P_ij Qprod_ij,\n    <g_i,Y_j>=-alpha_i^2 L_ij for i<j,\n    <g_i,Y_j>=0 for i>=j.                            (7)\n\nTo verify the last identity, each increment\nDeltaY_l=Y_(l+1)-Y_l=-A_l g_l-tau_(l+1)g_(l+1)\nis orthogonal to g_(l+1) because\n<g_l,g_(l+1)>=-beta_l alpha_l^2 and\ntau_(l+1)alpha_(l+1)^2=A_l beta_l alpha_l^2.\nEvery later gradient is a scalar multiple of g_(l+1) plus fresh\northogonal directions, so DeltaY_l is orthogonal to all those gradients.\nSince Y_u=0, summation proves the claimed identity. Taking the earlier\ngradient inner products of the same increments proves the formula for\nL_ij, including its signs.\n\nWe will prove theta_l<=r<1 below; hence beta_l<1 and Qprod_ij<1. By (7),\nevery proposed future supporting plane g_j has value at an old proximal\nquery Y_i+d g_i at most its current value d alpha_i^2. The comparison\nholds at odd lag by its negative sign and at even lag because P_ij<=1.\nFor an old plane i at a later query in block j, its value minus the\ncurrent plane is exactly\n\n    alpha_i^2 [-L_ij+d P_ij((-1)^(j-i)-Qprod_ij)].        (8)\n\nFor even lag the exact strongest condition is\n\n    L_ij >= (tau_j-1)P_ij(1-Qprod_ij),  j-i even.        (9)\n\nFor odd lag no extra condition is needed once (9) holds for all even\nlags: at lag one L=R_i>=0; at larger odd lag,\nL_ij=L_(i,j-1)+P_(i,j-1) R_(j-1)>=0 by (9), and the\nd coefficient in (8) is negative. Thus (9), for all even pairs, is a\nNECESSARY AND SUFFICIENT complete legality test for the prescribed\nrelay data. Queries inside a block require no additional comparisons:\ntheir d coordinates lie between the two actual endpoint queries.\n\nFor sufficiency construct the ONE fixed function\n\n    J(z)=max(0,<g_u,z>,...,<g_v,z>),\n    f_min(x)=min_z [J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                (10)\n\nAll comparisons just proved say that the selected g_l is a subgradient\nof J at the proposed y. Its subgradient inequality plus the quadratic\nterm proves y is the unique minimizer in (10), with value and gradient\ngiven by (2). To recall the elementary smoothness argument, adding two\nproximal subgradient inequalities gives\n||g(x)-g(x')||^2<=<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>. The two minimizing\ninequalities then identify the envelope derivative as g and prove its\n1-Lipschitz property; joint convexity proves convexity. Thus (10) is a\ngenuine realization whenever (9) holds. Necessity of (9) follows from\n(6) for the actual endpoint query in block i and first query in block j.\nThis also shows that no other interpolant can evade (9).\n\nThe bound below is for ALL interpolants of (2), not only (10). In\nparticular it never assumes a virtual Y_l is a minimizer of another\ninterpolant. That unsupported inference would lose the positive-height\nand location distinction that this calculation is intended to retain.\n\nFor any legal interpolant, the first checkpoint sample is\nx=(w_u+1)g_u, f=(w_u+1/2)alpha_u^2. Apply interpolation\nwith ANY minimizer z. Since its minimum is zero, (6) gives\n<g_u,z><=0. Therefore <e_u,z><=0, and\n||e_u-z||^2=1+||z||^2-2<e_u,z>>=1.\nOrigin is a minimizer. The actual closest-minimizer distance is exactly\none for every legal completion, including the maximal one.\n\n## 3. One retained old query still forces a fixed correlation loss\n\nUse (9) at j=l+2. With P=beta_l beta_(l+1) its exact form is\n\n    A_l-beta_l(tau_(l+1)+A_(l+1))\n                 +D_l beta_l beta_(l+1)>=0,\n    D_l=1+(tau_(l+2)-1)theta_l theta_(l+1).           (11)\n\nThis compares an old ACTUAL query against a later ACTUAL query. Positive\nmargins cancel in its middle coefficient:\n\n    tau_(l+1)+A_(l+1)=M_l+r_(l+1).\n\nHere are the schedule estimates needed to bound (11). The exact recursion\nis M_(l+1)=2M_l+r_l, M_0=1. With A_mass=a/(c-2),\n\n    M_l=A_mass c^l+(1-A_mass)2^l,\n    0<A_mass<1,\n    a<=r_l/M_l<=r, M_(l+1)<=c M_l.                  (12)\n\nIndeed a<1/lambda=lambda-2<r, and M_l/c^l decreases from one to\nA_mass. Further, lambda<58/24 implies\nc<=lambda(129/128)<(58/24)(129/128)=1247/512<39/16.\nThus, using m>=128,\n\n    c<=39/16, r<=7/16, ac>=127/128,\n    theta_l=A_l/tau_(l+1)<=r_l/M_l<=r.              (13)\n\nThe elementary lambda bound follows from sqrt(2)<17/12, whose square\nis checked by 2<289/144. The displayed rational c bound has slack.\n\nFor the coefficient D_l in (11),\n\n    D_l=1+(1-1/tau_(l+2)) A_l A_(l+1)/tau_(l+1),\n    D_l/M_l <= 1/M_l+c(r_l/M_l)^2 <=1+c r^2.\n\nSince beta_(l+1)<=r and c r_l/M_l>=ca>=127/128,\n\n    (M_l+r_(l+1)-D_l beta_(l+1))/M_l\n       >=1+127/128-r(1+c r^2)\n       >=88511/65536 >4/3.                          (14)\n\nThe middle fraction is obtained by substituting c=39/16 and r=7/16.\nConsequently every interior transfer l+2<=v satisfies\n\n    beta_l <= (3/4) A_l/M_l <= (3/4) r_l/M_l.        (15)\n\nThis holds with arbitrary margins. They do not buy back the lost\ncorrelation. The final transfer l=v-1 only uses beta_l<=r.\n\n## 4. Exact location energy, including every retained height\n\nThe increments DeltaY_l defined after (7) are mutually orthogonal. For\nl<j, DeltaY_j is a linear combination of g_j,g_(j+1), both orthogonal to\nDeltaY_l. They are also orthogonal to g_v. Their exact squared norms are\n\n    ||DeltaY_l||^2=A_l^2 alpha_l^2-tau_(l+1)^2 alpha_(l+1)^2.\n\nHence the ACTUAL post-checkpoint point (3) obeys the telescoping identity\n\n    ||X||^2\n      = A_u^2 alpha_u^2\n            +sum_(i=u+1)^v (A_i^2-tau_i^2)alpha_i^2. (16)\n\nAll transverse displacement from the starting point is present in this\nidentity. Positive heights enter via\n\n    A_i^2-tau_i^2\n      =(r_i+C_i)(r_i-C_i-2w_i) <= r_i^2-C_i^2.      (17)\n\nDefine s=c r>1, rho=3/4 and eta=rho s^2. The inequality s>1 follows\nfrom c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1. By (13),\n\n    s<=273/256, eta<=223587/262144<1.\n\nThe first term of (16) is at most r^2. For i=u+j, 1<=j<=k,\nC_i=M_(u+j-1)<=c^(j-1) M_u. The norm recurrence and (15) give\n\n    C_i^2 alpha_i^2\n       <= r^2 rho eta^(j-1) if 1<=j<k,\n       <= r^2 eta^(k-1)     if j=k.                  (18)\n\nThis counts the loss on every transfer except the last, exactly once.\nFor k>=1 the sum of (18) is at most r^2 rho/(1-eta): the exact\nexpression is r^2[rho(1-eta^(k-1))/(1-eta)+eta^(k-1)],\nand rho/(1-eta)>=1 since s>1. Also r_i/C_i<=c r=s for i>u.\nCombining (16)--(18) yields the parameter-sensitive bound\n\n    ||X||^2 <= r^2+(s^2-1)r^2 rho/(1-rho s^2)\n             = r^2/(4-3c^2 r^2)=:Xi_m.              (19)\n\nFor k=0, ||X||^2<=r^2<=Xi_m gives the same result. Since the\nright side increases with c,r on the stated range,\n\n    Xi_m <= (49/256)/(4-3(273/256)^2)\n          =12544/38557 <1/3.                        (20)\n\nNo positive height is added as an independent payoff; it has already\naffected both the incoming amplitude and actual outgoing location.\n\n## 5. Actual terminal displacement and free-gradient completion\n\nThe entire right copy H_(m-1) lies after every selected first occurrence,\nso T>=M_(m-1)-1. Its final step is b=1+a<3/2. Set M=M_(m-1).\nThere is at least one queried tail sample, and the last queried position\nis X-(T-b)q. Optimum interpolation and smoothness on the ACTUAL final\nstep give, for every legal completion,\n\n    2f(x_n)\n      <=2<q,X>-(2T+1-b^2)||q||^2\n      <=||X||^2/(2T+1-b^2).                         (21)\n\nThe denominator is positive because M>=2^(m-1)>57. This argument\ndoes not prescribe the terminal gradient and does not replace the\nfull maximal interpolant by a reduced-anchor function. It is a necessary\nbound on that same full interpolant, using its retained last query.\n\nSince M_m<=c M, (13) and b^2<=9/4 imply\n\n    (2M_m-1)/(2T+1-b^2)\n       <=(2c M-1)/(2M-13/4) <5/2.                  (22)\n\nTo check the last inequality, its right side minus its left numerator\nafter multiplication by the positive denominator is at least\n(M-57)/8>0. Equations (19)--(22) prove (4), with the exact rational\nGamma=(5/2)(12544/38557)=31360/38557<5/6.\n\nThis finite estimate covers ANY number of selected relay transitions,\nincluding zero and one, and arbitrary positive interior heights. Its\nconstant is not obtained by fitting a numerical experiment.\n\n## 6. Exact benchmark comparison, derived from its actual recursion\n\nFor completeness rederive the needed bound for the exact benchmark,\ninstead of treating a previous unverified ruler calculation as a fact.\nLet p=log_2(lambda)>1 and write C_n^bench=S_circle(n)+1. The accepted\nrecursions in b98bb807d514e80d become the mass joins\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nFor F(t)=(1+t)^p-1 and t=exp(z), direct differentiation gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp z)\n       =p t(1+t)^(p-2)[(1+t)^p-1-p t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe positive bracket follows by integrating its positive derivative for\nt>0. Convexity at z and -z yields\nF(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. Set A=t^p B and\nD=(1+t)^p B. Then (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB and therefore\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/p)+B^(1/p))^p.\n\nInduction over the exact primitive splits gives C_n^bench<=(n+1)^p.\nSince sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A for nonnegative A,D, the dominant join\nsatisfies G(A,D)<=4A+D. Inducting over a split with n=k+l+1 gives\n\n    B_n <=1+4[(k+1)^p+l^p]<=1+4n^p.                 (23)\n\nThe last inequality is the superadditivity of x^p, obtained by\nintegrating its increasing derivative on nonnegative arguments.\n\nThe exact mass formula (12) gives\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n      =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m] -> e,\n\nbecause A_mass->1 and 2/c is uniformly below one. Thus (23) entails\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1) K <=2/e.                        (24)\n\nFor the sharper geometric asymptotic, Xi_m in (19) tends to\n(lambda-2)^2=1/lambda^2. The ratio in (22), before its coarse 5/2\nbound, has limsup at most lambda, uniformly in all selected checkpoints\nbecause T>=M_(m-1)-1 and M_(m-1) tends to infinity. Combining\n(19),(21),(24) gives (5): 2/(e lambda). No asymptotic equality for\nB_n was assumed and no other benchmark was substituted.\n\n## 7. Scope and the materially different next step\n\nThe obstruction includes positive height at the FIRST selected endpoint\nas well as all later ones, every nonstationary Markov correlation,\nevery legal interpolant of the specified data, arbitrary dimension,\nand a free last gradient after the common queried final cell.\n\nSections 1--6 do NOT cover negative affine intercepts in the relay data;\nSection 8 below closes that additional freedom while retaining the same\nposition/gradient family and the first separating jet. The argument still\ndoes NOT cover non-Markov inherited correlations, other checkpoint organizations, or\ngradient changes inside the ordinary ruler gaps or final queried cell.\nThose are genuine changes of mechanism, not choices of w_l in this\nfamily. In particular max3's shared-coordinate split-and-merge direction\nis distinct and remains outside this obstruction.\n\nThe next max construction must break the location identity (16) or its\nfixed-loss consequence (15), or vary the remaining queried tail. Merely\nputting larger positive homogeneous heights at more relay endpoints\ncannot provide the needed terminal-correlation credit. This conclusion\nis a self-contained candidate, not a new fact and not the original goal.\n\n## 8. Arbitrary later affine intercepts obey the SAME bound\n\nThere is a stronger version that does not prescribe homogeneous function\nvalues at any relay block after the first. Keep EXACTLY the positions,\ngradients, margins w_l in [0,r_l], and Markov norm recurrence in Section 1.\nKeep the first endpoint value (w_u+1/2)alpha_u^2. At each later block l\nallow an arbitrary affine intercept\n\n    -w_l alpha_l^2 <= eta_l <=0, eta_u=0,\n    f(Y_l+(tau_l-t)g_l)\n        =eta_l+(tau_l-t-1/2)alpha_l^2.               (25)\n\nThus the ACTUAL endpoint proximal height is eta_l+w_l alpha_l^2,\nanywhere from zero to the homogeneous maximum. These are precisely the\npossible values consistent with the two optimum inequalities: their\nlower inequality requires the actual height to be nonnegative, and the\nupper inequality requires eta_l<=0. Equality of the gradient throughout\na block forces (25) once its endpoint value is chosen. Consequently\nthere are no other unaccounted function values within this position and\ngradient family. Only genuinely legal choices of all eta_l are included.\n\nBounds (4), (5), (19), and (21) remain valid for this ENTIRE extended\nclass, including all its arbitrary terminal queried-cell levels and free\nlast gradient. In particular negative intercepts cannot repair this\nfamily after its retained first separating query.\n\nHere is a full replacement for the only value-dependent step of the\nupper proof. Adding both ordered interpolation inequalities between two\nactual samples gives\n\n    <g_i-g_j,y_i-y_j>>=0.                            (26)\n\nTake the endpoint of block l and the first query in block l+2. By (7),\nthe left side divided by alpha_l^2 is\n\n    L_(l,l+2)+w_l(1-beta_l beta_(l+1))\n      +(tau_(l+2)-1)beta_l beta_(l+1)\n                               (theta_l theta_(l+1)-1).\n\nSubstituting L and A_l+w_l=r_l turns (26) into the EXACT necessary\ncondition\n\n    r_l-beta_l(M_l+r_(l+1))\n          +(D_l-w_l)beta_l beta_(l+1)>=0,            (27)\n\nwhere D_l is the coefficient in (11). Since w_l>=0, it follows that\nr_l-beta_l(M_l+r_(l+1)-D_l beta_(l+1))>=0.\nThe same denominator estimate (14), which uses only the actual geometry,\ntherefore proves\n\n    beta_l <=(3/4)r_l/M_l for every l+2<=v.          (28)\n\nThe sharper A_l numerator in (15) need not survive arbitrary intercepts;\nit is NOT asserted here. Formula (18) and the rest of the norm proof use\nonly the weaker bound (28), so their proofs and constants are unchanged.\nEvery position identity in Section 4 is independent of function values.\nThe initial value eta_u=0 still forces <e_u,z><=0 at every minimizer,\nexactly as in Section 2. Hence the true initial distance remains one.\nFinally Section 5 uses only the actual optimum and the retained last\nqueried jet. This proves the complete asserted extension.\n\nFor clarity, the COMPLETE legality conditions for (25) can also be\nwritten explicitly, so intercept freedom is not a relaxation of the\ndata domain. In addition to the intervals in (25), for EVERY i<j set\nepsilon_ij=(-1)^(j-i), and define\n\n    d_ij=tau_j-1 if j-i is even, and d_ij=w_j otherwise.\n\nAll pairs are equivalent to the two conditions\n\n    eta_j-eta_i <=w_i alpha_i^2(1-epsilon_ij P_ij),\n    eta_i-eta_j <=alpha_i^2\n                  [L_ij-d_ij P_ij(epsilon_ij-Qprod_ij)].  (29)\n\nTo prove this equivalence, evaluate the future plane eta_j+<g_j,z>\nat every old proximal point Y_i+d g_i. Its slope relative to the current\nplane is alpha_i^2(epsilon_ij P_ij-1)<0, so its maximum occurs at\nthe actual endpoint d=w_i, giving the first condition. Evaluating an old\nplane at a query of block j gives (8) plus eta_i-eta_j. The maximizing\nactual d is tau_j-1 at even lag and w_j at odd lag, giving the second.\nAll within-block pairs are equalities, and the zero-plane comparisons\nare exactly the lower endpoints of (25). Optimum upper inequalities\nare eta_l<=0. Thus every ordered pair has been retained.\n\nWhen (25),(29) hold, the fixed Moreau envelope of\nJ_eta(z)=max(0,max_l[eta_l+<g_l,z>]) realizes all relay samples by\nthe same subgradient argument as (10). It has minimum zero at origin.\nConversely (6) forces every condition in (29) for any legal realization.\nThis supplies a finite real-function realization of the FULL legal data\ndomain, although the upper bound permits all other interpolants as well.\n\nThe fallback plan of altering only these intercepts has therefore failed\nwith a whole-domain quantitative reason. Retuning levels alone leaves\nboth (27) and the location-energy identity (16) intact. Section 9 further\nchecks whether changing the norm-transfer equality is an actual degree\nof freedom for this Markov class.\n\n## 9. The norm-transfer identity is FORCED for every fresh antialigned relay\n\nThere is no separate norm-parameter escape within the following larger,\nintrinsically stated class. Use the same selected first-occurrence blocks\nu,...,v, now with v>=u+1. Start at e_u, with optimum 0 and a positive\nparallel first gradient g_u=t e_u, t>0. Require only that successive\nnonzero relay gradients have the form\n\n    g_(l+1)=-beta_l g_l+z_(l+1), beta_l>0,\n    z_(l+1) orthogonal to the entire earlier span.   (30)\n\nThe sizes of z_(l+1), beta_l, all gradient norms and ALL subsequent\nsample values are initially free. Keep one separating first endpoint\njet satisfying equality in its upper optimum inequality,\n\n    f(x_endpoint,u)\n        =<g_u,x_endpoint,u>-||g_u||^2/2.            (31)\n\nRequire actual legality of every sample and common gradients on the\nspecified ordinary blocks. After checkpoint v allow the same arbitrary\ncommon QUERIED terminal cell with free last gradient. Then bounds\n(4) and (5) hold for this ENTIRE class too.\n\nTo prove this, let X_l be the actual start of block l and define from\nthe ACTUAL data\n\n    tau_l=<g_l,X_l>/||g_l||^2,\n    w_l=tau_l-C_l, Y_l=X_l-tau_l g_l.               (32)\n\nAt the endpoint of block l, the two optimum interpolation inequalities\ngive\n\n    0<=f_endpoint,l-||g_l||^2/2\n       <=<g_l,X_l-C_l g_l>.\n\nTherefore tau_l>=C_l, so w_l>=0. Initially tau_u=1/t and Y_u=0.\nSuppose <g_l,Y_l>=0. The actual recurrence gives\n\n    X_(l+1)=Y_l-(r_l-w_l)g_l.\n\nThe fresh direction in (30) is orthogonal to Y_l and g_l. Hence\n\n    <g_(l+1),X_(l+1)>\n       =beta_l(r_l-w_l)||g_l||^2\n       =tau_(l+1)||g_(l+1)||^2.                    (33)\n\nIts right side is positive. Thus A_l=r_l-w_l>0 for EVERY l<v,\nand (33) forces exactly the norm-transfer identity used in Section 1.\nThe Euclidean Gram inequality\n||g_(l+1)||^2>=beta_l^2||g_l||^2 further forces\nbeta_l<=A_l/tau_(l+1). Also Y_(l+1)=X_(l+1)-tau_(l+1)g_(l+1)\nis orthogonal to g_(l+1). The fresh-space condition propagates the\northogonality of earlier increments to all later gradients just as in\n(7). Thus the entire geometric representation is forced; it was not\nan extra chosen restriction on the gradient norms.\n\nFor the LAST margin w_v, (32) gives only w_v>=0, with NO upper bound.\nThis is harmless and should not be silently excluded. Review each step:\n\n* Every theta_l and residual R_l, l<v, still has A_l>=0 and\n  tau_(l+1)>=C_(l+1), so all correlation and denominator bounds hold.\n* Formula (27) uses w_l only for l<=v-2. The factor\n  (tau_v-1)/tau_v in its coefficient is <=1 for arbitrary w_v>=0.\n* The final term in (16) obeys\n  A_v^2-tau_v^2=(r_v+C_v)(r_v-C_v-2w_v)<=r_v^2-C_v^2\n  for EVERY w_v>=0, even if A_v<0.\n* The first term still has w_u<r_u, because v>=u+1 and (33)\n  forces it. This is why at least one gradient transition is specified.\n\nConsequently (19)--(22) and the exact benchmark comparison are\nunchanged. By (31), eta_u=0, so the separating query still certifies\nthe true initial distance one. All later possible values fall precisely\nwithin (25), with its now unbounded last w_v, and the all-pair conditions\n(29) remain the exact legality test. Hence the extension is complete.\n\nIf a subsequent gradient vanishes, its sample is a minimizer, so the\nentire remaining trajectory is stationary and the conclusion is direct.\nThe case of only one relay gradient, with arbitrary first margin, is\nthe previously identified two-queried-cell problem; Section 9 makes no\nnew claim about its sharp finite constant. Section 1 already includes\nthat boundary case when w_u<=r_u.\n\nThe intrinsic class (30) is now closed even with free innovation norms,\nall positive heights and affine intercepts after (31), and unbounded\nlast geometric margin. A genuinely new next construction must use\ninherited components beyond the immediately previous gradient, a\npositive adjacent coefficient at some transitions, a nonparallel or\nnonseparating initial jet with a fully recomputed normalization, other\ncheckpoint organization, or varying ordinary/final queried gradients.\nMerely specifying a different fresh-vector norm is not a new route:\nlegality forces (33).\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["62bd541a94134051", "b4fac4b78e891348", "6161ec62bd499f3d"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "research/max_positive_interior_markov_obstruction.md", "status": "complete_unverified_candidate"}, "glossary": {"eta_l": "Affine proximal intercept of relay block l, with -w_l||g_l||²<=eta_l<=0 and eta_u=0.", "tau_l": "Intrinsic coefficient <g_l,X_l>/||g_l||²>=C_l.", "w_l": "Intrinsic geometric margin tau_l-C_l; differs from actual height if eta_l<0.", "A_l": "r_l-w_l, positive before every further antialigned fresh transition.", "C_l": "One plus actual mass of the ordinary relay block l.", "alpha_l": "Norm of g_l.", "beta_l": "Positive coefficient in g_(l+1)=-beta_l g_l+z_(l+1).", "z_(l+1)": "Fresh vector orthogonal to the entire previous span, with initially unrestricted norm.", "theta_l": "Forced transfer ratio A_l/tau_(l+1).", "Xi_m": "Upper bound (c-2)^2/[4-3c²(c-2)²] on the actual remaining-tail start norm squared.", "P_ij": "Product of beta_l for i<=l<j.", "Qprod_ij": "Product of theta_l for i<=l<j.", "L_ij": "Alternating weighted residual from all retained relay planes.", "K": "Attained global Huber score1/(2M_m-1).", "Gamma": "Uniform bound31360/38557 on this class divided by K."}}
{"id": "b46af9bbdbe52d0f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:33:24.404176+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C40 exponent-count clarification: the final bound includes qc<1 by replacing (qc)^(N-1) with (1+1/8192)^N before grouping fourth powers. Thus the stated uniform score bound also covers r=0 and low blocking depths.", "evidence": "Let v=1+1/8192>1. From m>=65536 and q<=c-2, 0<qc<=v, hence (qc)^(N-1)<=v^(N-1)<v^N, whether or not qc<1. The binomial expansion gives v^4<1+1/1024, and (1+1/1024)/(201/200)=205000/205824<255/256. Therefore 5(qc)^(N-1)/(201/200)^(N/4-1)<5(201/200)(255/256)^(N/4), exactly the bound in e1d71a04bf4d0921. The proof file now states this intermediate replacement explicitly.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["e1d71a04bf4d0921"], "artifact": "research/c40_two_predecessor_merge_obstruction.md"}, "glossary": {}}
{"id": "bc10e343efecf012", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:53:55.545548+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C42 charges arbitrary odd-split changes in the actual shared-coordinate two-predecessor DAG. For m>=512, N>=512 phases, arbitrary even splits and at most J<=N/4096 changes between consecutive odd splits, EVERY legal choice of energies and EVERY full terminal completion has score/K<(45/8)exp(-N/128)<45/128, hence limsup B_n score<=45/(64e)<1. The proof gives an explicit ruler-level variation penalty for all split policies; every J=o(N) policy has vanishing payoff as N grows. This is a scoped unverified obstruction, not an upper bound on unrestricted W.", "evidence": "# C42. Arbitrary splits with a quantified cost for odd-split changes\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate; no verification allocation. No numerical\nsolver or sweep. The family below is explicit for m>=512, N>=512.\n\n## 1. The fixed real family and all normal comparisons\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Choose 0<=r<=m-9, let k=m-r,\nN=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M, and\n\n    A=1+q,\n    q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n    tau_(N-1)=t>=1, T_(N-1)=Mt.\n\nThe N ordinary blocks are exactly the actual copies of H_r; their\ncheckpoints have excesses Mq_i. All original physical steps are retained.\n\nChoose every theta_j in[0,1] independently for 2<=j<N. Choose positive\nR_i, E_i=R_i^2, and transfers\n\n    a_01=R_1, a_02=theta_2 R_2,\n    a_(i,i+1)=(1-theta_(i+1))R_(i+1), i>=1,\n    a_(i,i+2)=theta_(i+2)R_(i+2), i>=1,\n\nomitting out-of-range indices. Every j>=2 receives total mass R_j\nfrom its two actual predecessors. Put\n\n    p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i,\n    C=conv(0,p_i), F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nThe p_i are actual independent vectors in R^N with triangular positive\ndiagonal, not a formal covariance matrix. The projection variational\ninequality and maximizing-difference bounds prove\ngrad F_C=P_C, convexity and1-smoothness.\n\nStart at R_0e_0. Before phase i>=1 its only nonzero coordinates are\nR_i e_i+theta_(i+1)R_(i+1)e_(i+1); subtract each actual step times p_i.\nThe checkpoint exhausts coordinate i and creates the next prescribed state.\n\nHere is the entire legality test. During phase i>=1 put\nz=(physical partial ordinary mass+1)/M in[1/M,1],\nd=theta_(i+1), e=theta_(i+2).\nMultiply all normal values by M. The own value is\n\n [(tau_i-z)E_i\n  -(1-d)(d tau_i+z(1-d))E_(i+1)\n  -z e^2 E_(i+2)]/tau_i^2.                               (1)\n\nThe only possibly nonzero FUTURE comparisons are\n\n [(d+z(1-d)/tau_i)E_(i+1)\n           -z e(1-e)E_(i+2)/tau_i]/tau_(i+1),            (2)\n z e E_(i+2)/(tau_i tau_(i+2)).                           (3)\n\nEvery past normal is nonpositive because its positive coordinate has\nbeen exhausted and all remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nAll other future normals are zero; the zero vertex must also be tested.\nTherefore (1)>=(2),(3),0 at BOTH z=1/M and z=1 is necessary and sufficient\nfor every actual query. At the root use its displayed p_0 to test the two\nendpoints directly. The terminal block requires t>=1.\nAll omitted final terms are zero.\n\nThe class is nonempty for every chosen sequence theta_j and finite word: fix terminal energy1,\nthen choose E_i successively backward sufficiently large to satisfy these\nfinite inequalities. Its own coefficient tau_i-z>=q_i>0 and every\nother term uses already fixed future energies. Finally choose E_0.\nThis is a constructive finite specification; the upper bound covers EVERY\nlegal energy choice, not just this conservative policy.\n\nEvery original query has F_C(x_i)=<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2.\nThe minimizer set C^polar has all coordinates nonpositive, by backward\ninduction in <p_i,z><=0. Thus0 is the actual closest minimizer and its\ninitial squared distance is E_0. The original objective and primitive\ndefect, with htot the entire physical mass, are\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0),\n V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.            (4)\n\nThe defect follows by telescoping the identity\n2h_iF_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2.\n\n## 2. Combine BOTH high-phase future comparisons\n\nConsider an internal low/high pair i,i+1, where i is even and2<=i<=N-4.\nCall it matched if theta_(i+1)=theta_(i+3), and mismatched otherwise.\nTHIS SECTION applies to a matched pair only.\nPut Q=q_(i+1), B=1+Q, rho=A/B and LOCAL u=theta_(i+2).\nThis local u can differ arbitrarily from pair to pair. For this matched pair put v=theta_(i+1)=theta_(i+3).\nNo equality with odd fractions outside this pair is assumed.\nThe low phase has d=v,e=u. Its comparisons with i+1 and i+2 give\n\n q E_i >= L1 E_(i+1)+L2 E_(i+2),\n    L1=(1+vq)(1-v+rho), L2=u[u-(1-u)rho],                 (5)\n E_(i+2)/E_i <= q/[u(1+u)],                              (6)\n\nwith (6) read as no restriction at u=0.\n\nThe HIGH phase has d=u,e=v. Its two future comparisons are\n\n Q E_(i+1) >= H1 E_(i+2)+H2 E_(i+3),\n    H1=(1+uQ)(1-u+B/A),\n    H2=v[v-(1-v)B/A],                                   (7)\n Q E_(i+1) >= H3 E_(i+2)+H4 E_(i+3),\n    H3=(1-u)(1+uQ),\n    H4=v[v+B/Bnext],                                    (8)\n\nwhere Bnext=tau_(i+3)>0 is the actual next high capacity, or terminal t.\nNo future residual has been reset.\n\nIf H2>=0, dropping its nonnegative term in(7) gives coefficient H1.\nIf H2<0, then0<v<1 and H4>0. Use(8) to upper-bound E_(i+3), multiply\nthat upper bound by H2<0, and insert it into(7). This gives\n\n Q E_(i+1) >= [(H1 H4-H2 H3)/(H4-H2)]E_(i+2).\n\nThe coefficient is exactly\n\n    (1+uQ)[1-u+(v Bnext+B)/(A+(1-v)Bnext)].\n\nThe ratio here lies between B/A and v/(1-v), because it is a ratio of\npositive affine functions of Bnext. At v=0, (7) has H2=0 and gives H1\ndirectly; v=1 belongs to the nonnegative case. Thus in ALL cases,\n\n Q E_(i+1) >=\n    (1+uQ)[1-u+min(B/A,v/(1-v))]E_(i+2),                 (9)\n\nwith v/(1-v)=+infinity at v=1. This is the inherited-coordinate cost\nmissed by using only the high zero comparison.\n\nCombine(5),(9),(6). The exact necessary pair bound is\n\n    E_(i+2)/E_i <= qQ / max(G, Q u(1+u)),                 (10)\n\nwhere\n\n G=(1+vq)(1-v+rho)(1+uQ)\n                       [1-u+min(1/rho,v/(1-v))]\n       +Q u[u-(1-u)rho].                                (11)\n\nThe denominator in(10) is shown positive next, so no sign assumption\nis hidden in taking its reciprocal.\n\n## 3. The scalar comparison: two splits still force a loss\n\nDefine G0=(1-u)(1+rho)+Qu. We prove\n\n    G>=min(G0,A).                                        (12)\n\nWhen v<=1/(1+rho), the minimum in(11) is v/(1-v).\nSet w=1-v. Then\n\n G=(A-qw)(1+rho/w)(1-uw)(1+uQ)\n                         +Qu^2-Qrho u(1-u).\n\nBoth nonnegative factors (A-qw)(1+rho/w) and1-uw increase as v increases:\nthe first has w derivative -q-A rho/w^2<0, and the second has w\nderivative -u<=0. Therefore its minimum is at v=0, where direct\ncancellation gives G=G0.\n\nFor v>=1/(1+rho), the minimum is1/rho. The only v-dependent factor\nis (1+qv)(1-v+rho), a concave quadratic. Its minimum on this interval\nis at an endpoint. At the lower endpoint the preceding result gives G>=G0.\nAt v=1, expansion gives\n\n G=A(1+uQ)+Qu^2+rho(1-u)[A+qQu] >=A.\n\nThis proves(12), including all endpoints u,v=0,1.\n\nThe ruler parameters obey, for m>=512,\n\n    2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16, Q>=cq>=1-1/m>=99/100.      (13)\n\nIndeed M_j=[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j, with0<a<c-2, gives\na<=q<=c-2 and M_m<=M c^k. The rational bounds follow from\nlambda<169/70 and a>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5.\n\nIf Q>=1, G0 is a convex combination of1+rho and Q, so G0>=1;\nalso A>1. If99/100<=Q<1, then rho=A/(1+Q)>7/10.\nFor u<=3/4, G0>=99/100+(1/4)(1/100+7/10)>1.\nFor u>=3/4, Q u(1+u)>=2079/1600>1.\nThus\n\n    max(G,Q u(1+u))>=1 at EVERY MATCHED high checkpoint.           (14)\n\nAt a LEVEL-ONE high checkpoint Q=cq<=273/256,\nrho>=1792/2645>2/3. If u<=3/4,\n\n    G0>=99/100+(1/4)(1/100+2/3)\n         =1391/1200>9/8,\n\nand A>7/5>9/8. If u>=3/4 the second term is again at least2079/1600.\nHence, uniformly in BOTH independent splits,\n\n    max(G,Q u(1+u))>=kappa:=9/8 at every MATCHED level-one high.  (15)\n\n## 4. Explicit cost of an arbitrary mismatched pair\n\nNow let v=theta_(i+1), u=theta_(i+2), w=theta_(i+3) be independent.\nKeep the low comparison(5), but use just the high phase's zero comparison:\n\n    Q E_(i+1) >=(1-u)(1+uQ)E_(i+2).\n\nIts discarded E_(i+3) term is w^2 E_(i+3)>=0. Since L1>0, substitution\ngives qQ E_i>=Fweak E_(i+2), where\n\n Fweak=(1+qv)(1-v+rho)(1-u)(1+uQ)\n                              +Q u[u-(1-u)rho].\n\nFor fixed u, this is concave in v, so its minimum is at v=0 or v=1.\nAt v=0 it equals\n\n    F0=(1-u)(1+rho)+Qu>=min(1+rho,Q)>=99/100.\n\nAt v=1 it equals\n\n    F1=Qu^2+rho(1-u)[A+qQu]\n                     >=Qu^2+D(1-u),   D=A^2/(1+Q).\n\nHere D<2Q and D/2<99/100: use A<23/16 and Q>=99/100.\nThe quadratic on the right has minimum D-D^2/(4Q)>=D/2 on[0,1].\nThus every pair, however its two odd fractions differ, satisfies\n\n    E_(i+2)/E_i <= qQ*2(1+Q)/A^2.                       (23)\n\nIf its high ruler level is ell=nu_2(i+2)>=1, then Q=q c^ell and\n\n    2(1+Q)/A^2 <= c^ell.                               (24)\n\nIndeed (24) is equivalent to 2/c^ell<=1+q^2, which follows from c>2.\nThis rough replacement costs at most c^ell relative to denominator1,\nor at most kappa*c^ell when a level-one kappa factor was lost.\n\nLet J be the number of mismatched INTERNAL pairs, and let Lbad be the\nsum of their high levels ell. Define Lbad=0 when J=0. Comparing all\nmatched and mismatched factors gives a total additional cost at most\n\n    Cbad=(kappa*c)^Lbad.                                (25)\n\nThis proof does not use the magnitude or sign of any split change; it\nallows the two odd fractions to be arbitrary distinct points of[0,1].\n\n## 5. Root and terminal costs, with no forgotten minimizer change\n\nFor the first pair, set LOCAL u=theta_2. The root endpoint against vertex2\ngives qE_0>=E_1+u(u+1)E_2.\nThe high phase's zero comparison gives\nQ E_1>=(1-u)(1+uQ)E_2, Q=cq.\nConsequently\n\n    E_2/E_0<=qQ/[1-u+2Qu]<=qQ.                           (16)\n\nThere are N/4 level-one highs in the whole word. The initial one uses\n(16). Matched internal level-one pairs pay kappa, other matched pairs\npay at least1, and(23)-(25) account for every mismatched pair. Counting\nthe exact ruler levels therefore yields\n\n    E_(N-2)/E_0 <= Cbad*(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/\n                                  [q kappa^(N/4-1)].    (17)\n\nNow retain ALL original queried jets and the optimum(0,0,0), and let f\nbe ANY convex1-smooth terminal completion, as in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nWhen r>=1, every original p_i is actually queried. Interpolation to a\nnew minimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0 from its queried value\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. Their triangular inequalities force all coordinates\nof z nonpositive;0 remains the true closest minimizer, with distance R_0.\n\nThe last step is h_last=1+a. Smoothness at that actual retained query gives\n\n    2f(x_n)/E_0 <=\n     [2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0)\n                     <2E_(N-1)/(MtE_0),\n\nbecause M>=2+a gives 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nThe last low phase has incoming fraction v=theta_(N-1) and obeys\n\n    qE_(N-2)>=(1+vq)[1-v+A/t]E_(N-1).\n\nThus E_(N-1)/t<=qE_(N-2)/A. Put K=1/(2M_m-1) and use M_m<=Mc^k\nwith(17). This proves\n\n    score_free/K <(4/A)*Cbad*(qc)^(N-1)/kappa^(N/4-1).          (18)\n\nWhen r=0, the old terminal normal is unqueried and must not constrain the\nnew minimum. The retained initial gradient instead gives\ndist(x_init,argmin f)^2>=E_0/A^2.\nAt the last query the position is\nR_i e_i+v R_(N-1)e_(N-1) and its gradient is\n[R_i e_i-(1-v)R_(N-1)e_(N-1)]/A.\nOne smoothness step bounds2f(x_n) by\n\n (1-A^(-2))E_i\n +[-2v(1-v)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-v)^2]E_(N-1)\n       <=(1-A^(-2))E_i.\n\nThe bracket is nonpositive because A<23/16.\nCombining the TRUE distance lower bound and(17) gives\n\n    score_free/K <2(2+q)*Cbad*(qc)^(N-1)/kappa^(N/4-1).         (19)\n\nA uniform constant5 bounds both prefactors. These are necessary bounds\non every full completion, not assertions that the smoothness cap is attained.\n\n## 6. Ruler-level counting and a uniform exact-profile loss\n\nThe number of high pairs whose level is at least s is at most N/2^s.\nConsequently\n\n    Lbad <= sum_(s>=1) min(J,N/2^s).\n\nIf J<=N/4096, split this sum after s=12 to get\n\n    Lbad<=12J+N/4096<=13N/4096<=7N/2048.                 (26)\n\nFor m>=512, the exact mass formula from Section3 gives\n\n qc<=c(c-2)\n    =1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2<1+8/m<=65/64.\n\nUse (qc)^(N-1)<=(65/64)^N, which is valid even if qc<1. Combining\n(18)-(19), the uniform prefactor5, and(25), we obtain\n\n score_free/K <5*kappa*exp[\n      N log(65/64)-(N/4)log kappa+Lbad log(kappa*c)].\n\nThe elementary bounds log(65/64)<=1/64 and log kappa>=1/9 follow from\nlog(1+x)<=x and log(1+x)>=x/(1+x). Also kappa*c<351/128 and\nexp(9/8)>1+9/8+(9/8)^2/2+(9/8)^3/6>351/128, so\nlog(kappa*c)<9/8. By(26), the exponent is at most\n\n    N[1/64-1/36+63/16384] < -N/128.\n\nHence the quantitative whole-family bound is\n\n    score_free/K <(45/8)exp(-N/128).                      (27)\n\nFor N>=512, exp(N/128)>=exp(4)>16, so\n\n    score_free/K <45/128<1.                              (28)\n\nThe comparison with the ACTUAL benchmark follows directly from its\naccepted recursions. Write rho_s=log_2(1+sqrt(2)). For\nF(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1, the second derivative of log F(exp w) is\n\n rho_s*z*(1+z)^(rho_s-2)*\n [(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s*z]/F(z)^2>0.\n\nConvexity at w and -w gives F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2. Substituting\nz=Y^(1/rho_s)/X^(1/rho_s) and comparing the positive root of\nt^2-(X+Y)t-XY proves\n\n    Omega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\n\nThe zero cases are limits. Induction in accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives\n1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s. The dominant join is at most4X+D,\nso the dominant recursion and superadditivity of x^rho_s give\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^rho_s.\n\nThe mass formula in Section3, with c=lambda(1+1/m), implies\nM_m/lambda^m->exp(1). Hence limsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1).\nFor every family in the stated range m>=512, N>=512 and J<=N/4096,\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free <=45/[64 exp(1)]<1.        (29)\n\nIf N grows, (27) makes this limit superior zero. In particular ANY\nsequence of split policies with J=o(N) is eventually covered and has\nvanishing normalized benchmark payoff. A policy with a positive density\nof odd-split changes is therefore necessary to escape this obstruction\nwhen the number of phases tends to infinity. This is only a necessary\ncondition within this specified two-predecessor family.\n\n## 7. Scope and status\n\nAll coordinates, all incoming fractions and all energies are real. Every\nold and future normal is retained in the exact legality test(1)-(3).\nThe same fixed simplex realizes all original physical steps; all queried\nvalues, initial closest-minimizer cost, terminal value and primitive\ndefect are explicit. Bounds(27)-(29) also cover arbitrary convex1-smooth\nterminal completions retaining every query and the optimum, even when the\nold final normal is unqueried and cannot constrain the new minimizer.\n\nThe two endpoints of each mismatched pair may differ by any amount.\nEvery even split is completely unrestricted. The only combinatorial\nrestriction for the explicit small bound is J<=N/4096; (23)-(25) and\n(18)-(19) still give a proved necessary bound without it.\n\nThis does not exclude a positive density of varying odd splits, longer\nforward memory, signed transfers, or revisiting an exhausted coordinate.\nIt is not an unrestricted W upper bound or a solution of the original\nall-schedule/all-horizon theorem. No earlier candidate is used as a\ncorrectness premise: all its needed calculations are reproduced here.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["40984c8e6ab5abcc", "8a4c55a6de4727ae"]}, "glossary": {"theta_j": "Fraction of coordinate-j mass transferred by predecessor j-2; its other predecessor j-1 transfers the remaining fraction, with the root convention displayed.", "E_i": "Squared coordinate mass R_i^2 in the explicit real flow.", "N": "Number 2^(m-r) of copies of H_r used as phases.", "M": "One plus the total physical step mass of H_r.", "q": "Low checkpoint excess a c^r divided by M.", "K": "Canonical Huber score 1/(2M_m-1), where M_m=1+sum H_m.", "J": "Number of internal low/high pairs whose two neighboring odd-coordinate fractions differ.", "Lbad": "Sum of the ruler levels nu_2(i+2) over those mismatched internal pairs.", "Cbad": "Explicit multiplicative variation penalty (kappa c)^Lbad, kappa=9/8.", "score_free": "2f(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 for any normalized convex 1-smooth completion retaining every original queried jet and the optimum."}}
{"id": "e08ee0674ce368d3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:02:08.892003+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Critical-scalar closure on the ACTUAL near-critical ruler, with uniform constants and genuine within-block derivative variation. (A) Arbitrarily asymmetric/nonperiodic subpower rays with a common reciprocal-checkpoint core mu=1/d_r, p_grow<=3/4 and kappa_ray C_grow<=23/10 have EXACT worst objective 1/(2S_m+d_r): every crossing run reaches0. (B) With arbitrary nonresonant 0<mu<=1/d_2, p_grow<=2/3 and kappa_ray C_grow<=7/4, the entire class has objective<=K=1/(2S_m+1), and supremum>=1/(2S_m+d_2), so it is asymptotically Huber-tight. Both classes have exact-B limsup<=2/e<1. An explicit horizon-knotted asymmetric square-root chord family is included. This does not cover all scalar derivatives or unrestricted W; all results are unverified candidates.", "evidence": "# Critical ruler: fixed asymmetric derivatives with within-block variation\n\nStatus: complete mathematical CANDIDATE, not verifier-accepted. No numerical\nsolver or fact_submit is allocated to this round. All new ingredients are\nproved together below. Earlier C23/C24/C26 concern a different ruler and are\nmotivation, not premises. The only accepted input is the exact benchmark\nrecurrence in fact b98bb807d514e80d. This does not upper-bound unrestricted W.\n\n## 1. The actual schedule and two whole-family statements\n\nFix the actual horizon parameter m>=128. Hold\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, K=1/(2S_m+1)\n\nfixed throughout the run. Set r_s=c-2 and A_mass=a/r_s. Direct summation gives\n\n    S_j+1=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                 (1)\n\nFor a number p_grow in[1/2,1), put beta_grow=1-p_grow and\n\n    C_grow=(p_grow/beta_grow)^p_grow.\n\nChoose two locally absolutely continuous profiles G_+,G_- on[0,infinity),\nequal to t on[0,1], with\n\n    0<=t G_sigma'(t)<=p_grow G_sigma(t) a.e. for t>1,\n    1/kappa_ray<=G_+(t)/G_-(t)<=kappa_ray for t>=1.    (2)\n\nHere kappa_ray>=1 and sigma denotes either sign. For any core radius R>0\nand curvature mu in(0,1], define ONE FIXED derivative and its objective by\n\n    g(sigma R t)=sigma mu R G_sigma(t),\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(s) ds.                       (3)\n\nAll parameters and the entire profiles may depend on m; they cannot change\nduring GD. These classes allow unbounded radial secant variation, asymmetry,\nand arbitrarily many nonperiodic knots. Ordinary ruler subblocks need not\nhave constant gradients. Initial points are arbitrary and are not saturated.\n\nStatement A, reciprocal cores. Suppose\n\n    1/2<=p_grow<=3/4, kappa_ray C_grow<=23/10,\n    mu=1/d_r for ANY integer r>=0.                  (4)\n\nEvery trajectory on H_m which strictly crosses zero hits zero subsequently.\nThe exact supremum, over the profiles, radius and nonzero initial point, is\n\n    sup 2f(x_n)/x_0^2=1/(2S_m+d_r).                 (5)\n\nThis is attained by a symmetric Huber derivative. For r>=m there is no\nstrict crossing, and the same conclusion holds. In particular the maximum\nover r in this class is 1/(2S_m+d_0)<K.\n\nStatement B, arbitrary small curvatures. Suppose instead\n\n    1/2<=p_grow<=2/3, kappa_ray C_grow<=7/4,\n    0<mu<=1/d_2.                                   (6)\n\nNo resonance or reciprocal-curvature condition is imposed. Every trajectory\nobeys\n\n    2f(x_n)/x_0^2<=K.                              (7)\n\nThe supremum over this entire class lies between 1/(2S_m+d_2) and K.\nSince d_2 is bounded uniformly in m, its ratio to K tends to one.\n\nA concrete scalable piecewise-linear subclass in (6) can be fixed before\nany query as follows. Put T=4^m. On each ray independently partition[1,T]\nby arbitrary knots whose successive ratios are at most4, and interpolate\nthe values sqrt(t) linearly. Beyond T keep the value sqrt(T) constant;\ninside[0,1] use t. The two partitions may differ and may have any number\nof additional horizon-dependent knots. For example, one partition has\nknots4^k, and the other has1,2,8,...,2*4^(m-1),4^m. Any segment with\nendpoint ratio q<=4 has maximal elasticity sqrt(q)/(1+sqrt(q))<=2/3.\nOn[1,T] its chord is between(2sqrt(2)/3)sqrt(t) and sqrt(t): the minimum\nchord-to-square-root ratio is2q^(1/4)/(1+sqrt(q)), minimized at q=4.\nThus the two complete profiles are comparable with\nkappa_ray=3/(2sqrt(2)), including their common outer plateau. Their\nproduct kappa_ray C_grow=3/2^(5/6)<7/4, verified by raising to the\nsixth power:729*128<117649. Taking, for instance, mu=1/(2d_2) and R=1\ngives explicit fixed functions with checked spatial order, slopes and\ntrapezoidal areas. The theorem covers every initial point for these\nfunctions, rather than choosing derivative pieces afresh at queries.\n\nFor BOTH classes the exact benchmark from b98bb807d514e80d satisfies\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1) sup 2f(x_n)/x_0^2<=2/e<1.       (8)\n\nThus these specified whole scalar mechanisms cannot reach the required\nbenchmark on the near-critical diagnostic. This is not an all-scalar bound.\n\n## 2. Legality, areas, and uniform schedule estimates\n\nIntegrating (2) gives 1<=G_sigma(t)<=t^p_grow<=t for t>=1. Also\n0<=G_sigma'(t)<=p_grow t^(p_grow-1)<=1. The unit core and continuity at1\ntherefore make each profile nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. Across the origin,\nG_+(t)+G_-(u)<=t+u proves the same bound. Hence (3) is nondecreasing,\nmu-Lipschitz, and continuous. Its integral is convex, differentiable and\n1-smooth, with unique minimizer0. Its complete values, including all outer\nareas, are\n\n    f(sigma R t)=mu R^2 F_sigma(t),\n    F_sigma(t)=t^2/2                           for t<=1,\n    F_sigma(t)=1/2+integral_1^t G_sigma(v)dv     for t>=1. (9)\n\nConsequently0 is the actual closest minimizer, and the normalization in (5)\nand (7) is precisely the original squared distance x_0^2.\n\nThe following rational bounds hold for every m>=128:\n\n    12/5<c<49/20, c<56/23,\n    2/5<a<1/lambda<5/12,\n    127/128<=ac<1, 0<a<r_s<1/2, 0<A_mass<1.       (10)\n\nFor c<56/23, use sqrt(2)<99/70, so\nc<(169/70)(129/128)=21801/8960<56/23. The other c bound follows too.\nSince a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda], a<1/lambda=lambda-2<r_s. Its lower bound\nfollows from a>=(127/128)/(49/20)=635/1568>2/5. Equation (1) implies\n\n    S_j+1<=c^j,\n    d_(j+1)/d_j=c-(c-1)/d_j<c.                    (11)\n\nWe will also need\n\n    S_j>= (7/4)d_j for every j>=3.                (12)\n\nIndeed S_j-(7/4)d_j equals\na(1/r_s-7/4)c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j-11/4, which increases with j.\nFor j=3, S_3=7+a(4+2c+c^2)>1603/125, whereas\nd_3=1+(ac)c^2<2801/400. The inequality\n1603/125>(7/4)(2801/400) proves (12).\n\n## 3. A complete crossing-clock inequality\n\nAbbreviate p=p_grow, beta=1-p and C=C_grow. On either ray define\n\n    Clock_sigma(t)=integral_1^t dv/G_sigma(v), t>0.\n\nIt is log t in the core. Outside the core put q_t=t/G_sigma(t).\nThe derivative condition gives q_t'>=beta/G_sigma(t), and hence\n\n    Clock_sigma(t)<=(q_t-1)/beta, t>=1.            (13)\n\nFor every h>1 and every strict overshoot z=hG_sigma(t)-t>0,\n\n    Clock_sigma(z)<= C h-1/beta.                  (14)\n\nHere h is a normalized stepsize mu d_j, not the physical stepsize d_j.\nWe prove all cases, including short crossing steps and core endpoints.\n\nIf z<=1, the bound G_sigma(t)<=t^p holds for every t>=0, also inside the\ncore. Maximizing h t^p-t by differentiation gives\n\n    z<=(beta C h)^(1/beta).\n\nThus log z<=log(beta C h)/beta<=C h-1/beta.\n\nIf z>=1 and t<=1, then h>=2, and monotonicity gives\nClock_sigma(z)<=z-1<=h-2. This is <=C h-1/beta because\nC>=1/(2beta). To prove the latter, the function\np log p+beta log beta is increasing for p>=1/2 and equals-log2 at1/2;\ntherefore beta C=p^p beta^beta>=1/2.\n\nIf z,t>=1 and z>=t, monotonicity and (13) yield\n\n    Clock_sigma(z)<=(q_t-1)/beta+(z-t)/G_sigma(t)\n                   =h+(1/beta-2)q_t-1/beta\n                   <=h/(2beta)-1/beta<=C h-1/beta,\n\nwhere q_t<=h/2 and p>=1/2 were used.\n\nFinally, if1<=z<=t, integrating (2) between z and t gives\nG_sigma(z)>=G_sigma(t)(z/t)^p. Therefore\n\n    z/G_sigma(z)<=q_t^p(h-q_t)^beta\n                  <=p^p beta^beta h=beta C h.\n\nThe maximum in the last inequality occurs at q_t=p h; this follows by\ndifferentiating p log q+beta log(h-q). Equation (13) proves (14).\n\nWrite D=C h-1/beta. If a crossing lands on the other ray, its new clock\nis at most kappa_ray D when D>=0: use the pointwise profile comparison\nand integrate if z>=1; for z<1 the new clock is negative. If D<0,\n(14) forces z<1, and BOTH ray clocks equal log z, so the new clock is\nat most D, with no comparison-factor loss. A noncrossing nonzero step\nreduces its own clock by at least h, since\n\n    integral_(t-hG_sigma(t))^t dv/G_sigma(v)>=h.\n\nWe also have Clock_sigma(t)<=t-1 for all t>0, using G_sigma>=1 outside\nthe core and log t<=t-1 inside it.\n\n## 4. Reciprocal cores: every crossing is annihilated\n\nAssume (4), and put sigma_clock=kappa_ray C and\ntau_clock=kappa_ray/beta. The function C=(p/(1-p))^p increases for\np>=1/2, so C<=3^(3/4)<23/10. In particular the class is nonempty\nthroughout the asserted p range. Also (10) gives\n\n    sigma_clock<=23/10<1/r_s.\n\nSince p/beta>=1 and p<=1, C<=(p/beta), so\n\n    tau_clock-sigma_clock=kappa_ray(1/beta-C)>=1.  (15)\n\nIf a trajectory ever reaches zero, it stays there. Otherwise choose its\nlast strict crossing. A crossing requires h=mu d_j>1 because G_sigma(t)<=t.\nThus its level j is greater than r. Every occurrence of d_j in the ruler\nis immediately followed by a complete H_j. This copy of H_j contains a\nlast occurrence of d_r, followed by H_r. The physical mass before that\nlast d_r, measured from the start of this copy of H_j, is\n\n    S_j-S_r-d_r.                                 (16)\n\nSuppose the overshoot radius z is greater than1. Section3 bounds its\ntarget clock by sigma_clock d_j/d_r-tau_clock. Define\nD_j=S_j-sigma_clock d_j. By (1),\n\n    D_j=a(1/r_s-sigma_clock)c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j-1-sigma_clock\n\nis increasing. Consequently\n\n    S_j-S_r-d_r-(sigma_clock d_j-tau_clock d_r)\n      =D_j-D_r+(tau_clock-sigma_clock-1)d_r>=0.    (17)\n\nThere is no subsequent crossing by our choice of the last one. Each\nsubsequent nonzero step decreases the target clock by its normalized\nstepsize. Equations (16)-(17) force the path into the core by the last\nd_r in that copy of H_j. If z<=1 it is already in the core; a\nnoncrossing core step keeps it there. The last d_r then maps x to\nx-d_r mu x=0. This proves the annihilation assertion without assuming\nthat an arbitrary earlier core entry is permanent.\n\nFor completeness, a run with no crossing, on either ray, has final\nradius v and gradient magnitude u. Monotonicity gives\n|x_0|>=v+S_m u. Since the actual derivative is mu-Lipschitz,\n\n    f(x_n)<=u v-u^2/(2mu).\n\nThis follows by integrating the bound |g(t)|<=min(mu|t|,u) from0 to\nthe final point. Therefore\n\n    2f(x_n)<=2|x_0|u-(2S_m+1/mu)u^2\n             <=x_0^2/(2S_m+1/mu).                 (18)\n\nFor G_+=G_-=min(t,1), start at x_0=R(1+2mu S_m). Every gradient on\nthe trajectory equals mu R, all iterates remain beyond the core, and\nx_n=R(1+mu S_m). Substitution into (9) gives equality in (18).\nTogether with annihilation this proves (5), including r>=m.\n\n## 5. Nonresonant cores: a uniform actual-map attraction bound\n\nAssume (6), and abbreviate\n\n    b_j=mu d_j, U_j=mu S_j, eta=7/4,\n    rho_core=1/8, k_env=1/8, A_env=c/8.\n\nLet I be the first level with t=b_I>1 and put J=I+1. Because mu<=1/d_2,\nI>=3. Also1<t<c by (11). If the horizon m<=I, all its normalized\nsteps are<=1, so no crossing occurs and (18) already proves (7).\n\nFirst, ANY crossing run on ANY whole H_j ends at radius less than\nrho_core in normalized coordinates x/R. Select its last strict crossing,\nat some level l>=I>=3, with h=b_l>1. The following full H_l has\nnormalized mass U_l>=eta h, by (12). Use Section3 with D=C h-1/beta.\n\nIf D>=0, the terminal clock is bounded by\n(kappa_ray C-eta)h-kappa_ray/beta. This increases with kappa_ray\nat fixed h, so under kappa_ray C<=eta it is at most\n\n    -eta/(beta C)<-105/32.\n\nTo justify the strict constant, beta C=p^p beta^beta increases on\n[1/2,2/3] and is at most4^(1/3)/3<8/15. If D<0, the terminal clock\nis at most(C-eta)h-1/beta<=C-eta-1/beta<=-eta-1=-11/4,\nusing C<=p/beta and C<=eta. Since exp(11/4)>8, both cases give\n\n    |Phi_j(x/R)|<rho_core for every crossing run.  (19)\n\nHere Phi_j is the actual map of the SAME fixed function under H_j.\nA noncrossing, nonzero run starting at radius v instead satisfies\n\n    Clock(radius_end)<=v-1-U_j.                  (20)\n\nWe now prove the actual-map invariant, for j>=J and0<=q<=rho_core,\n\n    |x/R|<=q b_j implies\n    |Phi_j(x/R)|<=min(A_env q,k_env rho_core).     (21)\n\nThis controls both possible signs without assuming an odd map or\nasserting that a reachable image is a centered interval.\n\nBase j=J. All steps of H_I are at most1, so a core-started run stays\nin the quadratic core. Its multiplier P is nonnegative and\nP<=exp(-U_I)<=exp(-eta t). The middle step t has radius factor t-1,\nand (t-1)P<(c-1)exp(-eta)<1. Thus a core-started whole H_J stays\nin the core and has absolute multiplier Q=(t-1)P^2. Furthermore\n\n    b_J Q<=c t(t-1)exp(-2eta t)\n           <=3c/(16eta^3)<=3/35<1/8.             (22)\n\nFor the middle inequality use exp(2eta t)>(2eta t)^3/6 and\n(t-1)/t^2<=1/4. Also rho_core b_J<c^2/8<1. Thus every input in\n(21) is a core input, and its endpoint is at most k_env q, which\nimplies both bounds in (21).\n\nInduction j>J. Write the actual word as H_(j-1),d_(j-1),H_(j-1),\nand let v_1 be the radius after its first block.\n\nIf c q<=rho_core, the induction hypothesis applies to the first\nblock because b_j<=c b_(j-1). It gives\nv_1<=min(A_env c q,k_env rho_core)<=q, since A_env c=c^2/8<1.\nThus v_1<=rho_core. The middle step uses the quadratic core and\nhas output radius |1-b_(j-1)|v_1<=b_(j-1)v_1. The second block,\nusing parameter v_1 in the induction hypothesis, gives\nradius_end<=min(A_env q,k_env rho_core).\n\nIf c q>rho_core, then v_1<=rho_core as well. A crossing first block\nuses (19). A noncrossing first block uses (20) and (12):\n\n    Clock(v_1)<=rho_core b_j-1-U_(j-1)\n               <=-(eta/c-rho_core)b_j-1<-3.       (23)\n\nFor the last inequality, eta/c>5/7. Also j>=I+2 and d_I>=d_3>6,\nso\n\n    b_j>=b_(I+2)>d_(I+2)/d_I\n        =c^2-(c^2-1)/d_I\n        >(5c^2+1)/6>5,                            (24)\n\nwhere c^2>lambda^2>29/5. Therefore the right side of (23) is less\nthan-1-5(5/7-1/8)=-221/56<-3. Since exp3>8, (23) implies the\ndesired v_1<rho_core. A zero iterate is harmless. The middle step\nnow has radius at most b_(j-1)rho_core, so the second induction\napplication gives radius_end<=k_env rho_core. Because q>rho_core/c\nand A_env=c k_env, this also is at most A_env q. The induction is\ncomplete. Notice that it explicitly handles partial-block entries and\nsubsequent exits; first entry is never presumed permanent capture.\n\n## 6. Nonresonant full objective, with original distance retained\n\nNo-crossing runs obey (18), and hence have normalized gap<=K.\nFor a crossing run with m>=J, (19) places the endpoint in the core.\nIf its initial normalized radius v_0 is at most rho_core b_m,\n(21) gives radius_end<=A_env v_0/b_m. If v_0>rho_core b_m,\n(19) gives radius_end<rho_core. In either case, using the complete\ncore area in (9),\n\n    2f(x_n)/x_0^2<=mu/b_m^2.                     (25)\n\nFor m>=I+2, (24), a>2/5 and (11) yield\n\n    b_m d_m>5a c^m>2c^m>2S_m+1.\n\nSince mu/b_m^2=1/(b_m d_m), (25) is strictly below K.\n\nIt remains to check the base horizon m=J=I+1, where (25) alone\nwould discard useful information. If v_0<=1, the entire run stays\nin the quadratic core by the base argument, so its normalized gap\nis mu Q^2<mu/64. If v_0>1 and the run crosses, (19) gives the same\nstrict bound mu/64. Finally\n\n    mu(2S_m+1)<2mu c^m<2b_m/a<2c^2/a<125/4<64.\n\nHere b_m=b_J<c^2, c<5/2 and a>2/5 were used. Thus mu/64<K.\nThis proves (7) for all cases. The lower end of the asserted squeeze\nis the Huber construction in Section4 with mu=1/d_2. It belongs to\n(6), and has normalized payoff1/(2S_m+d_2).\n\n## 7. Exact benchmark comparison\n\nWe use ONLY the exact recursions in accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d.\nWrite C_n=S_circle(n)+1.
