Their mass joins are\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gamma(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2,\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Gamma(C_k,B_l).\n\nPut rho=log_2(lambda). We give the needed sharper estimates directly:\n\n    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                (26)\n\nLet E(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. With t=exp z, differentiation gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log E(exp z)\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/E(t)^2>0.\n\nThe strict inequality follows by differentiating (1+t)^rho-1-rho t.\nConvexity at z,-z yields E(t)E(1/t)>=E(1)^2=2. For A=t^rho B\nand D=(1+t)^rho B this means (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, so\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.\n\nInduction proves the first assertion of (26). The inequality\nGamma(A,D)<=4A+D follows by squaring sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A.\nInduction using (k+1)^rho+l^rho<=(k+l+1)^rho proves the second.\n\nFinally A_mass tends to1, (1+1/m)^m tends to e, and (2/c)^m tends to0.\nEquation (1) gives (S_m+1)/lambda^m ->e. Hence the ACTUAL finite\nbenchmark, without any fitted dyadic constant or phase assumption, obeys\n\n    K B_(2^m-1)\n      <=[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2S_m+1) ->2/e.          (27)\n\nBoth (5) and (7) imply (8).\n\n## 8. Scope and the next real gap\n\nNo external theorem was used as a black box. Exact scalar-order/area\nconstructions and the earlier fixed13/5 clock arguments informed this\nchoice of family, but every estimate on the actual near-critical word\nhas been derived here, with uniform constants.\n\nThe restrictions are substantive: a common quadratic neighborhood;\nouter logarithmic slope at most3/4 in the reciprocal case, or2/3 in\nthe small-curvature nonresonant case; a specified ray-comparison bound;\nand, for the latter case, mu<=1/d_2. General nonresonant curvatures\nabove1/d_2, independent core curvatures, outer ramps with logarithmic\nslope near or above1, or profiles without a common quadratic core are\nnot covered. No upper bound on all scalar functions, no unrestricted\nW_n upper bound, and no completion of the project target is asserted.\n\nThere is an exact countertest against silently removing resonance from\nthe annihilation conclusion. Take mu=1, G_+=G_-=min(t,1), R=1, and\nepsilon=[2 product_(h in H_m)(1+h)]^(-1)>0 as the initial point.\nEvery prefix radius is below1/2, by induction using the bound1+h for\neach update, so the whole trajectory uses g(x)=x. All h=d_j exceed1;\ntherefore every update strictly crosses zero and none reaches it.\nIts final normalized objective is the strictly positive quantity\n\n    product_(h in H_m)(h-1)^2\n      =(ac)^(2(2^m-1)) c^(-2m).\n\nFor the last identity, the multiplicity of d_j is2^(m-j-1), the total\nis n=2^m-1, and the multiplicity-weighted sum of j is n-m (induction\nin m verifies this identity). This refutes the extended annihilation\nclaim, not an objective bound at K. Also S_1/d_1<3/4 and\nS_2/d_2<3/2 on the actual parameter range, so the uniform7/4 waiting\nbudget used in Section5 cannot be imported to the first three levels.\nThe latter strict inequality follows from\nS_2-(3/2)d_2=3/2+a(2+c-(3/2)c^2)<0 using a>2/5,c>12/5.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "plan_id": "bad41398ca074ef2", "artifact": "research/max_critical_scalar_subpower_closure.md", "status": "unverified; no fact_submit allocated"}, "glossary": {"lambda": "The silver ratio 1+sqrt(2).", "c_m": "Near-critical ruler ratio lambda(1+1/m), held fixed for the horizon.", "a_m": "Near-critical coefficient (1-1/m)/c_m, held fixed for the horizon.", "d_j": "Checkpoint step 1+a_m c_m^j in the current finding.", "S_j": "Total mass of the j-level actual near-critical ruler, with the horizon parameters held fixed.", "r_s": "The positive scalar c_m-2, used only in this proof.", "A_mass": "The scalar a_m/(c_m-2) in the exact ruler sum formula.", "G_sigma": "Dimensionless radial derivative on sign sigma; common unit quadratic core and outer elasticity bound.", "p_grow": "Upper bound on t G_sigma'(t)/G_sigma(t) outside the unit core.", "beta_grow": "The positive complement 1-p_grow.", "C_grow": "The crossing-clock factor (p_grow/(1-p_grow))^p_grow.", "kappa_ray": "Uniform multiplicative comparison bound between the two ray derivatives.", "R": "Positive common physical core radius.", "mu": "Common physical curvature of the quadratic core.", "Clock_sigma": "Integral from1 to t of1/G_sigma on one ray, equal to log t in the unit core.", "Phi_j": "Actual fixed-function normalized GD map for the full j-level ruler.", "rho_core": "The fixed normalized radius1/8 in the attraction proof.", "k_env": "The fixed contraction coefficient1/8 in the attraction proof.", "A_env": "The scalar c_m/8 in the actual-map attraction invariant."}}
{"id": "8e8cc2d8c1739221", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:04:03.286328+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C43 closes the ENTIRE real nonnegative two-predecessor merge family, with every split fraction independent and the terminal completion free. A positive boundary energy retaining its signed future-coordinate term contracts by qQ/(1+q) across every high/low pair. On the actual near-critical ruler, m>=512 and N>=32 phases imply score_free/K<21(3/4)^(N/2)<7/27 and limsup B_n score_free<=14/(27e)<1; growing N gives zero. Frequent odd-split changes do not escape. This is a full-class candidate obstruction, still unverified and not an unrestricted W upper bound.", "evidence": "# C43. Every nonnegative two-predecessor merge loses the benchmark\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No fact_submit allocation, numerical\nsolver or sweep. All needed arguments are written below; earlier candidates\nare not correctness premises. The original exact-profile target remains open.\n\nFor the near-critical word and every legal member of the entire real\ntwo-predecessor family below, including EVERY independent split fraction,\nthe normalized objective of ANY full terminal completion obeys\n\n    score_free/K <21(3/4)^(N/2),                         (T1)\n\nprovided m>=512 and N=2^(m-r)>=32. In particular it is below7/27.\nConsequently limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=14/(27e)<1, and it tends\nto zero if N tends to infinity. The actual GD, all old/future normals,\ntrue initial distance and primitive defect are included.\n\n## 1. The word, real flow and complete legality test\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), n=2^m-1.\nFix 0<=r<=m-5, let k=m-r, N=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, and q=a c^r/M.\nWrite\n\n    A=1+q,\n    q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n    tau_(N-1)=t>=1, T_(N-1)=Mt.\n\nHere nu_2 is the binary valuation of a positive integer. These N phases\nare the actual copies of H_r in H_m, and every intervening physical\ncheckpoint is 1+Mq_i. Each phase's ordinary mass is M-1.\n\nChoose ALL theta_j in[0,1] independently for 2<=j<N, positive R_i,\nE_i=R_i^2, and actual nonnegative transfers\n\n    a_01=R_1, a_02=theta_2 R_2,\n    a_(i,i+1)=(1-theta_(i+1))R_(i+1), i>=1,\n    a_(i,i+2)=theta_(i+2)R_(i+2), i>=1,\n\nomitting out-of-range indices. Every coordinate j>=2 receives total\nmass R_j from its two preceding coordinates. In the real Euclidean\nspace R^N with standard orthonormal vectors e_i, set\n\n    p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i,\n    C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),\n    F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nThe maximizing point is the Euclidean projection P_C(x). The projection\nvariational inequality gives its uniqueness and\n||P_C(x)-P_C(y)||<=||x-y||. Maximizing-difference inequalities then give\ndifferentiability with grad F_C=P_C; thus F_C is convex and1-smooth.\n\nStart at R_0 e_0. Before every phase i>=1 the exact state is\n\n    X_i=R_i e_i+theta_(i+1)R_(i+1)e_(i+1),\n\nwith the last term omitted at i=N-1. Subtract each original physical\nstep times p_i throughout the ordinary block and its checkpoint.\nThe total subtraction before phase i+1 is T_i p_i, which exhausts\ncoordinate i and creates exactly X_(i+1). No physical step is compressed\nor reordered in asserting this trajectory.\n\nFor completeness, here are ALL normal tests. During phase i>=1 put\nz=(ordinary partial mass+1)/M in[1/M,1],\nd=theta_(i+1), e=theta_(i+2). Multiplying by M, the current normal value is\n\n [(tau_i-z)E_i\n  -(1-d)(d tau_i+z(1-d))E_(i+1)\n  -z e^2 E_(i+2)]/tau_i^2.                               (1)\n\nThe only possibly nonzero future values are\n\n [(d+z(1-d)/tau_i)E_(i+1)\n               -z e(1-e)E_(i+2)/tau_i]/tau_(i+1),        (2)\n z e E_(i+2)/(tau_i tau_(i+2)).                           (3)\n\nEvery past normal is nonpositive: its positive coordinate has been\nexhausted, while the remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nEvery other future value is zero. Therefore (1)>=(2),(3),0 at BOTH\nz=1/M and z=1 is necessary and sufficient for every actual query in\nthis phase, since all differences are affine in z. At the root use\nits displayed p_0 and test the same two endpoints against p_1,p_2,0;\nother future normals vanish and there are no past normals. At the last\nphase t>=1 gives its own nonnegative value and all past values are\nnonpositive. Out-of-range terms are always omitted.\n\nFor any fixed split policy and finite word the family is nonempty:\nfix terminal energy1, then choose E_i backward sufficiently large.\nIts own coefficient tau_i-z>=q_i>0, and all comparison terms involve\nalready fixed future energies. Finally choose E_0. The theorem covers\nevery legal energy choice, not merely this conservative construction.\n\nAt every original query,\nF_C(x)=<x,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. Its minimizer set is C^polar. The inequalities\n<p_i,z><=0 imply, by backward induction, that every coordinate of z is\nnonpositive. Hence the actual closest minimizer to R_0e_0 is0, with\nsquared initial distance E_0. If htot is the total physical mass of H_m,\nthe original terminal objective and primitive defect are exactly\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0),\n V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.            (4)\n\nThe second identity follows by summing\n2h_i F_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2\nand then subtracting the defining terminal term of V.\n\n## 2. Uniform parameter bounds\n\nPut M_j=1+sum H_j. The recursion M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j gives\n\n M_j=b c^j+(1-b)2^j,  b=a/(c-2),  0<b<1.\n\nSince a<c-2, a<=a c^j/M_j<c-2, and M_m<=M c^k.\nFor m>=512 we may use the coarser rational bounds\n\n    2/5<q<7/16,  12/5<c<39/16,  A<23/16,\n    Q:=q c^ell>=cq>=1-1/m>=99/100, ell>=1.              (5)\n\nFor instance lambda<169/70 and\na>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5 establish the lower q bound;\nthe upper c bound already holds for m>=128.\nIn addition\n\n qc<=c(c-2)\n    =1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2\n    <1+8/m<=65/64.                                      (6)\n\n## 3. A boundary energy retaining the shared coordinate\n\nIndex the ODD phases by j=0,...,J, where J=N/2-1.\nAt phase i=2j+1 write\n\n O_j=E_(2j+1), B_j=tau_(2j+1),\n u_j=theta_(2j+2), L_j=E_(2j+2)    (j<J).\n\nThus B_j=1+Q_j with Q_j=q c^(nu_2(2j+2)) for j<J,\nwhereas B_J=t is the unqueried-terminal parameter.\nDefine\n\n D_j=(A^2/B_j)O_j\n            +u_j[u_j-(1-u_j)A/B_j]L_j,  j<J,\n D_J=(A^2/t)O_J.                                        (7)\n\nThe second coefficient in D_j CAN BE NEGATIVE. It must not be discarded.\nWe prove D_j>0 and its quantitative comparison directly from the legal\nhigh phase.\n\nFor j<J abbreviate B=B_j, Q=Q_j, u=u_j, O=O_j, L=L_j, and let\n\n    H=(1+uQ)(1-u+B/A).                                   (8)\n\nThe high phase's zero comparison at z=1 gives\n\n    Q O >= (1-u)(1+uQ)L+w^2 O_(j+1)\n          >= (1-u)(1+uQ)L,                              (9)\n\nwhere w=theta_(2j+3). We claim\n\n    H D_j >= A O >0.                                     (10)\n\nIf the coefficient K_u=u[u-(1-u)A/B] in(7) is nonnegative,\nD_j>=A^2 O/B and H>=B/A prove(10).\nIf K_u<0, put d=1-u>0 and use(9) to upper-bound L. Multiplication\nby the negative K_u reverses this bound, giving\n\n D_j/O >= Z/[B d(1+uQ)],\n Z=A^2 d+Q u(Aq d+B u).\n\nThe exact identity\n\n Z(d+B/A)-ABd\n     =A^2 d^2+Q u(Aq d+B u)(d+B/A) >=0\n\nproves(10) after multiplying by H=(1+uQ)(d+B/A).\nThis includes u=0 or1 via the first case. Thus D_j is a positive energy\neven when its displayed residual coefficient is negative.\n\n## 4. Every high/low passage contracts the boundary energy\n\nFix j<J and abbreviate Bnext=B_(j+1), O'=O_(j+1).\nLet zeta=u_(j+1) and L'=L_(j+1) when j+1<J; omit their term at j+1=J.\nThe high phase's first future comparison is\n\n Q O >= H L\n       +w[w-(1-w)B/A]O'.                                (11)\n\nThe following low phase's first future comparison is\n\n q L >= (1+qw)(1-w+A/Bnext)O'\n       +zeta[zeta-(1-zeta)A/Bnext]L'.                    (12)\n\nBoth are exact instances of(1)>=(2), with every inherited mass present.\nMultiply(11) by q and substitute(12), since H>0.\nThe coefficient of O' becomes\n\n C(w)=H(1+qw)(1-w+A/Bnext)\n                       +q w[w-(1-w)B/A].               (13)\n\nWe show C(w)>=H A^2/Bnext+q for EVERY independent w in[0,1].\nFirst,\n\n H-(1+B/A)=u[Q(1-u+B/A)-1]>=0,\n\nbecause Q B/A>1 follows from(5). Consequently C has nonpositive\nquadratic coefficient q[(1+B/A)-H], so it is concave in w.\nIts smaller endpoint is w=1:\n\n C(0)-C(1)=H(1-Aq/Bnext)-q>0.\n\nIndeed Bnext>=1, Aq<161/256, H>=1+B/A>2, and\n2(1-161/256)-7/16=78/256>0. This proves the assertion.\nEquations(7),(11)-(13) therefore imply\n\n    qQ O >= H D_(j+1)+q O_(j+1).                         (14)\n\nCombining(14) with(10) and dropping only the positive q O_(j+1) gives\n\n    D_(j+1) <=(q Q_j/A) D_j.                             (15)\n\nThere is NO relation required between w and any earlier odd split.\nEvery even split is also independent. The positive energy(7), rather\nthan pointwise elimination of L', is what makes all those changes harmless.\n\nAt the root, its first-future endpoint normal gives\n\n q E_0 >=(1+A/B_0)O_0+u_0[u_0-(1-u_0)A/B_0]L_0\n         >=D_0,                                         (16)\n\nbecause B_0>Aq. Iterating(15) is legitimate by(10), and at the last\nboundary D_J=A^2 E_(N-1)/t. The number of factors Q_j is N/2-1;\ntheir high levels sum to N-k-1, as follows from the exact ruler counts\n2^(k-ell-1) of each level ell=1,...,k-1. Hence\n\n D_J/E_0 <= q^(N-1)c^(N-k-1)/A^(N/2-1),\n\n    E_(N-1)/(tE_0)\n        <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/A^(N/2+1).                   (17)\n\n## 5. Free terminal completion and the true initial distance\n\nRetain EVERY original queried jet and the optimum(0,0,0), and consider\nANY convex1-smooth terminal completion f. This includes the maximal\ncompletion from accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nIf r>=1, every p_i is actually queried. Interpolation of that jet to\nany new minimizer z yields <p_i,z><=0, because its retained value is\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The triangular inequalities imply that all\ncoordinates of z are nonpositive. Since0 remains a minimizer, its\ndistance R_0 remains the TRUE closest-minimizer distance. Additional\northogonal dimensions cannot decrease it.\n\nThe last physical step is h_last=1+a. Smoothness at its retained query\nbounds the terminal objective by\n\n 2f(x_n)/E_0\n <=[2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0)\n <2E_(N-1)/(MtE_0),\n\nsince M>=2+a gives 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nSet K=1/(2M_m-1); use M_m<=Mc^k and(17) to obtain\n\n    score_free/K <4(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2+1).                 (18)\n\nIf r=0, the old terminal normal is UNQUERIED and cannot constrain a\nnew minimizer. The retained initial gradient instead gives, by\n1-Lipschitz continuity from any minimizer,\n\n    dist(x_0,argmin f)^2>=||p_0||^2>=E_0/A^2.             (19)\n\nWrite Llast=E_(N-2) and v=theta_(N-1). At the last query the actual\nposition is R_(N-2)e_(N-2)+v R_(N-1)e_(N-1) and its gradient is\n\n    [R_(N-2)e_(N-2)-(1-v)R_(N-1)e_(N-1)]/A.\n\nOne smoothness step of length A gives\n\n 2f(x_n)<=(1-A^(-2))Llast\n    +[-2v(1-v)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-v)^2]E_(N-1)\n <=(1-A^(-2))Llast,                                     (20)\n\nbecause the bracket is nonpositive for A<23/16.\nWe now bound Llast through the PRECEDING, queried high boundary D_(J-1).\nIts parameters have Q=cq<=65/64 and B=1+Q. From(9) and(7), multiplying\n(9) by the positive A^2/(BQ), we get\n\n D_(J-1)/Llast\n >= A^2(1-u)/(BQ)+u[Aq(1-u)+Bu]/B\n >= [A^2/(BQ)](1-u)+u^2\n >=(3/4)(1-u)+u^2>=39/64>1/2.                            (21)\n\nHere A^2/(BQ)>(49/25)/(8385/4096)>3/4 by(5)-(6).\nUsing(15)-(16) but omitting the final high passage gives\n\n Llast/E_0\n <=2q^(N-3)c^(N-k-2)/A^(N/2-2).                         (22)\n\nCombining the ACTUAL distance bound(19), the objective bound(20),\n(22) and M_m<=c^k now yields\n\n    score_free/K\n      <4(2+q)A^2(qc)^(N-2)/A^(N/2).                    (23)\n\nThis second argument neither assumes the old terminal gradient is\nqueried nor uses it to restrict the new closest minimizer.\n\n## 6. Uniform quantitative bound and the actual exact benchmark\n\nLet z0=65/64>1. To cover qc<1 as well, use\n(qc)^(N-1)<=z0^N and (qc)^(N-2)<=z0^N.\nThe exact rational inequality\n\n    z0^2/A <(65/64)^2/(7/5)=21125/28672<3/4\n\nand the prefactor bounds 4/A<20/7<21 and\n4(2+q)A^2<4(39/16)(23/16)^2=82524/4096<21 show from\n(18),(23) that\n\n    score_free/K <21(3/4)^(N/2).\n\nFor N>=32, (3/4)^4=81/256<1/3, hence this is strictly below\n21/81=7/27. For growing N the ratio tends to zero uniformly over every\nsplit fraction, energy, r, t and full terminal completion in the family.\n\nTo compare with the ORIGINAL benchmark, use accepted b98bb807d514e80d:\nits shifted joins are\nOmega(X,Y)=(X+Y+sqrt((X+Y)^2+4XY))/2 and\nGamma(X,D)=2X+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8XD)/2.\nWrite rho_s=log_2(lambda). For F(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1, the second derivative\nof log F(exp w) is\n\n rho_s*z*(1+z)^(rho_s-2)*\n [(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s*z]/F(z)^2>0.\n\nConvexity at w and-w gives F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2. Taking\nz=Y^(1/rho_s)/X^(1/rho_s) and comparing the positive root of\nt^2-(X+Y)t-XY proves\nOmega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\nThe zero cases follow by continuity. Induction in the accepted recursions\ntherefore gives 1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s.\nSince Gamma(X,D)<=4X+D, another induction gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^rho_s.\n\nThe exact mass formula in Section2 gives M_m/lambda^m->exp(1), so\n\n    limsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1).\n\nConsequently every sequence in the stated range has\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=14/[27 exp(1)]<1,\n\nwith limit superior zero when N tends to infinity.\n\n## 7. Scope and the surviving gap\n\nThis is the complete nonnegative two-predecessor incidence class:\nevery coordinate can merge arbitrary fractions from its two immediate\npredecessors, every split can depend on the entire word and horizon,\nand every compatible positive energy is allowed. No stationarity, parity\nconstancy, small variation, or independent-child assumption remains.\nThe proof includes every actual phase and full terminal completion.\n\nIt still assumes a positive triangular coordinate transfer with memory\ntwo. It does not cover transfers from three or more preceding phases,\nsigned transfers, or reactivating an exhausted coordinate. Cases N<32\nare outside the stated uniform bound (separate small-window candidates\nexist, but are not invoked here). Most importantly it is NOT an upper\nbound on unrestricted W_n(H_m), and it does not settle the original\nall-horizon/all-schedule exact-profile problem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["bc6d55bfdf97d558", "bc10e343efecf012", "8a4c55a6de4727ae"]}, "glossary": {"theta_j": "Independent fraction of coordinate-j mass transferred by phase j-2 in the displayed real two-predecessor DAG.", "E_i": "Squared coordinate mass R_i^2 at phase i.", "N": "Number 2^(m-r) of physical H_r phases.", "A": "1+q, with q=a c^r/(1+sum H_r).", "O_j": "E_(2j+1), the squared mass of an odd phase.", "B_j": "1+q c^(nu_2(2j+2)) for an odd checkpoint phase, and t for the final odd phase; this is distinct from the exact benchmark B_n.", "u_j": "theta_(2j+2), the even-coordinate split immediately after odd phase j.", "L_j": "E_(2j+2), the squared mass of the low phase immediately after odd phase j.", "D_j": "A^2 O_j/B_j+u_j[u_j-(1-u_j)A/B_j]L_j, the positive boundary energy retaining the possibly negative future-coordinate coefficient; terminal value A^2 O_J/t.", "H": "(1+uQ)(1-u+B/A), the coefficient in the queried high phase's first future-normal comparison.", "K": "1/(2M_m-1), the canonical Huber score of the actual word.", "score_free": "2f(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 for any convex 1-smooth completion retaining every original query and the optimum."}}
{"id": "fd7036b719ef9645", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:11:19.321036+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Mixed-sign fresh Markov interface: complete all-old-query legality is explicit, and EVERY positive adjacent coefficient t_l>0 must pay eta_(l+1)-eta_l >=||z_(l+1)||²/t_l>=0. Starting from the separating eta_u=0 jet, total positive-innovation charge is bounded by affine-intercept decreases at negative transitions. Thus homogeneous intercepts cannot support fresh positive innovations, while Huber t=1,z=0 retains zero charge. An exact actual-ruler negative-then-positive prefix with both innovations nonzero and ALL query/value comparisons satisfied shows the paid mechanism is nonempty. No full-tail payoff or whole mixed-sign W bound is claimed.", "evidence": "# Positive fresh transitions require an actual intercept budget\n\nStatus: candidate, not verifier-accepted. No numerical or fact_submit budget\nis allocated. This is an exact interface and a scoped homogeneous-family\nobstruction, NOT a claimed full mixed-sign payoff bound. The construction\nand inequality retain every actual query and its value. Accepted input:\nthe smooth-convex interpolation and construction in1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. Intrinsic data and a real Gram construction\n\nFix the actual near-critical ruler from TASK, with m>=128,\nc=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, r_l=a c^l, d_l=1+r_l,\nH_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,d_l,H_l), M_l=1+sum H_l.\nSelect consecutive first-occurrence levels u,...,v. The ordinary block\nbefore the first selected checkpoint has capacity C_u=M_u, and\nC_l=M_(l-1) for u<l<=v. Capacity is one plus the actual block sum.\nThe total displacement coefficient in ordinary block l plus its\ncheckpoint is D_l=C_l+r_l.\n\nStart at x_0=e_u with norm1, optimum0, and parallel g_u=e_u/tau_u.\nChoose tau_l>=C_l, and put\n\n    w_l=tau_l-C_l, B_l=tau_l-D_l=w_l-r_l.\n\nFor each nonzero transition choose t_l of the same sign as B_l, and\ndefine theta_l=B_l/tau_(l+1). Require\n\n    t_l(theta_l-t_l)>=0.\n\nLet alpha_u=1/tau_u. With fresh orthonormal directions e_(l+1), set\n\n    g_(l+1)=t_l g_l+z_(l+1),\n    z_(l+1)=alpha_l sqrt(t_l(theta_l-t_l)) e_(l+1),\n    alpha_(l+1)^2=t_l theta_l alpha_l^2.            (1)\n\nEach fresh vector is orthogonal to the entire earlier span. Thus (1)\nis an actual finite-dimensional Euclidean construction, not a formal\ncovariance prescription. Positive t_l are additionally restricted to\nt_l<=1; every legal instance must obey this by the argument below.\nNegative transitions automatically have |t_l|<=c-2<1: because\nB_l<0, |theta_l|=(r_l-w_l)/tau_(l+1)<=r_l/M_l<=c-2.\nThe last inequality follows from\nM_l=[a/(c-2)]c^l+[1-a/(c-2)]2^l and0<a<c-2.\n\nWrite X_l for the actual block start, X_u=e_u, and define\n\n    Y_l=X_l-tau_l g_l,\n    X_(l+1)=Y_l+B_l g_l,\n    Y_(l+1)=Y_l+B_l g_l-tau_(l+1)g_(l+1).          (2)\n\nThese are exactly GD displacements because tau_l-B_l=D_l.\nChoose an affine intercept eta_l in\n\n    -w_l alpha_l^2<=eta_l<=0, eta_u=0.             (3)\n\nAt EVERY actual ordinary-block query, including its checkpoint query,\nwith executed ordinary mass s, prescribe\n\n    x=Y_l+(tau_l-s)g_l,\n    gradient=g_l,\n    f(x)=eta_l+(tau_l-s-1/2)alpha_l^2,\n    0<=s<=C_l-1.                                  (4)\n\nThis determines all actual values, not merely endpoint heights. The\nproximal point is y=x-g_l=Y_l+d g_l, with d=tau_l-s-1 in\n[w_l,tau_l-1]; the proximal value is delta=f(x)-alpha_l^2/2\n=eta_l+d alpha_l^2. Include the optimum sample at0 with value0.\n\nConversely, ANY legal first-order fresh Markov relay with a radial first\ngradient and the stated separating first jet eta_u=0 has this intrinsic\nrepresentation: set\ntau_l=<g_l,X_l>/||g_l||^2. The two optimum interpolation inequalities\ngive tau_l>=C_l and exactly the interval(3). Actual constant gradients\nforce the affine values(4). Since a fresh z is orthogonal to X_(l+1),\nthe identity\n\n    tau_(l+1) alpha_(l+1)^2=t_l B_l alpha_l^2      (5)\n\nforces (1), including its innovation norm. A zero next gradient gives\na minimizer and a stationary remaining trajectory, which may be treated\nseparately. Thus the nonzero formulas lose no norm freedom in this class.\n\n## 2. Complete all-pair legality, including old-query costs\n\nThe interpolation inequality from1252b8823bb5a290 in proximal variables is\n\n    delta_j>=delta_i+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.                (6)\n\nThe increments in (2) are orthogonal to the next gradient by (5), and\ntherefore to every later gradient by freshness and the first-order\nrecurrence. Since Y_u=0, this gives <g_j,Y_i>=0 for j>=i.\nFor i<j put\n\n    T_ij=product_(l=i)^(j-1) t_l,\n    R_ij=alpha_j^2/alpha_i^2,\n    L_ij=sum_(l=i)^(j-1)(B_l-tau_(l+1)t_l)T_il,\n    T_ii=1.\n\nThe exact old inner products are\n\n    <g_i,g_j>=alpha_i^2 T_ij,\n    <g_i,Y_j>=alpha_i^2 L_ij.                     (7)\n\nIn particular |T_ij|<=1. Define\n\n    d_ij=tau_j-1 if T_ij>=R_ij, and d_ij=w_j otherwise.\n\nBesides(3), the necessary and sufficient conditions for ALL ordered\nrelay query pairs are precisely, for every i<j,\n\n    eta_j-eta_i<=w_i alpha_i^2(1-T_ij),\n    eta_j-eta_i>=alpha_i^2[L_ij+d_ij(T_ij-R_ij)].  (8)\n\nTo prove this, the plane eta_j+<g_j,.> minus the current plane at\nan old proximal query Y_i+d g_i equals\neta_j-eta_i+d alpha_i^2(T_ij-1). It is largest at d=w_i.\nThe old plane minus the current plane at a query Y_j+d g_j equals\neta_i-eta_j+alpha_i^2[L_ij+d(T_ij-R_ij)]. Its maximum is at the\nendpoint d_ij as defined. Thus (8) is equivalent to every cross-block\nordered inequality, including both endpoints and all interior queries.\nWithin a block the inequalities are equalities. The optimum comparisons\nare exactly (3). The accepted interpolation construction therefore\nsupplies ONE convex differentiable1-smooth function for precisely these\nlegal data. This does not drop later terminal-cell anchors when one\nextends the instance: they must be added to the same interpolation test.\n\nThe first eta_u=0 jet also certifies true distance1 to the minimizer set.\nFor any minimizer z, its interpolation inequality with that jet gives\n0>=eta_u+<g_u,z>, so <e_u,z><=0. Hence ||e_u-z||>=1, and the\nincluded minimizer0 attains1. No alternative closer minimizer changes\nthe normalization.\n\n## 3. Positive transition cost: an exact innovation budget\n\nConsider a positive adjacent coefficient t=t_l>0, abbreviating\ntau'=tau_(l+1), C'=C_(l+1), B=B_l, theta=B/tau'. Legality and(5)\nforce B>0, so w_l>r_l. Adjacent actual queries lie a distance\nd_l g_l apart. Adding their two ordered smooth interpolation inequalities\ngives cocoercivity\n\n    d_l(1-t)alpha_l^2>=||g_l-g_(l+1)||^2.\n\nThus0<t<=1. The PSD construction also gives t<=theta. From (8),\nthe EXACT lower bound on the intercept increase across this pair is\n\n eta_(l+1)-eta_l >=alpha_l^2 E_l,\n\n E_l=B(1-t)+t(theta-1),                 if theta<=1,\n E_l=B(1-t)+C't(theta-1),               if theta>=1.       (9)\n\nThe first case uses the next block's FIRST query; the second case uses\nits actual endpoint. Both are old-query costs, not free value choices.\n\nIn either case E_l>=theta-t. For theta<=1,\n\n    E_l=theta(tau'-1)(1-t)+(theta-t)>=theta-t.\n\nFor theta>=1, B=theta tau'>=theta C', and1-t>=0, so\n\n    E_l>=C'[theta(1-t)+t(theta-1)]=C'(theta-t)>=theta-t.\n\nThe actual innovation norm from (1) is\n||z_(l+1)||^2=t(theta-t)alpha_l^2. Consequently EVERY positive\ntransition satisfies\n\n    eta_(l+1)-eta_l >= ||z_(l+1)||^2/t_l >=0.      (10)\n\nThis retains independent innovation norms; it is not an assumption\nthat all gradients have the same size. In particular, if all affine\nintercepts are zero, every positive transition has zero fresh innovation.\nMore generally, with eta_u=0 and all eta_l<=0, summing(10) and using\nthe total positive and negative variations of the intercept sequence gives\n\n    sum_(l:t_l>0) ||z_(l+1)||^2/t_l\n       <=sum_(l:t_l<0) (eta_l-eta_(l+1))_+.       (11)\n\nIndeed no positive transition decreases eta, the total increase over\nall transitions is no more than the total decrease because eta_v<=eta_u,\nand every decrease therefore occurs at a negative transition.\nThus a positive fresh direction must spend an affine-intercept deficit\ncreated earlier by negative transitions. It cannot start from the initial\nzero-intercept state for free.\n\nThe calibration t_l=1,z_(l+1)=0,theta_l=1 has zero charge. It is the\nconstant-gradient Huber boundary: tau_(l+1)=tau_l-D_l and eta_l=0.\nThus (10)-(11) preserve the known Huber witness, and do NOT imply the\nfalse strictly-below-K bound for all positive adjacent coefficients.\n\n## 4. An explicit genuine mixed-sign prefix, with all pairs checked\n\nThe budget does NOT prohibit positive fresh innovations after a negative\nintercept has been created. Here is an exact actual-ruler calibration with\nu=0,v=2; it uses the four actual queried points before checkpoint d_2.\nLet\n\n    D=1+ac=d_1, C=1+a=d_0,\n    tau_0=1, tau_1=2D, tau_2=C,\n    beta=a/(8tau_1), t_0=-beta, t_1=1/2,\n    E=D-C/2,\n    eta_0=eta_2=0, eta_1=-E alpha_1^2.            (12)\n\nThe capacities are C_0=C_1=1,C_2=C. Thus w_0=w_2=0 and\nw_1=2D-1. The intrinsic signed budgets are B_0=-a and B_1=D.\nUse the actual Euclidean vectors in (1), namely\n\n    g_0=e_0,\n    g_1=-beta e_0+sqrt(beta(a/tau_1-beta)) e_1,\n    alpha_1^2=beta a/tau_1,\n    g_2=(1/2)g_1+alpha_1 sqrt((1/2)(D/C-1/2)) e_2.\n\nBoth innovations are strictly nonzero, since beta<a/tau_1 and D>C>1.\nIn particular this is a genuine negative-then-positive fresh transition.\nAll four actual queries and values are given by (4); the second g_2\nquery follows the actual ordinary step d_0, not an invented checkpoint.\n\nEvery optimum constraint holds because0<E<w_1. To check ALL other\npairs it suffices to check the three block pairs in (8):\n\n* Pair0,1: the upper constraint is eta_1<=0. Its lower right side is\n  -a+beta-w_1 alpha_1^2, which is below-E alpha_1^2 because\n  a>beta and w_1>E.\n* Pair1,2: its lower constraint is exactly\n  eta_2-eta_1>=E alpha_1^2, with equality. Its upper constraint is\n  E<=w_1/2, equivalent to C>=1.\n* Pair0,2: its upper constraint is eta_2<=0, with equality. Its\n  lower right side is L_02=-a+beta(D+C/2)<0, since C<D and\n  beta=a/(16D).\n\nAll internal-block inequalities are equalities, as already proved in\nSection2. Therefore the accepted full interpolation construction gives\none fixed everywhere-defined convex1-smooth function realizing the\nentire stated prefix, with true closest initial distance1. The positive\ninnovation is financed by the actual negative eta_1; all older queries\nremain present. This is a prefix calibration, not an asserted objective\npayoff for the full remaining H_m tail. The tail must still be run on\nthat same fixed function or included in a new full interpolation proof.\n\n## 5. Scope and what is still needed\n\nThis is a complete finite signed model and exact legality test, together\nwith a whole-domain necessary budget for its positive innovations. It is\nnot an attained benchmark-scale objective construction, and not an upper\nbound for the full mixed-sign class. The homogeneous version cannot obtain\nfresh positive innovations. With negative intercepts, one must jointly\nrealize(8), pay(11), and evaluate the actual final objective including the\nterminal displacement and closest minimizer. Merely naming a positive\nterminal correlation or picking a real Gram matrix does not accomplish it.\n\nNo claim is made that positive collinear stages may simply be deleted:\ntheir actual steps and queries still change the waiting masses. Nor is\nthere a claim that (11) alone bounds the final objective. This is the\ninterface to carry into the final allotted round if mixed-sign history\nis selected; otherwise a two-lag recurrence must be specified with genuine\nold-direction reuse and every old query kept. All statements remain\ncandidates pending the verifier gate, and the original target is open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["319c5740dc6abb8e"], "artifact": "research/max_mixed_sign_intercept_budget.md", "status": "unverified; no objective-scale claim"}, "glossary": {"t_l": "Signed coefficient of g_l in the next fresh first-order gradient.", "z_(l+1)": "Actual Euclidean innovation orthogonal to the entire earlier span.", "tau_l": "Intrinsic ratio <g_l,X_l>/||g_l||² at the actual ordinary-block start.", "C_l": "One plus the actual ordinary-block mass preceding selected checkpoint l.", "D_l": "Actual ordinary-block-plus-checkpoint mass C_l+r_l.", "B_l": "Signed available radial coefficient tau_l-D_l=w_l-r_l.", "w_l": "Intrinsic endpoint margin tau_l-C_l.", "theta_l": "Signed ratio B_l/tau_(l+1).", "eta_l": "Actual affine proximal intercept f(x)-<g_l,x>+||g_l||²/2, constant over the queried block.", "alpha_l": "Actual gradient norm ||g_l||.", "T_ij": "Product of the signed first-order coefficients from i to j-1.", "R_ij": "Squared-norm ratio alpha_j²/alpha_i².", "L_ij": "Exact old-direction component <g_i,Y_j>/alpha_i², given by the signed sum in the proof.", "d_ij": "Actual endpoint coordinate maximizing the old-plane comparison in queried block j."}}

The explicit prefix still cannot win with a constant queried tail\n\nFor this SAME concrete prefix, let X denote the actual point just after\ncheckpoint d_2, and let T be the total remaining step mass. Direct norm\naccounting keeps every original displacement. After d_0, X_1=-a e_0.\nAt block1, tau_1=2D and alpha_1^2=a^2/(32D^2), so after d_1,\n\n    ||X_2||^2=a^2-3D^2 alpha_1^2=29a^2/32.\n\nAt block2, tau_2=C and alpha_2^2=a^2/(64CD), while its total\nordinary-plus-checkpoint mass is C+r_2. Consequently\n\n    ||X||^2=a^2[29/32+(r_2^2-C^2)/(64CD)]<a^2.   (13)\n\nIndeed C,D>1, r_2=(ac)c<c<49/20, and r_2^2-C^2<c^2-1<6;\nthe positive part of the added term is therefore less than3/32.\n\nAllow ANY legal completion of the same prefix with a common arbitrary\ngradient q at every remaining QUERIED tail point, all values/intercepts\nfree, and the terminal gradient free. Let b=d_0 be the actual last step.\nThe last queried point is X-(T-b)q. Its optimum interpolation inequality\nand smoothness on the actual last step give\n\n    2f(x_n)<=2<q,X>-(2T+1-b^2)||q||^2\n             <=||X||^2/(2T+1-b^2).                (14)\n\nThis necessary bound applies to the full accepted maximal interpolant\nwith ALL old anchors, not a reduced-anchor replacement. The denominator\nis positive. Here T=S_m-(S_2+d_2), and S_2+d_2<9 uniformly. Also\nb<3/2 and S_m>=2^m-1, so\n\n    (2S_m+1)/(2T+1-b^2)<2 for m>=128.\n\nThus the ENTIRE completed constant-queried-tail class of this explicit\npaid mixed-sign prefix obeys\n\n    2f(x_n)<2a^2 K< (25/72)K,\n    limsup_(m->infinity) 2f(x_n)/K<=1/(1+sqrt2)^2. (15)\n\nFor the last limit, the mass ratio in the preceding display tends to1\nand a tends to1/(1+sqrt2). This is a scoped comparison to the actual\nHuber baseline K, not an upper bound on all mixed-sign recurrences.\nThe paid prefix is therefore a real calibration but does not supply\nthe desired new payoff through a constant final queried cell.\n\n## 6. Scope and what is still needed\n\nThis is a complete finite signed model and exact legality test, together\nwith a whole-domain necessary budget for its positive innovations. It is\nnot an attained benchmark-scale objective construction, and not an upper\nbound for the full mixed-sign class. The homogeneous version cannot obtain\nfresh positive innovations. With negative intercepts, one must jointly\nrealize(8), pay(11), and evaluate the actual final objective including the\nterminal displacement and closest minimizer. Merely naming a positive\nterminal correlation or picking a real Gram matrix does not accomplish it.\n\nNo claim is made that positive collinear stages may simply be deleted:\ntheir actual steps and queries still change the waiting masses. Nor is\nthere a claim that (11) alone bounds the final objective. This is the\ninterface to carry into the final allotted round if mixed-sign history\nis selected; otherwise a two-lag recurrence must be specified with genuine\nold-direction reuse and every old query kept. All statements remain\ncandidates pending the verifier gate, and the original target is open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["fd7036b719ef9645"], "artifact": "research/max_mixed_sign_intercept_budget.md", "status": "unverified whole scoped package"}, "glossary": {"K": "The actual global Huber baseline1/(2S_m+1) on the supplied near-critical ruler.", "S_m": "Total step mass of the actual m-level ruler.", "X": "Actual point after checkpoint level2 for the explicit paid mixed-sign prefix.", "T": "Actual remaining tail step mass S_m-(S_2+d_2).", "q": "Common gradient at every queried tail point; the endpoint gradient remains free.", "b": "The actual final ruler step d_0=1+a_m."}}
{"id": "19f9dda7a15723b3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:13:29.831325+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C44 excludes the complete positive separable flow family a_ij=u_i beta_j with UNBOUNDED forward memory and arbitrary nonstationary source weights. All old/future normals imply D_i>0 and <p_i,p_j>=-u_iD_j/(T_iT_j)<=0. Retaining the squared future tail gives an exact boundary-energy transfer bounded by the waiting/child factors. On the actual near-critical word, m>=512,N>=16, every legal member and full terminal completion satisfies score_free/K<7(3/4)^(N/2)<7/9, hence limsup B_n score_free<=14/(9e)<1; growing N gives zero. Candidate only, not a W upper bound.", "evidence": "# C44. Unbounded-memory merging with separable incoming weights\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier, numerical solver or sweep.\nEvery necessary argument is reproduced below. No earlier candidate is used\nas a correctness premise.\n\nThis family lets each coordinate receive mass from EVERY earlier phase,\nwith arbitrary nonnegative source weights, but requires the incoming\nprofiles to be proportional: a_ij=u_i beta_j for i<j. On the actual\nnear-critical word, m>=512 and N>=16 phases imply, for every legal member\nand every full terminal completion,\n\n    score_free/K <7(3/4)^(N/2)<7/9.\n\nThus limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=14/(9e)<1, and the limit superior\nis zero when N tends to infinity. This concerns the entire stated\nseparable family, not arbitrary positive flows or unrestricted W.\n\n## 1. Fixed real flow with arbitrarily long memory\n\nSet lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Choose 0<=r<=m-4 and write\nk=m-r, N=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M, A=1+q. Define\n\n q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n tau_(N-1)=t>=1, T_(N-1)=Mt.\n\nHere nu_2 is binary valuation. The N phases are exactly the physical\ncopies of H_r, each of ordinary mass M-1 and, except the last, followed\nby the original checkpoint 1+Mq_i.\n\nChoose u_0>0, u_i>=0 for 1<=i<N-1, and put u_(N-1)=0. Choose\nbeta_j>0 for 1<=j<N, and a separate R_0>0. For i>=1 put\n\n U_i=sum_(l<i)u_l>0, R_i=U_i beta_i, E_i=R_i^2,\n W_i=sum_(j>i)beta_j^2,\n D_i=U_i beta_i^2-u_i W_i.\n\nSet E_0=R_0^2 and W_0=sum_(j>=1)beta_j^2. In R^N define\n\n p_0=(R_0e_0-u_0 sum_(j>=1)beta_j e_j)/(MA),\n p_i=(U_i beta_i e_i-u_i sum_(j>i)beta_j e_j)/T_i, i>=1,\n C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),\n F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nThe e_i are the standard orthonormal basis. Every coordinate j>=1\nreceives total mass U_j beta_j=R_j from its earlier phases, with transfer\na_ij=u_i beta_j. These are actual Euclidean vectors with a positive\ntriangular diagonal. Euclidean projection onto C maximizes the displayed\nformula; the projection variational inequality proves it is nonexpansive.\nMaximizing-difference inequalities then give grad F_C=P_C, so F_C is\nconvex and1-smooth.\n\nStarting at R_0e_0, the exact state before phase i>=1 is\n\n    X_i=U_i sum_(j>=i)beta_j e_j.                         (1)\n\nSubtracting T_i p_i produces X_(i+1); every physical step inside the\nphase subtracts its own actual length times p_i. No step is reordered.\n\nFor complete legality, let z=(ordinary partial mass+1)/M in[1/M,1].\nMultiplying normal values by M, the current normal in phase i>=1 is\n\n [ (tau_i-z)U_i D_i-z u_i(U_i+u_i)W_i ]/tau_i^2,          (2)\n\nand EVERY future normal j>i is\n\n    (U_i+z u_i/tau_i)D_j/tau_j.                          (3)\n\nEvery past normal is nonpositive: its positive coordinate is exhausted\nand all remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative. Compare(2)\nwith zero and with ALL(3), at BOTH z=1/M and z=1. This is necessary\nand sufficient for every actual query because all differences are affine.\nAt the root the corresponding current value is\n\n    [(A-z)E_0-z u_0^2 W_0]/A^2,\n\nand its future-j value is (z u_0/A)D_j/tau_j. Compare these at both\nendpoints against every future vertex and zero. The final phase has\nu_(N-1)=0 and t>=1, so it passes its own/zero and past-normal tests.\n\nFor every fixed finite source-weight sequence the class is nonempty.\nChoose beta_(N-1)>0, then each beta_i successively backward sufficiently\nlarge to satisfy its finite tests; its own beta_i^2 coefficient is positive.\nFinally choose R_0. Earlier tests have not yet been fixed and are handled\nin their own backward turn. The bound below holds for ALL legal choices.\n\nAt any nonterminal phase i>=1, the endpoint zero comparison in(2) is\n\n q_i U_i D_i>=u_i(U_i+u_i)W_i>=0.                        (4)\n\nIf u_i>0 then W_i>0 and(4) gives D_i>0; if u_i=0 this follows from\nits definition. The final D_(N-1)=U_(N-1)beta_(N-1)^2 is positive.\nIn particular every inter-gradient correlation is\n\n    <p_i,p_j>=-u_i D_j/(T_i T_j)<=0, i<j,                (5)\n\nwhere T_0=MA. Thus separable positive sharing, despite its long memory,\ncannot produce positive pairwise gradient correlations.\n\nThe polar inequalities <p_i,z><=0 force every coordinate of z\nnonpositive by backward induction. Since0 is a minimizer, it is the\nactual closest minimizer to R_0e_0, with squared distance E_0.\nEvery queried value is <x,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. If htot=sum H_m, the\noriginal normalized objective and primitive defect are exactly\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0),\n V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.            (6)\n\nFor the defect, sum the exact identity\n2h_iF_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2\nand subtract the defining terminal term.\n\n## 2. A transfer bound retaining the entire squared tail\n\nDefine the positive boundary energy\n\n    Z_i=U_i D_i/tau_i, i>=1.                             (7)\n\nAt the terminal phase Z_(N-1)=E_(N-1)/t.\nThe endpoint comparison of phase i with phase i+1 gives, from(2)-(3),\n\n q_i U_i D_i>=u_i(U_i+u_i)W_i\n                   +tau_i(tau_i U_i+u_i)D_(i+1)/tau_(i+1).\n\nSince W_i>=beta_(i+1)^2>=D_(i+1)/U_(i+1) and U_(i+1)=U_i+u_i,\nthe squared-tail term must pay at least u_i D_(i+1). Therefore\n\n Z_(i+1)/Z_i\n <=q_i tau_i(U_i+u_i)/\n           [tau_i^2 U_i+u_i(tau_i+tau_(i+1))]            (8)\n <=max(q_i/tau_i, q_i tau_i/(tau_i+tau_(i+1))).\n\nThe last inequality follows by viewing the ratio as a positive\ndenominator-weighted average of its values at u_i=0 and U_i=0;\nU_i is actually positive, so no endpoint singularity is used.\n\nAt the root, its endpoint comparison with phase1 gives\n\n q E_0>=u_0^2W_0+A u_0 D_1/tau_1\n       >=u_0 D_1+A u_0 D_1/tau_1.\n\nHere D_1<=u_0 beta_1^2<=u_0 W_0. Since U_1=u_0, this becomes\n\n    Z_1/E_0<=q/(A+tau_1).                                (9)\n\nThese inequalities explicitly retain the growing source sum U_i and\nthe older-coordinate residual W_i. Dropping either would give a false\napparent gain from accumulating many branches.\n\n## 3. Multiply the exact ruler factors\n\nThe mass recursion gives, with b=a/(c-2) in(0,1),\n\n    M_j=1+sum H_j=b c^j+(1-b)2^j.\n\nConsequently M_m<=Mc^k and a<=q<c-2. For m>=512 use\n\n 2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16,\n cq>=1-1/m>=99/100,\n qc<=c(c-2)<1+8/m<=65/64.                                (10)\n\nFor example lambda<169/70 and a>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5\ngive the first lower bound. The expansion\nc(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2 gives the last one.\n\nAt a LOW phase tau_i=A and tau_(i+1)>=1. Since A q<1,\n\n    A^2<A+tau_(i+1),\n\nso the first alternative in(8) is larger and the factor is at most q/A.\n\nAt a HIGH phase tau_i=B=1+Q with Q>=99/100, its next phase is low,\ntau_(i+1)=A. The bounds imply BQ>A, so B^2>B+A and the second\nalternative in(8) is larger. The factor is at most QB/(B+A).\n\nWrite B_1=1+cq=tau_1 (not the benchmark with the same letter).\nThere are N/2-1 high phases, with levels summing to N-k-1, and the\nsame number of low phases after the root. Thus (8)-(9) give\n\n Z_(N-1)/E_0\n <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-1)]\n               *product_(high i) tau_i/(tau_i+A)\n <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-1)].                  (11)\n\nThe count follows directly from 2^(k-ell-1) high checkpoints at level\nell=1,...,k-1. No asymptotic replacement of the physical word is made.\n\n## 4. Full terminal completion and the actual distance\n\nLet f be ANY convex1-smooth completion retaining all queried jets and\nthe optimum(0,0,0), including the maximal completion in accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nIf r>=1, every p_i is actually queried. Interpolation to any new\nminimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0, since the retained value is\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The triangular inequalities force its coordinates\nnonpositive, so0 remains the true closest minimizer at distance R_0.\nAdditional orthogonal dimensions cannot lower the distance.\n\nThe last physical step is h_last=1+a. Its actual retained query gives\n\n 2f(x_n)/E_0\n <=[2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0)\n <2E_(N-1)/(MtE_0),\n\nbecause M>=2+a yields 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nPut K=1/(2M_m-1). Use M_m<=Mc^k and(11):\n\n score_free/K\n <[4A/(A+B_1)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2)\n <2(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2).                                 (12)\n\nThe last inequality uses B_1>A.\n\nIf r=0, the old terminal gradient is UNQUERIED. Use instead the\nLipschitz-gradient lower bound\n\n    dist(x_0,argmin f)^2>=||p_0||^2>=E_0/A^2.             (13)\n\nAt the final query i=N-2, put v=U_i/U_(i+1) in(0,1]. Its position is\nR_i e_i+v R_(i+1)e_(i+1), and its gradient is\n[R_i e_i-(1-v)R_(i+1)e_(i+1)]/A. One smoothness step of length A gives\n\n 2f(x_n)<=(1-A^(-2))E_i\n +[-2v(1-v)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-v)^2]E_(i+1)\n <=(1-A^(-2))E_i.                                       (14)\n\nThe bracket is nonpositive for A<23/16.\n\nThe final low phase's own zero comparison says\n\n q E_i>=u_i(AU_i+u_i)beta_(i+1)^2.\n\nSince Z_i=[E_i-u_i U_i beta_(i+1)^2]/A, it follows that\n\n    Z_i>=E_i/A^2.                                       (15)\n\nTo check this explicitly, the subtracted numerator is at most\nq U_i E_i/(AU_i+u_i)<=q E_i/A; A-q=1 then proves(15).\n\nRepeat the product(8)-(9) but omit the final low factor q/A. It yields\n\n Z_(N-2)/E_0\n <=q^(N-2)c^(N-k-1)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-2)].\n\nCombine(13)-(15), multiply by K^(-1)<2c^k, and use A^2-1=q(2+q):\n\n score_free/K\n <[2(2+q)A^4/(A+B_1)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2)\n <7(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2).                                 (16)\n\nFor the numerical rational constant, A+B_1>3 and\n2(39/16)(23/16)^4/3=21827598/3145728<7.\n\nNo unqueried normal has been used to constrain the new minimizer.\nThe terminal bound follows only from retained queries and their actual\nphysical last displacement.\n\n## 5. Uniform payoff and the original exact benchmark\n\nSet z0=65/64>1. Then (qc)^(N-1)<=z0^N even when qc<1.\nAlso\n\n    z0^2/A<(65/64)^2/(7/5)=21125/28672<3/4.\n\nEquations(12),(16) prove\n\n    score_free/K<7(3/4)^(N/2).\n\nFor N>=16, (3/4)^4=81/256<1/3 gives score_free/K<7/9.\nIf N grows, the ratio tends to zero uniformly in all source weights,\ntarget weights, energies, r, terminal margin and full completion.\n\nFor the exact benchmark use accepted b98bb807d514e80d and write\nrho_s=log_2(lambda). Put F(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1. The second derivative of\nlog F(exp w) equals\n\n rho_s*z*(1+z)^(rho_s-2)*\n [(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s*z]/F(z)^2>0.\n\nThus F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2 by midpoint convexity in w. Comparing the\npositive root of t^2-(X+Y)t-XY with\n(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s proves\n\n    Omega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\n\nInduction in the accepted primitive recursion gives\n1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s. Since the accepted dominant join\nGamma(X,D)=2X+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8XD)/2 is at most4X+D,\nits recursion then gives B_n<=1+4n^rho_s.\nThe displayed exact mass formula gives M_m/lambda^m->exp(1), hence\n\n    limsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1).\n\nWe conclude\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=14/[9 exp(1)]<1,\n\nwith limit superior zero whenever N tends to infinity.\n\n## 6. Scope\n\nThe source weights may vary arbitrarily, vanish after the root, and\ndepend on the horizon. Every future coordinate has a potentially\nunbounded number of contributing parents. Every old and future normal\nand the true normalization are explicit. This is a complete exclusion\nof separable positive incoming profiles, including free terminal data.\n\nIt does not cover a general a_ij whose column profiles are not\nproportional. In particular two independent temporal source profiles,\nsigned transfer, or reactivation of old coordinates remain outside it.\nIt does not upper-bound unrestricted W and does not prove the original\nexact-profile target.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["624bcb56cdc90ae7", "8e8cc2d8c1739221"]}, "glossary": {"u_i": "Arbitrary nonnegative source weight, u_0>0, in a_ij=u_i beta_j.", "beta_j": "Positive target-coordinate weight in the separable forward transfers.", "U_i": "Sum of source weights u_l over l<i.", "R_i": "Actual coordinate mass U_i beta_i for i>=1; R_0 is the independent initial mass.", "W_i": "Squared future target-weight tail sum_(j>i) beta_j^2, not the unrestricted objective W_n.", "D_i": "U_i beta_i^2-u_i W_i, forced positive by the own/zero projection comparison.", "Z_i": "Positive boundary energy U_i D_i/tau_i.", "N": "Number 2^(m-r) of physical H_r phases.", "K": "Canonical Huber score 1/(2M_m-1), with M_m=1+sum H_m.", "score_free": "Actual normalized objective 2f(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 for any convex1-smooth terminal completion retaining all queried jets and the optimum."}}
